| 311 | Audit `DepthStencilState` API and static presets against FNA   | ✅      | **Opens Phase 37.** Audited CNA's `DepthStencilState` against FNA's `Graphics/States/DepthStencilState.cs` line-by-line: the full 16-property surface, all 3 static presets (`Default`/`DepthRead`/`None`), and the default-constructor values (`DepthBufferEnable=DepthBufferWriteEnable=true`, `DepthBufferFunction=LessEqual`, `StencilEnable=false`, `StencilFunction=CounterClockwiseStencilFunction=Always`, all 4 stencil-op fields `=Keep`, `TwoSidedStencilMode=false`, `StencilMask=StencilWriteMask=Int32.MaxValue`, `ReferenceStencil=0`) already matched FNA exactly — no bug in the core state values (now covered by the existing `DepthStencilStateTests.cpp`). **Real, expected finding** (already tracked as Task 866 from Task 291's audit): FNA's private preset constructor sets `Name` (e.g. `"DepthStencilState.Default"`) on each preset; CNA's didn't. Fixed by threading a `name` parameter through the private preset constructor, mirroring Task 291 (`SamplerState`)/Task 301 (`BlendState`) exactly. Added 5 new tests (`Name` per preset ×3, `ToString()` returns preset name, default-constructed `Name` is empty) to `DepthStencilStateTests.cpp`. This closes `DepthStencilState`'s portion of Task 866 — `RasterizerState` remains open (its own later audit task in this phase list). **Found and flagged (not fixed, per Task 301→302 precedent) a Task-302-shaped bug for Task 312 to fix**: `GraphicsDevice`'s constructor never initializes `depthStencilState_`/`rasterizerState_` from `DepthStencilState::Default`/`RasterizerState::CullCounterClockwise` (confirmed via `GraphicsDevice.cpp`'s constructor member-init list — only `blendState_` is initialized there, from Task 302's fix; `depthStencilState_`/`rasterizerState_` are absent, so they use their own plain default constructors). Since `DepthStencilState::Default`'s values (`true,true`) coincide with `DepthStencilState()`'s own default values, this was invisible until this task's `Name` fix — exactly Task 302's "coincided until Name existed to distinguish them" pattern. Confirmed via FNA's `GraphicsDevice.cs` constructor: `DepthStencilState = DepthStencilState.Default; RasterizerState = RasterizerState.CullCounterClockwise;`. EasyGL ctest: 2069/2071 (2 pre-existing, unrelated failures unchanged). Vulkan ctest: 2002/2009 (5 Task-868 confirmations, `Vulkan_DepthBias`'s usual sub-case, and one recurrence of `Vulkan_RenderTargetUsage`'s pre-existing parallel/order-dependent flakiness — reconfirmed unrelated via 3/3 passes in isolation immediately after). |
| 312 | Verify default depth/stencil state on `GraphicsDevice`         | ✅      | **Found and fixed a real bug, same shape as Task 302's `BlendState` finding, flagged by Task 311.** FNA's `GraphicsDevice` constructor sets `DepthStencilState = DepthStencilState.Default;` and `RasterizerState = RasterizerState.CullCounterClockwise;` (confirmed via `GraphicsDevice.cs`); CNA's `depthStencilState_`/`rasterizerState_` members were plain default-constructed, never copied from the presets. Values coincidentally matched for `depthStencilState_` (`DepthStencilState::Default`'s `{true,true}` equals the plain default constructor's own values) — invisible until Task 311 gave `Default` a `Name`, making the divergence (`""` vs `"DepthStencilState.Default"`) newly detectable, exactly Task 302's pattern. Fixed by initializing both `depthStencilState_(DepthStencilState::Default)` and `rasterizerState_(RasterizerState::CullCounterClockwise)` in the constructor's member-init list, positioned in declaration order (`blendState_`, `depthStencilState_`, `rasterizerState_`, then `blendFactor_`) to avoid `-Wreorder`. 3 new tests in `GraphicsDeviceDefaultStateTests.cpp`: `DepthStencilState`'s `Name` and value match, and `RasterizerState`'s value match (deliberately no `RasterizerState` `Name` test — its preset `Name` gap, Task 866's remaining portion, isn't fixed yet, so testing an empty-vs-empty `Name` match would be meaningless; add that test alongside `RasterizerState`'s own future audit task). EasyGL ctest: 2072/2074 (2 pre-existing failures unchanged). Vulkan ctest: 2006/2012 (only pre-existing/documented failures: `Vulkan_DepthBias`'s usual sub-case, 5 Task-868 confirmations). |
| 313 | Pixel test: depth write enabled vs disabled                    | ✅      | New `examples/easygl_depthstencilstate_write_enable_test.cpp`: three-quad differential test — far quad A (depth 0.8, writes), near quad B (depth 0.2, `DepthBufferWriteEnable` is the variable under test), then quad C at an in-between depth (0.5) with normal writes-on state; C's pass/fail reveals whether the depth buffer still holds A's 0.8 (write correctly skipped → C passes → BLUE) or was overwritten by B's 0.2 (write bug → C rejected → GREEN). A second check (B drawn with writes ON) is a sanity control proving the depth test itself works, not just a broken always-reject/always-pass state. **Confirms `DepthBufferWriteEnable` works correctly on both EasyGL and Vulkan** — both checks pass on both backends. **Found and had to work around two real, separate discoveries while designing this test, both now documented so future tasks don't re-diagnose them:** (1) with an identity World/View/Projection (as used here), this project's Vulkan backend's `colored3d.vert.glsl` passes vertex Z straight through as clip-space Z, and (correctly, matching real XNA/DirectX semantics, confirmed via the shader's own comment) expects it in DirectX's `[0,+w]` range, NOT OpenGL's `[-1,+1]` — a raw negative Z is silently clipped away entirely on Vulkan (but not on EasyGL/OpenGL, which is more permissive), so any future identity-matrix depth pixel test on this project MUST keep Z within `[0,1]` or it will silently lose geometry on Vulkan only, with no error. (2) **A new, severe, MASSIVE bug, tracked as Task 870, NOT fixed here**: `VulkanGraphicsBackend::ApplyDepthStencilState` discards 13 of its 15 parameters — `depthFunc` (so `DepthBufferFunction` is ignored, hardcoded per-pipeline-creation-function as a mix of `VK_COMPARE_OP_LESS`/`VK_COMPARE_OP_LESS_OR_EQUAL` instead of FNA's actual `LessEqual` default) and the *entire* stencil-test parameter set (`stencilEnable` and everything downstream of it) — confirmed via grep that no Vulkan pipeline ever sets `VkPipelineDepthStencilStateCreateInfo::stencilTestEnable`/`front`/`back` at all, so stencil testing is completely non-functional on Vulkan regardless of `DepthStencilState.StencilEnable`. EasyGL and Bgfx are both fully, correctly implemented by contrast (real compare-func mapping plus full front/back stencil op support) — confirmed by direct code reading, the same "one backend hardcodes/ignores, the other two are correct" shape as Task 868's `BlendState` finding. This directly explains why my first test design (C at the SAME depth as A) failed on Vulkan for the WRONG reason (equal-depth comparisons always fail under the hardcoded strict-`LESS` pipeline, regardless of `DepthBufferWriteEnable`) — fixed by moving C to an unambiguous in-between depth instead. **Expect Task 314 (`DepthBufferFunction` pixel test) and Task 315 (stencil enable/disable) to hit Task 870 directly** — do not re-diagnose it as a new bug. EasyGL ctest: 2073/2075 (2 pre-existing, unrelated failures unchanged). Vulkan ctest: 2007/2013 (only pre-existing/documented failures: `Vulkan_DepthBias`'s usual sub-case, 5 Task-868 confirmations; `Vulkan_FillMode_WireFrame` did not recur this run). |
| 314 | Pixel test: depth comparison functions                         | ✅      | New `examples/easygl_depthstencilstate_compare_function_test.cpp`: 5 side-by-side columns, one per `CompareFunction` (`Always`/`Never`/`Less`/`LessEqual`/`Greater`), each drawing a reference quad A (depth 0.5, `DepthStencilState::Default`) then a quad B at a depth chosen to give an unambiguous, function-distinguishing expected result (`Always`@0.9→PASS, `Never`@0.1→FAIL, `Less`@0.3→PASS, `LessEqual`@0.5 equal→PASS, `Greater`@0.7→PASS — `Less`/`Greater` use opposite depth directions so a direction swap would be caught, and `LessEqual`'s equal-depth case distinguishes it from strict `Less`). **EasyGL: all 5 checks pass exactly as predicted.** **Vulkan: 4 of 5 checks fail — a full, direct reconfirmation of Task 870 (found by Task 313), not a new bug.** The failure pattern is fully consistent with the exercised pipeline hardcoding `VK_COMPARE_OP_LESS` regardless of what's requested: only `Less` (which happens to coincide with the hardcoded behavior) passes; `Always`/`Never`/`LessEqual`/`Greater` all fail in exactly the way predicted by "every comparison actually evaluates as strict-Less no matter what `DepthBufferFunction` is set to." Kept `Vulkan_DepthStencilState_CompareFunction` registered as a documented known failure (same pattern as Task 868's `Vulkan_BlendState_*` tests). EasyGL ctest: 2074/2076 (2 pre-existing, unrelated failures unchanged). Vulkan ctest: 2006/2014 (5 Task-868 confirmations, this task's Task-870 confirmation, `Vulkan_FillMode_WireFrame`'s pre-existing order-dependent flake, and `Vulkan_DepthBias`'s usual sub-case — all pre-existing/documented). |
| 315 | Verify stencil enable/disable behavior                         | ✅      | New `examples/easygl_depthstencilstate_stencil_enable_test.cpp`: two-column differential test — a "stamp" pass writes stencil=1 on each column's left half (0 elsewhere via an explicit full-screen zero-stamp first, since `GraphicsDevice::Clear` ignores `ClearOptions::Stencil` entirely, tracked as new Task 871), then a GREEN quad drawn over the whole column with `StencilFunction=Equal, ReferenceStencil=1` and `StencilEnable` as the variable under test (`true` for Check A, `false` for Check B). **Found and fixed a real, severe, previously-undiscovered bug while running this on EasyGL first**: the test initially failed identically to Vulkan (both stencil halves showed GREEN regardless of `StencilEnable`) — traced to `EasyGLGraphicsBackend`'s SDL/GL context creation never requesting `SDL_GL_STENCIL_SIZE`, so **no EasyGL window in this project has ever had a real stencil buffer**, making the stencil test trivially always-pass at the GL level (per spec, when there's no stencil plane) regardless of how correctly `ApplyDepthStencilState`'s `glStencilFunc`/`glStencilOp`/`glEnable(GL_STENCIL_TEST)` calls were already wired (confirmed correct by code reading in Task 313/314's write-ups — the bug was the underlying GPU resource never existing, not the state-application code). Fixed with a one-line `SDL_GL_SetAttribute(SDL_GL_STENCIL_SIZE, 8)` addition; full ctest reconfirmed zero regressions. **After the fix, both checks pass correctly on EasyGL** — `StencilEnable` genuinely gates the stencil test. **Vulkan: Check A fails outright (both halves GREEN, no gating at all) — a full, direct reconfirmation of Task 870** (stencil testing is completely non-functional there); Check B passes coincidentally (same pattern as the `DepthBufferFunction` test). **Also found an additional, compounding root cause for Vulkan specifically while investigating**: `VulkanGraphicsBackend::FindDepthFormat()` checks `VK_FORMAT_D32_SFLOAT` (a stencil-LESS format) *before* the two stencil-capable formats (`VK_FORMAT_D32_SFLOAT_S8_UINT`/`VK_FORMAT_D24_UNORM_S8_UINT`) — since `D32_SFLOAT` is a mandatory-support Vulkan format on essentially all hardware, Vulkan's depth attachment almost never has a stencil aspect at all, so even a hypothetical fix to `ApplyDepthStencilState` (Task 870) would still need this format-preference order fixed too. Noted as an addendum to Task 870, not a separate task number (same root fix effort). **Flagged, not investigated**: whether `RenderTarget2D`/`RenderTargetCube`'s own separate depth/stencil renderbuffer allocation on EasyGL requests stencil bits — the fix here only covers the default backbuffer/window; render-target stencil support is an open question for whichever future task first needs it. EasyGL ctest: 2075/2077 (2 pre-existing, unrelated failures unchanged). Vulkan ctest: 2006/2015 (5 Task-868 confirmations, `Vulkan_DepthStencilState_CompareFunction`/`StencilEnable` — both Task-870 confirmations, `Vulkan_FillMode_WireFrame`'s pre-existing order-dependent flake, `Vulkan_DepthBias`'s usual sub-case — all pre-existing/documented). |
| 316 | Verify stencil read/write masks                                | ✅      | New `examples/easygl_depthstencilstate_stencil_mask_test.cpp`: 4 columns, 2 differential pairs. `StencilMask` (read mask) pair: stamp stencil=0x05, then compare `ReferenceStencil=0x01` with a narrow `StencilMask=0x01` (expect PASS: `0x01&0x01==0x05&0x01`) vs a full `StencilMask=0xFF` (expect FAIL: `0x01!=0x05` unmasked) — the opposite expected outcomes prove the mask is genuinely applied, not coincidental. `StencilWriteMask` pair: stamp stencil=0xFF, then `Replace` with `ReferenceStencil=0x00` under a narrow `StencilWriteMask=0x0F` (only lower 4 bits writable, expect final buffer `0xF0`, read back and confirmed PASS) vs a full `StencilWriteMask=0xFF` (expect final buffer `0x00`, same read-back check correctly FAILs). **EasyGL: all 4 checks pass exactly as predicted.** **Vulkan: 2 of 4 fail — a further, clean reconfirmation of Task 870** (`stencilMask`/`stencilWriteMask` are both discarded, unused parameters in `ApplyDepthStencilState`, and stencil testing never gates at all since `stencilTestEnable` is never set) — the 2 checks that *expect* PASS pass purely by coincidence (nothing ever gates, so every column renders green regardless of mask), while the 2 contrast checks that expect FAIL correctly fail, revealing the bug — the same asymmetric-pass pattern already seen for `BlendState` (Task 305/308) and `DepthStencilState` (Task 315). Kept `Vulkan_DepthStencilState_StencilMask` registered as a documented known failure. EasyGL ctest: 2076/2078 (2 pre-existing, unrelated failures unchanged). Vulkan ctest: 2007/2016 (5 Task-868 confirmations, 3 Task-870 confirmations — `CompareFunction`/`StencilEnable`/`StencilMask` — and `Vulkan_DepthBias`'s usual sub-case; `Vulkan_FillMode_WireFrame` did not recur this run). |
| 317 | Verify front-face stencil operations                           | ✅      | New `examples/easygl_depthstencilstate_stencil_ops_test.cpp`: 4 columns — 3 isolate one `StencilOperation` slot each (`StencilFail`/`StencilDepthBufferFail`/`StencilPass`, each set to `Increment` while the other two slots are `Decrement` traps), verified via a stamp (`Replace`, buffer=0x05) → an operation draw whose stencil/depth compare outcome routes to exactly one slot → a read-back compare (`Equal`, expects 0x06). A 4th contrast/control column reuses the `StencilPass` setup but deliberately reads back with the WRONG reference (0x99, expects REJECT/BACKGROUND) — **critical for this test's validity**: without it, all 3 op-slot checks expect the same PASS/GREEN outcome, which a fully-bypassed stencil test (as already found on Vulkan, Task 870) would *also* produce, giving zero power to detect the bug. Discovered this exact gap mid-development: an earlier 3-column version coincidentally passed all checks on Vulkan too, for the wrong reason. **EasyGL: all 4 checks pass exactly as predicted**, including the contrast column correctly rejecting. **Vulkan: 3 of 4 pass purely by coincidence (nothing ever gates); the contrast column correctly fails — a fourth, clean reconfirmation of Task 870.** Kept `Vulkan_DepthStencilState_StencilOps` registered as a documented known failure. EasyGL ctest: 2077/2079 (2 pre-existing, unrelated failures unchanged). Vulkan ctest: 2007/2017 (5 Task-868 confirmations, 4 Task-870 confirmations — `CompareFunction`/`StencilEnable`/`StencilMask`/`StencilOps` — and `Vulkan_DepthBias`'s usual sub-case; `Vulkan_FillMode_WireFrame` did not recur this run). |
| 318 | Verify two-sided stencil operations if API exposes them        | ✅      | Confirmed FNA's API exposes it fully: `DepthStencilState.TwoSidedStencilMode` plus `CounterClockwiseStencilFunction`/`Fail`/`DepthBufferFail`/`Pass` (all already present in CNA, only `ReferenceStencil` is shared between faces — FNA has no per-face reference value). New `examples/easygl_depthstencilstate_stencil_twosided_test.cpp`: a genuinely differential 2-column test from the outset (applying Task 317's lesson directly, not after a failed first attempt) — both columns stamp the same baseline (0x05) then draw the SAME deliberately back-facing triangle (`RasterizerState.CullMode=None` + reversed winding) with identical front-face (`Equal`/`Decrement`, passes) and CCW (`NotEqual`/`Increment` on fail, fails) property values, differing ONLY in `TwoSidedStencilMode`. Column 0 (`TwoSidedStencilMode=true`): CCW settings apply to the back-facing triangle → stencil compare fails → `CounterClockwiseStencilFail`(`Increment`) fires → buffer becomes 0x06 → read-back (`Equal`, ref=0x06) PASSES. Column 1 (`TwoSidedStencilMode=false`, contrast): front-face settings apply to ALL faces including this back-facing one → stencil compare passes → `StencilPass`(`Decrement`) fires → buffer becomes 0x04 → the SAME read-back check correctly REJECTS (0x04≠0x06). **EasyGL: both checks pass exactly as predicted** (also confirms `CullMode::None` + reversed-winding back-face rendering behaves correctly, a prerequisite this test relies on). **Vulkan: column 0 passes by coincidence, column 1 (contrast) fails — a fifth, clean reconfirmation of Task 870** (`twoSidedStencilMode`/CCW parameters are all discarded, stencil testing never gates at all). Kept `Vulkan_DepthStencilState_StencilTwoSided` registered as a documented known failure. EasyGL ctest: 2078/2080 (2 pre-existing, unrelated failures unchanged). Vulkan ctest: 2007/2018 (5 Task-868 confirmations, 5 Task-870 confirmations — `CompareFunction`/`StencilEnable`/`StencilMask`/`StencilOps`/`StencilTwoSided` — and `Vulkan_DepthBias`'s usual sub-case; `Vulkan_FillMode_WireFrame` did not recur this run). |
| 319 | Verify `ReferenceStencil` device state is used by all backends | ✅      | **Closes Phase 37.** Confirmed via FNA source that `GraphicsDevice.ReferenceStencil` is a real, independent device property (`FNA3D_Get/SetReferenceStencil`), analogous to `GraphicsDevice.BlendFactor` (Task 309) — settable WITHOUT reassigning the whole `DepthStencilState`, and expected to immediately affect subsequent stencil compares (FNA's own source even carries a `FIXME: Does this affect the value found in DepthStencilState?` comment, confirming this really is meant to be an independent override, not a proxy). **Found and fixed a small bug of the exact same shape as Task 309/312**: `GraphicsDevice::setDepthStencilStateProperty` never propagated the assigned state's own `ReferenceStencil` into `GraphicsDevice`'s own `referenceStencil_` member, so `getReferenceStencilProperty()` could return stale data after assigning a new `DepthStencilState`. Fixed by calling `setReferenceStencilProperty(value.getReferenceStencilProperty())` from within `setDepthStencilStateProperty`, mirroring Task 309 exactly. New unit test in `GraphicsDeviceDefaultStateTests.cpp` confirms this. **Found a SECOND, much bigger, universal bug while verifying the independent-override behavior with a real pixel test, tracked as new Task 872, NOT fixed**: `GraphicsDevice::setReferenceStencilProperty` is a pure local no-op with **zero backend connection on all 3 backends** — confirmed via grep that `IGraphicsBackend` has no `SetReferenceStencil` method at all, unlike `BlendFactor`'s case (Task 309) where the backend method already existed and just wasn't being called. This is a genuinely universal gap, unlike Task 870 (Vulkan-specific) — confirmed failing on **both** EasyGL and Vulkan via new `examples/easygl_graphicsdevice_reference_stencil_test.cpp`: stamp stencil=0x05, assign a compare `DepthStencilState` with baked-in `ReferenceStencil=0x05` (would PASS at face value), then call `setReferenceStencilProperty(0x99)` directly and draw — a correct implementation must REJECT (0x99≠0x05) but both backends incorrectly PASS (the override never reaches the GPU, so the state's own stale 0x05 is still what's compared). Kept both variants registered as documented known failures. EasyGL ctest: 2079/2082 (2 pre-existing failures + this task's new, expected `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil` failure). Vulkan ctest: 2007/2020 (5 Task-868 confirmations, 5 Task-870 confirmations, this task's new Task-872 confirmation, `Vulkan_FillMode_WireFrame`'s pre-existing flake, and `Vulkan_DepthBias`'s usual sub-case — all pre-existing/documented or newly-expected). |
| 320 | Document depth/stencil support matrix                          | ✅      | **Closes Phase 37.** New `docs/depthstencilstate-support.md` synthesizes Tasks 311–319's findings, mirroring Task 300's `docs/sampler-state-support.md` precedent: full API-conformance summary, a per-backend support-matrix table, the central Task 870 finding (Vulkan's `DepthBufferFunction`/stencil-test support almost entirely fake, confirmed 5 times), Task 315's EasyGL stencil-buffer fix (`SDL_GL_STENCIL_SIZE`), the test-design lesson learned mid-phase (a test where every check expects the same outcome can't distinguish "works" from "stencil test bypassed"), and links to all newly-tracked bugs (866's remaining `RasterizerState` portion, 869, 870, 871, 872) rather than duplicating their write-ups. Doc-only task, no new tests; cross-checked every linked task/bug number against `plan_graphics.md`'s current wording before publishing — all consistent. |

## Phase 39 — RenderTarget2D and RenderTargetCube completeness

| #   | Task                                                                          | Status | Notes                         |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | ----------------------------- |
| 331 | Audit `RenderTarget2D` constructors and properties against FNA                | ✅      | **Opens Phase 39.** Audited CNA's `RenderTarget2D` against FNA's `Graphics/RenderTarget2D.cs` line-by-line: all 3 public constructor overloads, `DepthStencilFormat`/`MultiSampleCount`/`RenderTargetUsage` properties, and `Dispose(bool)`'s "still bound" guard already matched FNA. **Real, fixed finding**: FNA's `RenderTarget2D` declares a public `IsContentLost` property (always `false`) and a public `ContentLost` event (never raised) — `RenderTargetCube` already has both (and so do `DynamicVertexBuffer`/`DynamicIndexBuffer`), but `RenderTarget2D` had neither. Fixed by adding `getIsContentLostProperty()`/`ContentLost` to `RenderTarget2D.hpp`, mirroring `RenderTargetCube`'s exact pattern. **Two real, confirmed, NOT-fixed-here gaps found and deliberately deferred to their dedicated Phase 39 tasks** (mirroring how Phase 38's audit deferred `DepthBias`/`ScissorTest` specifics to their own tasks): (1) the constructor computes level count as `mipMap ? 1 : 1` — always 1 regardless of `mipMap`, because no backend's `CreateRenderTarget2D` accepts a mip count or allocates RT mip storage at all (confirmed by reading `EasyGLRenderTargetBackend`'s constructor and all 4 backends' `IGraphicsBackend::CreateRenderTarget2D` signatures) — tracked as Task 336. (2) `preferredMultiSampleCount` is stored verbatim in `multiSampleCount_` and returned as-is; FNA clamps it via `FNA3D_GetMaxMultiSampleCount`, but CNA never queries backend capability and no backend's `CreateRenderTarget2D` even accepts a multisample count — tracked as Task 337. New test `examples/easygl_rendertarget2d_properties_test.cpp` (registered for both backends as `EasyGL_RenderTarget2D_Properties`/`Vulkan_RenderTarget2D_Properties`, 15 checks each) exercises all 3 constructor overloads' property defaults/explicit values, confirms the new `IsContentLost`/`ContentLost` fix, and pins the two deferred gaps' CURRENT values with explanatory comments (rather than silently skipping them) so Tasks 336/337 have a regression marker to update when they land. EasyGL ctest: 2301/2304 (3 pre-existing/documented, unchanged). Vulkan ctest: 2228/2241 (13 pre-existing/documented, unchanged). |
| 332 | Audit `RenderTargetCube` constructors and properties against FNA              | ✅      | Audited CNA's `RenderTargetCube` against FNA's `Graphics/RenderTargetCube.cs` line-by-line: the single public constructor overload (with default args covering FNA's 2-overload split), `DepthStencilFormat`/`MultiSampleCount`/`RenderTargetUsage` properties, and `IsContentLost`/`ContentLost` already matched FNA (these were already correct, unlike `RenderTarget2D` before Task 331). **Two real, fixed findings**: (1) `GetTypeName()` was never overridden, so a `RenderTargetCube` reported itself as `"Microsoft.Xna.Framework.Graphics.TextureCube"` (inherited from the base class) instead of its own type name — fixed by adding the override, matching `RenderTarget2D`'s existing pattern. (2) Attempted to add `RenderTarget2D`'s `Dispose(bool)` "still bound" guard (present on `RenderTarget2D`, absent on `RenderTargetCube`) but this **does not compile and cannot be added without an architecture change**: `RenderTargetBinding` only stores `Texture*`, and `RenderTargetCube` inherits `GraphicsResource` directly, not `Texture` (Task 863's already-tracked architectural gap) — so a `RenderTargetCube*` can never be compared against `RenderTargetBinding::getRenderTargetProperty()`. Confirmed while investigating: `GraphicsDevice::SetRenderTarget(RenderTargetCube*, CubeMapFace)` never pushes anything into `currentRenderTargets_` at all (unlike the `RenderTarget2D` overload), so `GraphicsDevice::GetRenderTargets()` can never reflect a bound cube face either — this is a direct, confirmed downstream consequence of Task 863, not fixed here (no architecture change per NEXT.md §9 without a deliberate scoped design pass). **Known lead confirmed**: `RenderTargetCube.cpp`'s constructor takes `bool mipMap` but never passes it to `TextureCube`'s mip-aware constructor — it uses the protected NOXNA `TextureCube(device, size, format, backend)` constructor instead, which hardcodes `levelCount_ = 1`, and `CreateRenderTargetCube(int size)` (all 3 backends) has no mip parameter at all — same shape as Task 331's Task-336 finding for `RenderTarget2D`, already covered by the general Task 336 (not a new task). Likewise `MultiSampleCount` is stored verbatim in `multiSampleCount_`, never clamped via an `FNA3D_GetMaxMultiSampleCount`-equivalent — same shape as Task 337, already covered (not a new task). New test `examples/easygl_rendertargetcube_properties_test.cpp` (registered for both backends as `EasyGL_RenderTargetCube_Properties`/`Vulkan_RenderTargetCube_Properties`, 15 checks each) exercises the constructor's property defaults/explicit values, confirms the new `GetTypeName()` fix, and pins the two deferred gaps' CURRENT values with explanatory comments so Tasks 336/337 have a regression marker for `RenderTargetCube` too. EasyGL ctest: 3311/3314 (3 pre-existing/documented, unchanged). Vulkan ctest: 3237/3250 (13 pre-existing/documented, unchanged). |
| 333 | Verify `RenderTarget2D` can be sampled as `Texture2D` after unbinding         | ✅      | **EasyGL and Vulkan were already covered and passing**: Task 87's `EasyGL_RenderTarget2D_Readback` and Task 148's `Vulkan_RenderTarget2D_FullCycle` both already render into a `RenderTarget2D`, call `SetRenderTarget(nullptr)` to unbind it, then draw it through `SpriteBatch::Draw(const Texture2D&, ...)` and pixel-verify the result — exactly this task's goal, already passing (reconfirmed this session, no change needed). **New, real, MASSIVE bug found and confirmed on Bgfx** (not previously tracked as its own task — only an informal comment in `bgfx_render_target_usage_test.cpp`, Task 179, suspected it): `BgfxSpriteBatchBackend::Draw` unconditionally `static_cast<const BgfxTextureBackend&>(texture)` on whatever `ITextureBackend` it's handed. When handed a `RenderTarget2D`'s backend (a `BgfxRenderTargetBackend`, which inherits only `IRenderTargetBackend`/`ITextureBackend` — NOT `BgfxTextureBackend`, an unrelated sibling class), this reads `BgfxRenderTargetBackend::fbo` (its first data member, a `bgfx::FrameBufferHandle`) at the same offset where `BgfxTextureBackend::textureHandle` (a `bgfx::TextureHandle`) lives — both are `struct { uint16_t idx; }` (confirmed in `bgfx.h`'s `BGFX_HANDLE` macro), so this compiles and doesn't crash, but it hands bgfx a **framebuffer handle index where a texture handle index was expected** — completely wrong data, not the RT's actual `colorTex` color attachment, since bgfx allocates framebuffer and texture handles from separate internal pools. Confirmed by direct layout analysis (100% deterministic, no ambiguity) and by a new runtime test (`bgfx_render_target_sample_test.cpp`) that exercises this exact path — it does not crash (bgfx has no strong handle-type validation), consistent with silently sampling the wrong/garbage texture rather than throwing. Cannot be pixel-verified (Bgfx has no usable GPU readback in this project, per Task 179/249/278's precedent) — tracked as **Task 873**, not fixed here (contained to Bgfx, but needs a scoped fix + a non-visual verification of the extracted handle, not a rushed guess). |
| 334 | Verify `RenderTargetCube` can be sampled as `TextureCube` after unbinding     | ✅      | **No existing test covered this at all** (unlike Task 333's `RenderTarget2D` case, which Tasks 87/148 already covered) — `vulkan_rtcube_test.cpp` (Task 142) renders into all 6 faces but only proves the backbuffer isn't corrupted afterward, never samples the cube back out. Wrote new tests on all 3 backends. **EasyGL: PASSES, architecturally sound and now pixel-verified** — new `easygl_rendertargetcube_sample_test.cpp` (`EasyGL_RenderTargetCube_SampleAfterUnbind`) renders solid blue into all 6 `RenderTargetCube` faces, unbinds, samples via `EnvironmentMapEffect` (`EnvironmentMapAmount=1`), and gets an exact blue match — `EnvironmentMapEffect::FillGpuDrawParams` reads `environmentMap_->GetBackend()` (shared by `RenderTargetCube`) and `EasyGLGraphicsBackend` calls `params.envMap->BindGL()` through virtual dispatch (no unsafe cast); both `EasyGLTextureCubeBackend`/`EasyGLRenderTargetCubeBackend` override it correctly (confirmed by code reading). **Vulkan: FAILS, two distinct real bugs found and confirmed** via new `vulkan_rendertargetcube_sample_test.cpp` (`Vulkan_RenderTargetCube_SampleAfterUnbind`, correctly RED in ctest, not a false pass): (1) An initial `Clear()`-only version of the test (mirroring `easygl_rt_roundtrip_test.cpp`'s Clear-only pattern, which is EasyGL-only, never ported to Vulkan) hit a Vulkan validation error — every cube-face image layer stayed `VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_UNDEFINED` instead of transitioning to `SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL`, because `VulkanGraphicsBackend::Clear()` only records a single global clear-colour scalar (`clearR_/G_/B_/A_`) and does not register the currently-bound RT in `RecordCommandBuffer`'s `usedRTs` list — only an actual draw call does, so a "bind RT, Clear, unbind" pattern with no draw call in between silently never gets a render pass recorded at all on Vulkan. Tracked as **Task 875**. (2) After switching the test to use a real `SpriteBatch` draw into each face (matching Task 142's own pattern) to work around finding (1), the test still fails — centre pixel renders black instead of blue, meaning the `RenderTargetCube`'s actual rendered content is not what `EnvironmentMapEffect` samples back out on Vulkan. A `VUID-vkCmdDrawIndexed-renderPass-02684` render-pass-compatibility validation warning also appears, but this is a red herring, not the cause — the SAME warning already appears in Task 142's own currently-passing `vulkan_rtcube_test.cpp` (reconfirmed by rerunning it), so it's pre-existing, cosmetic, and orthogonal to the actual pixel failure. `VulkanRenderTargetCubeBackend` correctly implements `IVulkanCubeSamplable` (mirroring `VulkanTextureCubeBackend`, confirmed by code reading in Task 332), so the sampling *path* is architecturally sound like EasyGL's — but the black result proves something in the actual chain (candidates: `SpriteBatch`-into-cube-face pixel correctness was never itself verified before this session — Task 142 only checks the backbuffer isn't corrupted, not that the faces got the right colour; or `GetOrCreateEnvMapDescSet`'s per-frame descriptor-set cache/write). Root cause not fully isolated — tracked as **Task 876**, not fixed here, needs its own dedicated investigation. **Bgfx: same unsafe-cast bug shape as Task 873, confirmed** — `BgfxGraphicsBackend`'s `envMapping` branch does `static_cast<const BgfxTextureCubeBackend&>(*params.envMap)`; a `RenderTargetCube`'s backend is a `BgfxRenderTargetCubeBackend` (unrelated sibling, inherits only `IRenderTargetCubeBackend`/`ITextureCubeBackend`), whose first data member is `bgfx::FrameBufferHandle fbo` (its actual colour texture, `cubeTex`, is the SECOND member) — casting reads `fbo` where `BgfxTextureCubeBackend::handle` should be, same framebuffer-handle-for-texture-handle confusion as Task 873, one level over. New `bgfx_render_target_cube_sample_test.cpp` (`Bgfx_RenderTargetCube_SampleAfterUnbind`) confirms no crash (consistent with silent wrong-data sampling, can't be pixel-verified — no Bgfx GPU readback). Tracked as **Task 874**, not fixed here. EasyGL ctest: 3312/3315 (3 pre-existing/documented, unchanged). Vulkan ctest: 3237/3251 (13 pre-existing/documented + 1 new correctly-failing test = 14, matches expectation). Bgfx ctest: 3224/3224 (100%). |
| 335 | Verify depth buffer creation for render targets                               | ✅      | Task 331's property test only proves `getDepthStencilFormatProperty()` returns whatever was passed at construction — a stored value never connected to any GPU resource. This task proves the FUNCTIONAL behavior: does a render target's depth buffer actually gate draws while rendering into it? New backend-agnostic test `examples/rendertarget2d_depth_test.cpp` (registered as `EasyGL_RenderTarget2D_DepthBuffer`/`Vulkan_RenderTarget2D_DepthBuffer`): creates a `RenderTarget2D` with `DepthFormat::Depth24Stencil8`, binds it, draws a near GREEN quad then a far RED quad at the same screen position with `DepthStencilState::Default` (depth test+write, LessEqual) — if the RT's depth buffer is real and wired into the offscreen-draw pipeline, RED must be rejected, leaving GREEN. Unbinds and samples the RT back via `SpriteBatch` (the already-proven-good `RenderTarget2D` sampling-after-unbind path from Tasks 87/148) to isolate "does depth actually function inside the RT" as the only new variable. **PASSES on both EasyGL and Vulkan** — the core depth-testing functionality genuinely works on both backends' render targets, not just the backbuffer. **3 real, scoped, format-fidelity gaps found (not fixed here, folded into new Task 877)**, distinct from "does it work at all": (1) EasyGL's `EasyGLRenderTargetBackend`/`EasyGLRenderTargetCubeBackend` both hardcode `DepthComponent24` for their depth renderbuffer regardless of the requested `DepthStencilFormat` — a `Depth24Stencil8` request silently gets NO stencil bits at all (confirmed by code reading, `depthRbo_.set_storage(DepthComponent24, ...)` in both classes, no format branching). (2) Vulkan's `VulkanRenderTargetBackend` constructor takes `bool /*hasDepth*/` but drops it entirely (comment: "Depth image (always created to match the 2-attachment rtRenderPass_)") — every `RenderTarget2D` gets a depth buffer allocated regardless of whether `DepthFormat::None` was requested, using the device's single global `depthFormat_` (the same one used for the backbuffer) rather than anything derived from the requested format. (3) Bgfx (code-reading only, no new pixel test — Task 179's existing `Bgfx_RenderTargetUsage` test already smoke-covers RT2D depth bind/unbind without crashing) is actually the most FNA-faithful of the three here: `BgfxRenderTargetBackend` correctly respects `hasDepth` (skips depth entirely if not requested) and always uses `D24S8` (which does include stencil bits) — but still doesn't differentiate exact requested `DepthFormat` values (e.g. `Depth16` would still get `D24S8`). EasyGL ctest: 3313/3316 (3 pre-existing/documented, unchanged). Vulkan ctest: 3239/3252 (13 documented, unchanged — new test passes). |
| 336 | Verify render target mipmap support or explicitly reject unsupported mips     | ✅      | **Confirmed the gap and implemented a real fix on EasyGL**, following FNA3D's actual native-source mechanism (`FNA3D_Driver_OpenGL.c`'s `OPENGL_ResolveTarget`: `if (target->levelCount > 1) { ...glGenerateMipmap... }` — XNA/FNA auto-regenerates a render target's full mip chain from level-0 content when it stops being the active target; games never render into non-zero mip levels directly). Previously, `RenderTarget2D`/`RenderTargetCube`'s `mipMap` constructor parameter was a complete no-op: `LevelCount` was hardcoded via a `mipMap ? 1 : 1` ternary (Task 331/332 findings), and no backend's `CreateRenderTarget2D`/`CreateRenderTargetCube` accepted a mip parameter at all. **Fixed on EasyGL** (mirroring Task 276's `TextureCube` mip-fix shape): `RenderTarget2D.cpp`/`RenderTargetCube.cpp` now compute `LevelCount` via `CalculateMipLevels` (matching `Texture2D`/`TextureCube`'s own pattern) and thread `mipMap` through to `CreateRenderTarget2D(..., mipMap)`/`CreateRenderTargetCube(size, mipMap)` (new parameter added to `IGraphicsBackend`'s interface, all 3 backends' signatures updated). `EasyGLRenderTargetBackend`/`EasyGLRenderTargetCubeBackend` now pre-allocate GPU storage for every mip level at construction (not just level 0), and — critically, since `IRenderTargetBackend::UnbindAsRenderTarget()`/`IRenderTargetCubeBackend::UnbindAsRenderTarget()` were confirmed to be **completely dead code, never called by anything in this project** (grep confirmed zero call sites before this fix) — `EasyGLGraphicsBackend` now tracks the currently-bound single RT/cube-face (`currentRt2D_`/`currentRtCube_`) and actually invokes `UnbindAsRenderTarget()` when switching away from one (in `SetRenderTarget2D`, a new `SetRenderTargetCubeFace` override, and `SetRenderTargets`' MRT path), which now calls `generate_mipmap()` when `levelCount_ > 1` — mirroring FNA3D's exact resolve-time mechanism. **Rigorously pixel-verified, not just property-checked**: new `examples/easygl_rendertarget2d_mip_test.cpp` (`EasyGL_RenderTarget2D_MipComplete`) fills a `mipMap=true` RT with solid blue, unbinds it (triggering the fix), then samples it with `TextureFilter::Anisotropic` (a mip-requiring filter that renders solid BLACK on GL-incomplete mip chains — the exact, already-established Task 867/299 diagnostic signature) — confirms BLUE (mip-complete). Verified the test genuinely discriminates: temporarily forcing `mipMap=false` reproduces the solid-black failure signature, then reverted. Updated the two Task 331/332 property tests' previously-pinned "known gap, LevelCount==1" assertions to their new correct values (`LevelCount==7` for a 64×64 full mip chain) — both now pass. **Not fixed, explicitly deferred**: Vulkan and Bgfx's `CreateRenderTarget2D`/`CreateRenderTargetCube` accept-and-ignore the new `mipMap` parameter (no functional change) — `LevelCount` is still computed correctly on all 3 backends (shared C++), matching the project's own established precedent for regular `Texture2D`/`TextureCube` (Task 867: property correct project-wide, GPU-side mip support lags per-backend) — tracked as new **Task 878**. MRT (`SetRenderTargets` with count>1) + per-target mips is also out of scope (documented in code comments) — MRT targets are never tracked as `currentRt2D_`/`currentRtCube_`, so switching away from MRT mode cannot trigger their mip regeneration; negligible real-world overlap. Full regression: EasyGL ctest 3312/3317 (3 pre-existing/documented failures unchanged; 2 additional `CueTest` failures in the full run reconfirmed as pre-existing flaky/unrelated audio tests by re-running them in isolation — both pass). Vulkan ctest: 3239/3252 (13 pre-existing/documented, unchanged). Bgfx ctest: 3224/3224 (100%, unchanged). |
| 337 | Verify MSAA render target creation and resolve behavior                       | ✅      | **Confirmed the gap and implemented a real fix on EasyGL**, following FNA's actual mechanism (`RenderTargetCube.cs`/`RenderTarget2D`: `MultiSampleCount = FNA3D_GetMaxMultiSampleCount(device, format, MathHelper.ClosestMSAAPower(preferred))`, then FNA3D's `OPENGL_GenColorRenderbuffer` allocates a real multisampled color renderbuffer via `glRenderbufferStorageMultisample`, resolved into the sampleable texture via `glBlitFramebuffer` in the same `OPENGL_ResolveTarget` function already touched for Task 336's mip fix). Previously, `preferredMultiSampleCount` was stored completely verbatim with zero clamping and zero backend wiring (Task 331 finding). **Fixed on EasyGL** (mirroring Task 336's exact fix shape and reusing the same resolve-on-unbind mechanism): added `ClosestMSAAPower` (mirroring FNA's `MathHelper.ClosestMSAAPower` bit-twiddling exactly) to `RenderTarget2D.cpp`/`RenderTargetCube.cpp`; threaded `multiSampleCount` through `IGraphicsBackend::CreateRenderTarget2D`/`CreateRenderTargetCube` (new parameter, all 3 backends' signatures updated); added `GetMultiSampleCount()` to `IRenderTargetBackend`/`IRenderTargetCubeBackend` so `RenderTarget2D`/`RenderTargetCube` query the backend's REAL, device-capability-clamped value after construction (via `glGetIntegerv(GL_MAX_SAMPLES, ...)`, mirroring `EasyGLGraphicsBackend`'s own existing backbuffer-MSAA clamp logic) instead of reporting the raw constructor argument — unlike `LevelCount` (Task 336), `MultiSampleCount` is inherently backend/device-capability-dependent even in real FNA, so per-backend divergence (EasyGL reports a real clamped value; Vulkan/Bgfx honestly report `0`, not implemented) is the *correct* design, not a shortcut. `EasyGLRenderTargetBackend`/`EasyGLRenderTargetCubeBackend` now render into a real multisampled color (and depth, if requested) renderbuffer and resolve it via `glBlitFramebuffer` into the single-sample `colorTex_`/`cubeTex_` face when the target is unbound (same `UnbindAsRenderTarget()` call site as Task 336's mip regeneration, correctly ordered: resolve first, then regenerate mips from the resolved content). RenderTargetCube reuses ONE shared multisample renderbuffer across all 6 faces (matching FNA's single `glColorBuffer`, since only one face renders at a time) and tracks the most-recently-bound face so the resolve targets the correct face image. **Rigorously pixel-verified with a differential anti-aliasing proof, not just plumbing**: new `examples/easygl_rendertarget2d_msaa_test.cpp` (`EasyGL_RenderTarget2D_MsaaResolve`) renders a diagonal-edged triangle into two RTs (`MultiSampleCount=0` and `=8`), resolves, samples back with `SamplerState.PointClamp`, and reads the centre pixel row: confirms the `MultiSampleCount=0` row is purely binary (hard aliased edge — no averaging exists) while the `MultiSampleCount=8` row has genuinely intermediate (partially-covered) pixel values — proof that real sub-pixel coverage averaging happened during the resolve, not just that solid colors survive it (a solid-fill-only test, like the existing backbuffer MSAA test, cannot distinguish these two cases). Updated the two Task 331/332 property tests' MultiSampleCount assertions to accept either EasyGL's real clamped value or Vulkan/Bgfx's honest `0`, while still catching a blind pass-through (e.g. literal `9999`) as a failure on any backend. **Not fixed, explicitly deferred**: Vulkan and Bgfx accept-and-ignore the new `multiSampleCount` parameter and report `MultiSampleCount=0` — tracked as new **Task 879**. While in this file, also fixed a documentation typo from the Task 336 session: several "Task 336/877" code comments incorrectly referenced Task 877 (the unrelated `DepthStencilFormat` task) instead of Task 878 (the actual Vulkan/Bgfx mip task) — corrected to 878 in all Vulkan/Bgfx `CreateRenderTarget2D`/`CreateRenderTargetCube` comments. Full regression, all 3 backends: EasyGL ctest 3315/3318 (3 pre-existing/documented, unchanged). Vulkan ctest 3239/3252 (13 pre-existing/documented, unchanged). Bgfx ctest 3224/3224 (100%, unchanged). |
| 338 | Verify setting `nullptr` render target returns to backbuffer                  | ✅      | **Core routing already extensively proven** — every existing `RenderTarget2D`/`RenderTargetCube` test that unbinds and then calls `GetBackBufferData` (Task 87/148/180/142, dozens more) already confirms `SetRenderTarget(nullptr)`/`SetRenderTargets({})` correctly routes subsequent reads/draws to the real backbuffer, not a stale RT — nothing new to add there. **Found and fixed a real, previously-undiscovered gap while auditing FNA's actual `SetRenderTargets` source** (`GraphicsDevice.cs`): FNA's `SetRenderTargets` **always** resets `Viewport` and `ScissorRectangle` to `(0, 0, newWidth, newHeight)` — the new render target's dimensions when binding, or the backbuffer's dimensions when unbinding (`// Set the viewport/scissor to the size of the backbuffer` / `// ...of the first render target`, verbatim FNA comments). Confirmed via code reading that CNA's `GraphicsDevice::SetRenderTarget(RenderTarget2D*)`/`SetRenderTarget(RenderTargetCube*, CubeMapFace)`/`SetRenderTargets(vector<RenderTargetBinding>&)` **never touched `Viewport` or `ScissorRectangle` at all** — a game's previously-set `Viewport`/`ScissorRectangle` would incorrectly survive a render-target switch, unlike real XNA/FNA (e.g. binding a differently-sized RT, or unbinding back to the backbuffer, left whatever scissor/viewport was last set, rather than the FNA-mandated full-target-size reset). **Fixed**: added `GraphicsDevice::ResetViewportAndScissorForRenderTarget(int width, int height)`, called from all 3 `SetRenderTarget*`/`SetRenderTargets` methods with the newly-bound target's own `Width`/`Height` (or `PresentationParameters.BackBufferWidth`/`Height` when unbinding/emptying), matching FNA's exact placement (before any `RenderTargetUsage::DiscardContents`-triggered `Clear`). **Rigorously pixel-verified, not just property-checked**: new `examples/rendertarget_viewport_scissor_reset_test.cpp` (registered for both EasyGL and Vulkan — `ScissorRectangle` is genuinely wired to the backend's scissor test on all 3 backends, unlike `Viewport`, see below) sets `ScissorTestEnable=true` with the scissor rect covering only the right half of the backbuffer, confirms it genuinely clips (sanity check), binds+immediately-unbinds a small `RenderTarget2D`, asserts the property is `(0,0,16,16)` while bound and `(0,0,backbufferW,backbufferH)` after unbinding, then proves the REAL GPU-level effect: a subsequent full-screen draw is no longer clipped to the stale right-half rect — both halves render correctly, proving the scissor rect was genuinely reset at the backend level, not just the C++ property. **Found and deliberately NOT fixed here, a separate, deeper, pre-existing architectural gap discovered while investigating this**: `GraphicsDevice::setViewportProperty()` has **zero backend wiring on any of the 3 backends** — confirmed via grep, no `IGraphicsBackend::SetViewport` method exists at all; every backend's actual `glViewport`/equivalent call is hardcoded to the full render-target/window size (`device_.set_viewport(0, 0, physW, physH)` on EasyGL, confirmed at multiple call sites), completely ignoring whatever `GraphicsDevice.Viewport` is set to. This means CNA's `Viewport` property is currently decorative at the GPU level — a game requesting a sub-region viewport (e.g. split-screen, render-to-atlas-subrect) would see zero effect on any backend. This is a much larger, pre-existing, three-backend-wide gap, unrelated specifically to render-target switching — tracked as new **Task 880**, not fixed here (my Task 338 fix makes the *property* correctly track FNA's reset behavior, which is real progress and testable via `ScissorRectangle`'s already-wired backend path, but `Viewport`'s own GPU effect remains unimplemented regardless of this task). Full regression, all 3 backends: EasyGL ctest 3316/3319 (3 pre-existing/documented, unchanged). Vulkan ctest 3238/3253 (13 pre-existing/documented + 1 reconfirmed-flaky unrelated `CueTest`, unchanged). Bgfx ctest 3223/3224 (1 reconfirmed-flaky unrelated `CueTest`, unchanged). |
| 339 | Verify multiple render targets with mixed formats reject invalid combinations | ✅      | **Confirmed no bug — this task's own premise doesn't match real FNA behavior, an audit-only closure (mirrors Task 330's precedent).** Read FNA's actual `GraphicsDevice.SetRenderTargets` source line-by-line: it performs **zero explicit validation** of format/size/count mismatches between bound targets — no `ArgumentException`, no format-compatibility check, nothing. It computes `newWidth`/`newHeight` from `renderTargets[0]` only and passes everything straight through to `FNA3D_SetRenderTargets`, delegating ALL compatibility enforcement to the native driver (GL/D3D11) — "reject invalid combinations" is simply not an XNA-level behavior to replicate; whatever happens with a genuinely mismatched MRT call is native-API-dependent, by design. **Confirmed CNA's own 3 backends behave the same way** (consistent with FNA, not a divergence): `EasyGLGraphicsBackend::SetRenderTargets`, `VulkanMRTProxy`'s constructor, and `BgfxGraphicsBackend::SetRenderTargets` all compute dimensions from `rts[0]` only and build their multi-attachment framebuffer/render-pass assuming uniform size across all targets, with zero CNA-level validation — any mismatch would be caught (or not) by the underlying GL/Vulkan/bgfx call itself, exactly mirroring FNA's own delegate-and-let-the-driver-decide philosophy. **One real, confirmed, new, minor divergence found while investigating**: FNA's actual simultaneous-render-target limit is `MAX_RENDERTARGET_BINDINGS = 4` (matching XNA's documented HiDef-profile spec), implicitly enforced via a fixed-size internal array (`Array.Copy` would throw if a game passed more than 4). None of CNA's 3 backends match this: EasyGL and Bgfx both hardcode a cap of 8 (`constexpr int kMaxMRT = 8` / `kMaxAttachments = 8`, silently truncating anything beyond 8 rather than throwing), and Vulkan has no explicit cap at the CNA level at all (relies entirely on whatever `VkPhysicalDeviceLimits::maxColorAttachments` the actual GPU reports). Tracked as new **Task 881**, not fixed here (a genuine but low-priority spec-compliance gap — no test in this repo exercises more than 2 simultaneous targets, so no real game code here is affected). **Confirmed pre-existing blocker, correctly NOT touched here per NEXT.md §9's explicit "no opportunistic fixes" instruction**: even the basic, SAME-size/format 2-target MRT case is already a documented, tracked, currently-failing bug on EasyGL (`EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, Task 145 — "attachment 1 stays black with 2 render targets") — this blocks any *meaningful* deeper verification of mismatched-format MRT specifically, since the matched-format baseline itself doesn't work correctly yet; re-diagnosing or bundling a fix for it here would violate the project's own explicit "each needs its own dedicated root-cause investigation" rule. No new test added — this task's finding is a source-level architecture/behavior audit (FNA source + CNA source read side-by-side), not something a new pixel test could usefully add given (a) FNA itself has no pass/fail behavior to assert against, and (b) the one concrete regression-worthy angle (the 4-target cap divergence, Task 881) has no existing test infrastructure exercising more than 2 targets to extend. No regression risk — no code changed. |
| 340 | Document MRT limits for each backend                                          | ✅      | **Closes Phase 39.** Wrote `docs/rendertarget-support.md`, a full Phase 39 synthesis (mirroring `docs/rasterizerstate-support.md`'s Phase 38 closer) covering Tasks 331–340. Per-backend MRT limits table: EasyGL and Bgfx both hardcode a cap of 8 simultaneous color attachments (silently truncating beyond that, no error — `constexpr int kMaxMRT = 8` / `kMaxAttachments = 8`, the latter matching bgfx's own `BGFX_CONFIG_MAX_FRAME_BUFFER_ATTACHMENTS` default); Vulkan has no CNA-level cap at all, relying entirely on the actual GPU's `VkPhysicalDeviceLimits::maxColorAttachments`. **None of the 3 match FNA's real `MAX_RENDERTARGET_BINDINGS = 4`** (already found and tracked as Task 881 in Task 339). The doc also summarizes every other Phase 39 finding into one place: 2 real fixes shipped (Task 336 mip support, Task 337 MSAA support — both EasyGL-only, Vulkan/Bgfx tracked as Tasks 878/879), 1 shared-code fix (Task 338's Viewport/ScissorRectangle reset), and 6 new tracked-not-fixed gaps opened this phase (Tasks 873–881, excluding 878/879 already counted). No new tests — this is a pure documentation/synthesis task, mirroring the Phase 38 precedent exactly; no code changed, no regression risk. |

## Phase 40 — Viewport, DisplayMode, and adapter behavior

| #   | Task                                                               | Status | Notes                                     |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------ | ----------------------------------------- |
| 341 | Audit `Viewport` API against FNA                                   | ✅      | **Opens Phase 40.** Read FNA's `Graphics/Viewport.cs` line-by-line and compared against CNA's `Viewport.hpp`/`.cpp`. **`Project`/`Unproject` math already matched FNA exactly** — same `world*view*projection` composition, same `WithinEpsilon(a, 1.0f)` perspective-divide guard, same screen-space mapping formulas (`((v.X+1)*0.5*Width)+X`, `((-v.Y+1)*0.5*Height)+Y`, `v.Z*(MaxDepth-MinDepth)+MinDepth`), same inverse in `Unproject`; `ToString()`, `AspectRatio`, `Bounds`, `TitleSafeArea` all matched FNA's intent too — no math bug found. **Real, fixed finding**: `X`, `Y`, `MinDepth`, `MaxDepth` were public raw fields (`int x`, `int y`, `float minDepth`, `float maxDepth`) with no getter/setter methods at all, while `Height`/`Width` in the *same class* already correctly used the project's `getXProperty()`/`setXProperty()` convention (`DEF_PROP` macro) — a direct CLAUDE.md violation, not an established alternate style, since FNA's `Viewport.cs` treats all six as equivalent C# properties. Fixed by converting all 4 to private `X_`/`Y_`/`MinDepth_`/`MaxDepth_` backing fields with matching `DEF_PROP`/`IMPL_PROP` declarations, mirroring `Height`/`Width`'s exact existing pattern (member order now follows FNA's own C# declaration order: Height, MaxDepth, MinDepth, Width, Y, X). Blast radius confirmed via full-codebase grep before editing: only `GraphicsDevice::UpdateViewportFromWindow()` (4 lines, `GraphicsDevice.cpp`), `tests/.../ViewportTests.cpp`, and `examples/easygl_viewport_state_test.cpp` touched the raw fields directly — all updated to `getXProperty()`/`setXProperty()`-style calls. Added a new `ViewportTest.SettersUpdateEachFieldIndependently` test (the pre-existing tests only ever exercised the constructors' initial values, never the setters). Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass (header change touches everything that includes `Viewport.hpp`): EasyGL ctest 3316/3320 (3 pre-existing + 1 reconfirmed-flaky unrelated `CueTest`, unchanged). Vulkan ctest 3239/3254 (13 pre-existing + 2 reconfirmed-flaky unrelated `CueTest`, unchanged — flaky test occasionally fails twice instead of once, already an established pattern per Task 336's writeup). Bgfx ctest 3224/3225 (1 reconfirmed-flaky unrelated `CueTest`, unchanged). |
| 342 | Add tests for `Viewport::Project` with known matrices              | ✅      | Task 341's audit found a real test-coverage gap: every pre-existing `Project`/`Unproject` test used **identity** world/view/projection matrices, so the perspective-divide branch (`if (!MathHelper::WithinEpsilon(a, 1.0f))`) was never actually exercised — with identity matrices `a` is always exactly `1.0`, so the divide is always skipped. | 345 | Audit `GraphicsAdapter` API against FNA                            | ✅      | Read FNA's `Graphics/GraphicsAdapter.cs` and `FNAPlatform/SDL3_FNAPlatform.cs` (`GetGraphicsAdapters`/`GetCurrentDisplayMode`/`GetMonitorHandle`) line-by-line against CNA's `GraphicsAdapter.hpp`/`.cpp`. `IsWideScreen`, `IsProfileSupported`, `QueryRenderTargetFormat`/`QueryBackBufferFormat` (including the exact 11-format allow-list) all already matched FNA exactly. **4 real, fixed findings**: (1) `AdaptersChanged()` passed the same string for both `DeviceName` and `Description` — FNA's SDL3 platform actually gives them different values (`DeviceName` = a synthetic `\\.\DISPLAYn` Windows-style path kept even on non-Windows platforms; `Description` = the real display name); fixed to match. (2) `queryDisplayModes()` never deduplicated modes that only differ by refresh rate and iterated forward, while FNA's SDL3 platform iterates in **reverse** and **dedupes by (width,height)** — fixed to match exactly (own dedicated test, `RealDisplay_SupportedDisplayModesHasNoWidthHeightDuplicates`, genuinely reproduces the pre-fix duplicate-mode bug in this environment when reverted). (3) **A real, more severe bug found while designing the test suite**: `static GraphicsAdapter& DefaultAdapter` was a raw C++ reference bound **once** at static-init time to whatever `AdaptersChanged()` first populated — since `AdaptersChanged()` unconditionally does `adapters_.clear()` (destroying every existing `GraphicsAdapter` via their owning `unique_ptr`s) before repopulating, any *subsequent* call to `AdaptersChanged()` (an intended, public, FNA-documented usage for responding to display reconfiguration) left `DefaultAdapter` — and the 2 production call sites in `GraphicsDeviceManager.cpp` that took its address — as a **dangling reference/pointer**. Root cause: FNA's own `DefaultAdapter` is a plain `get`-only *property* (`return Adapters[0];`, always re-evaluated), never a cached field — CNA's `getDefaultAdapterProperty()` already matched this correctly, but the extra raw-field alias didn't and was also itself a `CLAUDE.md` convention violation (a C# property should never become a public C++ field). Fixed by **removing** the `DefaultAdapter` field entirely and repointing both `GraphicsDeviceManager.cpp` call sites at `getDefaultAdapterProperty()` (new regression test `DefaultAdapterRemainsValidAcrossAdaptersChanged` calls `AdaptersChanged()` twice and re-fetches the default adapter each time). (4) Stale Doxygen comments on `getDeviceIdProperty()`/`getVendorIdProperty()` claimed "Not implemented for the SDL backend", but both are actually implemented via a Linux `/sys/class/drm` PCI sysfs query (`queryPciIds()`) — corrected the comments to describe real behavior. **Confirmed, intentional, documented (not fixed) deviation from FNA**: FNA's `DeviceId`/`Revision`/`SubSystemId`/`VendorId` all unconditionally `throw NotImplementedException`; CNA's `DeviceId`/`VendorId` return real PCI IDs on Linux (0 elsewhere) and `Revision`/`SubSystemId` always return 0 — a deliberate, more-useful improvement kept as-is rather than reverted to throwing, since no code depends on the throw and reverting would be a pure functionality regression. Also removed a dead `friend class GraphicsAdapterFactory;` declaration (that class doesn't exist anywhere in the codebase; the private constructor is only ever called from `GraphicsAdapter`'s own static `AdaptersChanged()`, which needs no friendship). **Closed a real test-coverage gap**: despite a stale comment in `GraphicsResourceDisplayModeCollectionTests.cpp` claiming `GraphicsAdapter`'s static factory is "covered by integration tests", no such test existed anywhere. Added new `tests/.../GraphicsAdapterTests.cpp` (23 tests) covering every public member, using the lightweight `SDL_InitSubSystem(SDL_INIT_VIDEO)` + `GTEST_SKIP()` pattern from `GameWindowTests.cpp` (no full `Game`/`GraphicsDevice` needed — `GraphicsAdapter` only touches SDL's display APIs). **Verified genuine discriminating power** for both the DeviceName/Description swap and the mode-dedup fix by temporarily reverting each in turn and confirming the corresponding new test fails, before restoring both. **New finding, documented and deliberately deferred to Task 347** (its own dedicated scope, "verify `DisplayModeCollection` enumeration and indexing"): FNA's `DisplayModeCollection` has a `this[SurfaceFormat format]` format-filtering indexer that CNA's `DisplayModeCollection` is completely missing, while CNA *adds* `getCountProperty()` and an integer `operator[](int)` that don't exist in FNA's API at all and are currently **not** wrapped in `NOXNA` — a `CLAUDE.md` convention violation in its own right. Not touched here to keep this task scoped to `GraphicsAdapter` itself. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass (touches shared `GraphicsDeviceManager.cpp`, so all 3 needed it): EasyGL ctest 3347/3350 (3 pre-existing, unchanged). Vulkan ctest 3269/3284 (13 pre-existing + 2 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest`; one `Vulkan_DepthBias` 0/4 anomaly during the full run reconfirmed as the normal documented 3/4 pattern when re-run in isolation — transient, not a regression). Bgfx ctest 3254/3255 (1 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest`). |
| 346 | Add safe fallback for systems where display mode enumeration fails | ✅      | Verified the fallback chain already present in `GraphicsAdapter.cpp` (from before this task) is structurally complete: `AdaptersChanged()` falls back to a single synthetic 800×480 "Default Display" adapter (`DeviceName="\\.\DISPLAY1"`, `Description="Default Display"`) when `SDL_GetDisplays()` fails; `queryDisplayModes()`/`queryCurrentDisplayMode()` fall back similarly when `SDL_GetFullscreenDisplayModes()`/`SDL_GetCurrentDisplayMode()` fail for a display that *does* enumerate (including the edge case where every returned mode pointer is `nullptr` — already correctly handled by the existing post-loop `if (result.empty())` check). **Found and fixed one real small bug while verifying**: the no-displays branch in `AdaptersChanged()` never called `SDL_free(displays)` before its early return — a latent memory leak in the (unlikely but possible) case where SDL returns a non-null array with `count<=0`; fixed with an unconditional `SDL_free(displays)` (documented safe on `nullptr` per SDL3's own docs) before the branch. **Added a genuine, non-fabricated regression test** for the "enumeration fails" path: confirmed via a standalone probe that `SDL_GetDisplays()` reliably returns `nullptr`/`count=0` with error "Video subsystem has not been initialized" whenever `SDL_INIT_VIDEO` hasn't been initialized — exactly the real headless-CI scenario this task is about, not a mocked/fabricated one (no SDL mocking layer exists anywhere in this project). New `HeadlessFallback_NoVideoSubsystemProducesSingleSyntheticAdapter` test in `GraphicsAdapterTests.cpp`: skips (via `GTEST_SKIP()`) if `SDL_WasInit(SDL_INIT_VIDEO)!=0` (something else in the process left video initialized — every other SDL-touching test in this project balances its own `SDL_InitSubSystem`/`SDL_QuitSubSystem` calls, so this reliably holds regardless of run order), otherwise calls `AdaptersChanged()` directly and verifies the single synthetic adapter's exact `DeviceName`/`Description`/mode/`CurrentDisplayMode` values, then restores a real enumeration afterward (`SDL_InitSubSystem`+`AdaptersChanged()`, deliberately without a matching `Quit`) so later tests in the same binary aren't left with the synthetic single-adapter cache. Verified this test genuinely exercises the real path (not skipped) both standalone and interleaved with other SDL-touching test files (`GameWindowTests.cpp`). Vulkan ctest 3274/3288 (13 pre-existing + 1 reconfirmed order-dependent flake — `Vulkan_FillMode_WireFrame` this run). Bgfx ctest 3258/3259 (1 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest`). |
| 347 | Verify `DisplayModeCollection` enumeration and indexing            | ✅      | Directly closes the finding from Task 345's `GraphicsAdapter` audit. Compared CNA's `DisplayModeCollection` against FNA's `Graphics/DisplayModeCollection.cs` line-by-line. Enumeration (`begin()`/`end()`, correctly `NOXNA`-wrapped) and integer indexing (`operator[](int)`, out-of-range throwing `std::out_of_range` for negative/`==count`/`>count`) both already matched FNA's `GetEnumerator()` behavior and were already fully tested. **Fixed the real gap found during Task 345**: FNA's `DisplayModeCollection` has a real, public `this[SurfaceFormat format]` indexer (filters to only the modes matching the given format, preserving original order, eagerly building a list — not lazy) that CNA was missing **entirely** — added as a second `operator[](SurfaceFormat)` overload (C++ allows overloading `operator[]` by parameter type, so it coexists cleanly with the existing integer overload; no ambiguity). Also fixed the **inverse** problem: CNA's `getCountProperty()` and integer `operator[]` don't exist in FNA's API at all, and were not wrapped in `NOXNA` — a `CLAUDE.md` violation in their own right — added `NOXNA` to both (matching `begin()`/`end()`'s existing correct treatment in the same class; `NOXNA` is a no-op marker macro, zero behavior change). New tests: `IndexBySurfaceFormatReturnsOnlyMatchingModesInOriginalOrder`, `IndexBySurfaceFormatReturnsEmptyWhenNoModeMatches`, `IndexBySurfaceFormatOnEmptyCollectionReturnsEmpty`. **Verified genuine discriminating power**: temporarily replaced the new indexer's filter with "return every mode unconditionally" and confirmed the 2 filter-correctness tests fail (the empty-collection test still trivially passes, as expected — it tests a different edge case), before reverting. | 348 | Verify backbuffer size follows window size when appropriate        | ✅      | Opens Phase 40's third sub-area (Viewport: Tasks 341–344; adapter/DisplayMode: Tasks 345–347, both closed). Traced the full real-window-resize chain end-to-end: `SDL_EVENT_WINDOW_RESIZED`/`SDL_EVENT_WINDOW_PIXEL_SIZE_CHANGED` → `Game`'s event loop calls `GameWindow::updateFromSDL()` → `GameWindow.ClientSizeChanged` fires → `GraphicsDeviceManager::INTERNAL_OnClientSizeChanged` → `GraphicsDevice::UpdateViewportFromWindow()`. **Real mechanism difference from FNA confirmed and correctly NOT "fixed"**: FNA's `INTERNAL_OnClientSizeChanged` immediately calls `ApplyChanges()`, which forwards the new physical window size into `PresentationParameters.BackBufferWidth`/`Height` (a full device Reset on every resize); CNA's own version *deliberately* does not (an existing, accurate source comment explains why: doing so "would forward the new physical window size as the virtual resolution, corrupting the game's logical coordinate space and breaking scaling on all backends") — confirmed this is real, working, and consistent with CNA's `PresentationMode::FixedHeightDynamicWidth` default (`Viewport` height stays pinned to `PreferredBackBufferHeight`, width adapts to the window's live aspect ratio). **A genuine discovery made while verifying the new test's discriminating power** (temporarily disabling the `ClientSizeChanged` subscription and re-running): CNA's `Viewport` tracking does *not* actually depend on that event at all — `GraphicsDevice::Present()` unconditionally calls `UpdateViewportFromWindow()` every single frame, a stronger, event-independent guarantee than FNA's. This meant the event-chain itself needed its own, separately-discriminating check (confirmed by instead breaking `GameWindow::OnClientSizeChanged()`'s `Raise()` call, which correctly failed only that specific check). **Closed a real, confirmed test-coverage gap**: Task 227's `easygl_backbuffer_resize_test.cpp` only exercises the GDM-API-driven resize path (`setPreferredBackBufferWidth/Height` + `ApplyChanges()`); nobody had tested an actual OS-level window resize. New `examples/easygl_real_window_resize_test.cpp` (`EasyGL_RealWindowResize`) calls `SDL_SetWindowSize()` directly on the live window (bypassing `GraphicsDeviceManager` entirely) and polls until the async X11/Xvfb resize event propagates, then asserts: (1) `Viewport` height stays pinned, (2) `Viewport` width changes, (3) `PresentationParameters.BackBufferWidth`/`Height` do **not** change (a regression marker pinning the confirmed FNA divergence above), (4) `GameWindow.ClientSizeChanged` fires at least once (the FNA API event contract, checked independently of finding #2's `Present()`-driven redundancy). EasyGL-only (matching Task 227's own precedent — Vulkan/Bgfx's `GetViewportSize()` return the raw physical window size unconditionally, with no `FixedHeightDynamicWidth`-style virtual-resolution scaling at all, so this test's fixed-height assertion doesn't apply there). **New findings confirmed and deliberately deferred to a new task, not fixed here** (a full presentation-mode implementation spans multiple backends, too large for this audit task): `PresentationMode::Letterbox`/`Overscan`/`Stretch`/`NativeBackBuffer` are not distinctly implemented in `EasyGLGraphicsBackend::getLogicalSize()` — only `FixedHeightDynamicWidth` has real, mode-specific logic; every other mode silently falls through to the same generic fallback, contradicting each mode's documented behavior (most notably `NativeBackBuffer`'s explicit "no scaling" promise). Vulkan/Bgfx don't implement any virtual-resolution/presentation-mode scaling at all in `GetViewportSize()` (always raw physical size); Vulkan's `SetVirtualResolution()` instead triggers `RecreateSwapchain()`, a materially different mechanism that needs its own dedicated investigation before concluding what (if anything) is actually broken there. Tracked as new **Task 882**. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass (new EasyGL-only test + inert `CMakeLists.txt` change, no shared production code touched): EasyGL ctest 3351/3355 (3 pre-existing + 1 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest`). Vulkan ctest 3274/3288 (13 pre-existing + 1 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest`, no order-dependent flake this run). Bgfx ctest 3259/3259 (100%). |
| 349 | Verify viewport reset after backbuffer resize                      | ✅      | Read FNA's `Graphics/GraphicsDevice.cs` `Reset()` line-by-line: it unconditionally does `Viewport = new Viewport(0, 0, PresentationParameters.BackBufferWidth, BackBufferHeight)`, discarding *any* previously-set Viewport (including a custom sub-region one, e.g. split-screen) — and `GraphicsDeviceManager.ApplyChanges()` always calls `Reset()`. FNA's `Present()`, by contrast, never touches `Viewport` at all (confirmed by reading it too). **Real, fixed bug found in CNA's equivalent mechanism**: CNA has no FNA-style `Reset()` wired into `GraphicsDeviceManager::ApplyChanges()` (a separate, already-tracked gap); instead `GraphicsDevice::UpdateViewportFromWindow()` re-derives the Viewport from the backend's current logical size, called both from `ApplyChanges()`'s backend-sync path and unconditionally every `GraphicsDevice::Present()` call (Task 348 already established this happens every frame via `Game::EndDraw()`). Its "did the size actually change?" guard compared the backend's freshly-computed size against **`viewport_`'s own current width/height** — meaning a game-set custom Viewport (legal FNA usage, e.g. split-screen, with dimensions that legitimately differ from the backbuffer) was silently stomped back to full-window size on the very next frame's `Present()`, even with **no resize at all**. Root cause: the check conflated "the game's current Viewport setting" with "the last size this method itself observed" — two different things. **Fixed** by adding dedicated `lastKnownViewportWidth_`/`lastKnownViewportHeight_` private fields to `GraphicsDevice` (`GraphicsDevice.hpp`), tracking the backend-derived size independently of whatever the game has set `Viewport` to; `UpdateViewportFromWindow()` now compares against these instead of `viewport_`'s own dimensions. This correctly preserves a custom Viewport across frames with no resize (matching FNA's `Present()` never touching it) while still resetting it to `(0,0,newW,newH)` the moment a genuine resize is detected (matching FNA's `Reset()`). **Second, independent finding surfaced while building the test** (documented, not separately fixed — it's a transient self-healing condition, not a persistent bug): `GraphicsDeviceManager::ApplyChanges()` calls `SDL_SetWindowSize()` then immediately queries the window size in the same call stack (via `SetVirtualResolution()`'s internal `UpdateViewportFromWindow()` call) with no SDL event pump in between; on X11/Xvfb this resize is asynchronous, so that immediate query can observe a stale physical size and compute a transiently-wrong viewport width for one call. This self-heals within a frame or two, since `Present()` re-derives the Viewport every frame and picks up the correct size once the async resize propagates — consistent with why Task 227's own resize test only ever asserted `Viewport` height exactly and checked width via `>0` rather than an exact value. New shared test `examples/viewport_reset_after_resize_test.cpp` (registered for both EasyGL and Vulkan, matching the task's scope column, mirroring Task 338's `rendertarget_viewport_scissor_reset_test.cpp` cross-backend precedent): sets a custom `Viewport(10,20,100,60)`, confirms it survives one full frame's `Draw()`+auto-`Present()` with no resize, then triggers a real resize via `GraphicsDeviceManager::setPreferredBackBufferWidth/HeightProperty()` + `ApplyChanges()`, and polls across frames (mirroring Task 348's `easygl_real_window_resize_test.cpp` polling precedent, needed precisely because of the async-propagation finding above) until both the physical window size and `Viewport` settle to `(0,0,640,360)`. **Verified genuine discriminating power**: temporarily reverted the `lastKnownViewportWidth_`/`Height_` fix back to comparing against `viewport_`'s own width/height and confirmed exactly the 4 "survives a frame's Present()" assertions failed (custom Viewport got stomped to `(0,0,800,480)` after a single frame with no resize), while the 4 post-resize assertions still passed — before restoring the fix. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass: EasyGL ctest 3353/3356 (3 pre-existing, unchanged, no `CueTest` flake this run). Vulkan ctest 3275/3289 (13 pre-existing + 1 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest`, unchanged). Bgfx ctest 3258/3259 (1 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest`, unchanged; no new EasyGL/Vulkan-only test registered here, matching Task 348's precedent, but still fully rebuilt/retested per project discipline since `GraphicsDevice.hpp`/`.cpp` are shared). |
| 350 | Document adapter/display limitations for non-desktop platforms     | ✅      | **Closes Phase 40.** Wrote `docs/viewport-displaymode-adapter-support.md`, a full Phase 40 synthesis (mirroring `docs/rendertarget-support.md`'s Phase 39 closer) covering Tasks 341–349, plus this task's own non-desktop-platform section. Re-audited FNA's `GraphicsAdapter.cs`/`DisplayMode.cs`/`DisplayModeCollection.cs`/`Viewport.cs` and `FNAPlatform/SDL3_FNAPlatform.cs` specifically for Android/mobile platform branching: **found zero** `#if`/platform-conditional code anywhere in this area — FNA is a thin, unconditional wrapper over plain SDL3 display calls (the few Android mentions elsewhere in `SDL3_FNAPlatform.cs` concern window-creation quirks and save-path resolution, not adapter/display enumeration); CNA's `GraphicsAdapter.cpp` mirrors this with no platform guards of its own. **Checked actual non-desktop build/run reality rather than assuming**: Android — `CNA` cross-compiles for `arm64-v8a` via NDK (confirmed, `docs/devices-build.md` §4), but has **never actually executed** any display/adapter code — no APK packaging integration exists, and the one attempted emulator run hard-failed on missing KVM acceleration before any CNA code ran (`docs/devices-build.md` §4.1, Task P9-4); the `#ifdef __ANDROID__` code that does exist and is confirmed compiled-in is entirely in `Microsoft::Devices::Sensors`, none of it in `GraphicsAdapter`/`Viewport`/`DisplayMode`. Web/Emscripten — `CMakeLists.txt` has real Emscripten-aware build scaffolding (EasyGL/WebGL2 default, prebuilt-SDL3 hint path) but **no actual `emcc` build has ever been performed** (no `.sdl-prebuilt-emscripten` dir exists); a real Emscripten/WebGPU target is unstarted future work, already tracked as Phase 69 (Tasks 10155–10161, all open). Documented (from SDL3's own public docs, explicitly labeled as anticipated-not-verified, not fabricated as confirmed fact) the expected behavior differences: Android typically reports exactly one display with a short native-resolution-only mode list and no real fullscreen mode-switching; Web/Emscripten has no real display-mode enumeration at all (canvas-as-display, browser-sandboxed). Concluded CNA's existing headless-fallback synthetic-adapter path (Task 346) is **not** the path Android would actually exercise (Android is expected to report a real single display, taking the normal per-display loop, not the empty-`SDL_GetDisplays()` fallback branch) but that no code change is anticipated to be necessary either way, since `DisplayMode`/`DisplayModeCollection` make no assumptions about display/mode count. **No new bug found or opened this task** (pure documentation/synthesis, matching Task 340's precedent exactly) — docs-only, no `.hpp`/`.cpp`/test files touched, no rebuild/regression run needed ("no code changed, no regression risk", same as Task 340). |
