## Phase 41 — Effect base class and compiled effect compatibility

| #   | Task                                                                          | Status | Notes                                       |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | ------------------------------------------- |
| 351 | Audit `Effect` base class against FNA                                         | ✅      | **Opens Phase 41.** Read FNA's `Graphics/Effect/Effect.cs` line-by-line against CNA's `Effect.hpp`/`.cpp`. **2 real, fixed findings**: (1) `GetTypeName()` returned bare `"Effect"` instead of the fully-qualified `"Microsoft.Xna.Framework.Graphics.Effect"` every other `GraphicsResource` subclass uses (`RenderTarget2D`, `Texture3D`, `BasicEffect`, …) — a `CLAUDE.md` violation, fixed. (2) `Effect`'s public `void Apply()` — a CNA invention with no FNA equivalent (FNA only has protected/internal `OnApply()` plus a separate `EffectPass.Apply()`) — was not wrapped in `NOXNA`; fixed. **A third, more serious real bug found and fixed while writing tests**: `Effect` declares `void Dispose(bool disposing) override;` with no `using GraphicsResource::Dispose;` — this **hides** the public zero-arg `IDisposable::Dispose()` inherited from `GraphicsResource` (C++ name-hiding), meaning `effect.Dispose()` **failed to compile** on any concrete effect (`BasicEffect`, `ShaderEffect`, all 7 stock effects — none of them redeclare `Dispose` themselves) unless called through a base-class pointer/reference. `Texture`/`Texture2D`/`RenderTarget2D` already carry the correct `using X::Dispose;` fix for this exact pattern; `Effect` was missing it — confirmed via grep that no existing test or production code anywhere called `.Dispose()` directly on a graphics `Effect` object, so this was a real, live, previously-undiscovered gap, not a hypothetical. Fixed by adding `using GraphicsResource::Dispose;`. **Verified genuine discriminating power**: the `Dispose` fix is directly proven by the new test file failing to *compile* without it (confirmed live during this task); the `GetTypeName()` fix was proven by temporarily reverting to `"Effect"` and confirming `EffectTest.GetTypeNameIsFullyQualified` fails, then reverting back. **Confirmed correct, not touched**: `Parameters`/`Techniques` construction, `CurrentTechnique`'s zero-validation setter (FNA's own setter is equally permissive — accepts any `EffectTechnique*`, including ones not in `Techniques`, confirmed via FNA source, not a bug), `GraphicsDevice` resource-reference registration (via the shared `GraphicsResource` constructor path, already proven correct by dozens of other resource classes' audits). **Real findings deliberately deferred, not fixed here** (narrow task scope): (a) FNA's `Effect` has a public `Effect(GraphicsDevice, byte[] effectCode)` bytecode constructor and a protected `Effect(Effect cloneSource)` clone constructor + public virtual `Clone()`; **none exist in CNA** — root cause is architectural: CNA's `Effect::OnApply()` is pure virtual (CNA has no MojoShader/.fx-bytecode-parsing pipeline at the base-class level; every concrete effect builds its own parameters/techniques programmatically), so FNA's exact base-class pattern can't be ported as-is. The bytecode-constructor question is explicitly Task 352's job. The `Clone()` gap is deferred to new **Task 883** — a correct implementation needs (1) an ownership-transfer model decision in C++ (FNA returns a GC-managed `Effect`; CNA needs e.g. `std::unique_ptr<Effect>`), (2) fixing a real aliasing hazard discovered while investigating this: `EffectPass::Apply()` calls `owner_->Apply()` where `owner_` is bound to the *original* `Effect*` at construction time — a naive default-copy `Clone()` would leave the clone's passes silently mutating/reading the *original* effect's state instead of its own, since ownership must be re-bound to the new clone (`this`), not copied verbatim, and (3) `Clone()` overrides + copy constructors in all 7 concrete stock-effect subclasses (mirroring FNA's own per-subclass `Clone()` overrides), which is too large in scope for this base-class-only audit. (b) FNA's `EffectPass.Apply()` throws `InvalidOperationException` if the pass being applied isn't part of the effect's current `CurrentTechnique`; CNA's `EffectPass::Apply()` has no such validation — this is explicitly Task 355's own dedicated scope ("Verify `EffectPass::Apply` updates current effect state consistently"), left untouched here. New `tests/Microsoft/Xna/Framework/Graphics/EffectTests.cpp` (13 tests, previously zero direct base-class coverage existed): construction (single "Default" technique, `CurrentTechnique` defaults to it, empty `Parameters`), const/mutable accessor parity, permissive `CurrentTechnique` setter, `Apply()` → `OnApply()` dispatch and making the effect current (verified indirectly via `GraphicsDevice::DrawPrimitives`'s "no effect has been applied" guard), `Apply()` after `Dispose()` throwing `ObjectDisposedException`, `GetTypeName()`, and `Dispose()` setting `IsDisposed`. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass: EasyGL ctest 3365/3369 (3 pre-existing + 1 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest`, unchanged). Vulkan ctest 3289/3302 (13 pre-existing, unchanged, no flake this run). Bgfx ctest 3272/3272 (100%, no flakes this run). |
| 352 | Decide explicit support policy for XNA `.fx` / compiled effect bytecode       | ✅      | **Decision (made by the project owner, not inferred): full support.** Confirmed via FNA source (`Graphics/Effect/Effect.cs`) that FNA's bytecode constructor/clone-constructor are thin wrappers delegating everything — container parsing, parameter/technique/pass reflection, and D3D9 Shader Model 2/3 bytecode translation — to FNA3D/MojoShader; FNA3D's own local checkout (`/rv/data/library/github.com/FNA-XNA/FNA3D`) is an uninitialized submodule (empty), so FNA3D's exact Vulkan-side MojoShader integration could not be verified line-by-line — noted as a knowledge gap, not asserted as fact. **Real, load-bearing discovery**: MojoShader's full C source (zlib-licensed, compatible with this project's MS-PL) is already present locally and readable at `/rv/data/library/github.com/u3d-community/U3D/Source/ThirdParty/MojoShader` — `mojoshader_effects.c` already implements exactly the XNA compiled-effect container parser this project would otherwise have to write from scratch; `mojoshader.c`/`mojoshader.h` transpile D3D9 SM2/3 bytecode to `GLSL`/`GLSL120`/`ARB1`/`NV2`-`NV4` profiles — **no SPIR-V profile exists**, so a Vulkan path needs a second GLSL→SPIR-V hop (`glslang` is present locally only as Android-NDK/Flatpak build tooling, not a repo-vendorable checkout — a real, tracked blocker, not assumed solved). This is the same scale of work as the WebGPU backend (Phases 56–69: new vendored native dependency, per-backend GPU-shader-translation paths, Bgfx feasibility unknown, real test-fixture sourcing with no XNA Content Pipeline tooling available) — **opened new Phase 74** (Tasks 10200–10208, dedicated task-number block mirroring WebGPU's `10000`+ convention) rather than trying to fit it into Phase 41. Full research + reasoning + phased breakdown written up in new `docs/fx-bytecode-support-plan.md` (do not duplicate its content here — see that file for the authoritative walkthrough). **Re-scoped Tasks 353/354** (see their own rows) to fit "full support is the target, not yet built" instead of the original unsupported/throw framing they were written under. No production code touched (pure decision/planning task); no rebuild needed. |

| 355 | Verify `EffectPass::Apply` updates current effect state consistently          | ✅      | Read FNA's `Graphics/Effect/EffectPass.cs` line-by-line: `Apply()` throws `InvalidOperationException("Applied a pass not in the current technique!")` if `parentTechnique != parentEffect.CurrentTechnique.TechniquePointer` (an opaque native handle comparison), then calls `OnApply()` + `INTERNAL_applyEffect(pass)`. Confirmed Task 351's finding for real: CNA's `EffectPass::Apply()` had **zero** such validation — `EffectPass` didn't even track which technique it belonged to. **Fixed**: gave `EffectTechnique` a stable identity token (`getIdInternal()`, a monotonically-increasing `std::uint64_t` assigned via a private static counter, mirroring FNA's opaque `TechniquePointer` handle without depending on C++ object addresses) and a new `EffectPass` constructor parameter `techniqueId` (default `0` = untracked, preserving every existing 2-arg test call site's compilation). `EffectPass::Apply()` now checks `owner_->getCurrentTechniqueProperty()->getIdInternal() == techniqueId_` before delegating to `owner_->Apply()`, throwing `System::InvalidOperationException` on mismatch — including when `CurrentTechnique` is null (FNA dereferences it unconditionally and crashes with a `NullReferenceException`; CNA maps that to this same defined exception instead of UB, a deliberate, documented deviation). **A second, more serious real bug found and fixed while writing the interleaved-technique-switch test**: `EffectTechniqueCollection` stored `EffectTechnique` **by value** in a `std::vector`, so `Effect`'s own `currentTechnique_ = &techniques_[0]` (captured at construction) would dangle the moment a second technique was ever added (`vector`'s capacity-1→2 growth guarantees reallocation) — the exact same class of bug as Task 345's `DefaultAdapter` dangling reference. Confirmed via C++ vector-growth semantics (guaranteed reallocation, not empirically reproduced via revert-and-observe — a freed 1-element buffer's bytes often survive unclobbered long enough to make such a revert test misleadingly pass, so this was reasoned from the standard's guarantees instead). Fixed by switching `EffectTechniqueCollection`'s backing storage to `std::vector<std::unique_ptr<EffectTechnique>>` (this project's established idiom for this exact problem, per `GraphicsAdapter::adapters_`), with a small custom `iterator`/`const_iterator` pair so `begin()`/`end()` still dereference to `EffectTechnique&` for range-for support — `EffectPassCollection` has the identical latent by-value-vector hazard but is deliberately left as-is (no current or new test exercises taking an `EffectPass&` across an `Add()` call), documented as new **Task 884**. New tests in `EffectTests.cpp`'s `EffectApplyTest` fixture (reusing Task 351's `TestEffect` mock, per this task's own "Unit test with mock effect" hint): `ApplyOnPassOfCurrentTechniqueSucceedsAndInvokesOnApply`, `ApplyOnPassNotInCurrentTechniqueThrowsInvalidOperationException`, `ApplyWithNullCurrentTechniqueThrowsInvalidOperationException`, and `ApplyIsConsistentAcrossInterleavedTechniqueSwitches` (adds a real second technique via the `NOXNA Add()` method, then proves `Apply()` correctly flips which pass succeeds/fails as `CurrentTechnique` is switched back and forth, with no stale state lingering from the previously-current technique). **Verified genuine discriminating power**: temporarily short-circuited the validation (`if (false && ...)`) and confirmed 3 of the 4 new tests fail with the exact expected assertions (the "succeeds" test correctly kept passing), then reverted. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass (header change touches everything that includes `EffectTechnique.hpp`/`EffectPass.hpp`): EasyGL ctest 3370/3375 (3 pre-existing + 2 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest`, unchanged). Vulkan ctest 3294/3308 (13 pre-existing + 1 reconfirmed order-dependent `Vulkan_FillMode_WireFrame` flake, unchanged). Bgfx ctest 3278/3278 (100%, no flakes this run). |
| 356 | Verify `EffectTechnique` selection changes applied pass collection            | ✅      | Read FNA's `Effect.cs` `CurrentTechnique` setter and `EffectPass.cs` `Apply()` again with this task's specific lens: does switching `CurrentTechnique` have any hidden side effect beyond the plain pointer swap, or any interaction with previously-applied passes? Confirmed FNA's setter does exactly two things — `FNA3D_SetEffectTechnique(GraphicsDevice.GLDevice, glEffect, value.TechniquePointer)` (a native call telling the compiled-effect backend/MojoShader which technique's GPU state is now active — an implementation detail with no C#-observable side effect of its own) — and `INTERNAL_currentTechnique = value` (the only part visible to a C# game). All real enforcement lives in `EffectPass.Apply()`'s own live comparison (`parentTechnique != parentEffect.CurrentTechnique.TechniquePointer`), which Task 355 already ported faithfully (`EffectPass::Apply()` compares `techniqueId_` against `owner_->getCurrentTechniqueProperty()->getIdInternal()`, read **live** at `Apply()` time, not a snapshot). **Conclusion: no production code fix needed — CNA's plain pointer-swap `setCurrentTechniqueProperty()` already provides the complete C#-visible contract**; the native `FNA3D_SetEffectTechnique` call has no CNA equivalent yet because CNA has no compiled-technique GPU representation at all (tracked under Phase 74, the `.fx` bytecode work). This is a verify-only task, matching Task 344's precedent (confirming already-correct-by-construction behavior with new tests, not fixing a bug). **New test added**: `CurrentTechniquePropertyPassCollectionTracksSelectedTechnique` in `EffectTests.cpp`, deliberately using a different access pattern than Task 355's existing `ApplyIsConsistentAcrossInterleavedTechniqueSwitches` (which holds fixed `EffectPass&` references captured once) — this new test instead re-fetches `getCurrentTechniqueProperty()->getPassesProperty()[0]` after each switch, proving the **live selection itself** (not just a previously-held pass reference) correctly resolves to each technique's own pass collection, bidirectionally (technique 0 → 1 → back to 0), each direction independently applying successfully. **Verified genuine discriminating power**: temporarily made `setCurrentTechniqueProperty()` a no-op (not updating `currentTechnique_` at all); confirmed the new test fails (wrong pass-identity pointer at line 328) alongside Task 355's existing technique-switch tests (same underlying setter, same failure class), then reverted. | 357 | Verify effect parameter lookup by name and semantic                           | ✅      | Read FNA's `Graphics/Effect/EffectParameterCollection.cs`/`EffectParameter.cs` line-by-line. **Confirmed FNA semantics exactly**: `this[int index]` is plain positional access; `this[string name]` linearly scans `elements` and returns the first `name.Equals(elem.Name)` match (ordinal, case-sensitive) or `null` if none — **no by-semantic collection indexer exists in FNA**; `GetParameterBySemantic(string)` is a separate public *method* (not an indexer) doing the identical linear-scan-first-match-or-`null` pattern over `Semantic` instead of `Name`. CNA's `EffectParameterCollection::operator[](int)`/`operator[](const std::string&)`/`GetParameterBySemantic(const std::string&)` (both mutable/const overloads) already matched all of this exactly — **no code fix needed**, this was a real coverage gap, not a behavior bug: `tests/.../EffectCollectionTests.cpp` (from Task 185) already covered basic found/not-found cases for all 3 lookups, but **case-sensitivity and duplicate-name/-semantic tie-breaking were never tested** before this task. Added 4 new tests to `EffectCollectionTests.cpp`: `IndexByNameIsCaseSensitive`, `IndexByNameFirstMatchWinsOnDuplicateNames`, `GetParameterBySemanticIsCaseSensitive`, `GetParameterBySemanticFirstMatchWinsOnDuplicates`. **Verified genuine discriminating power**: temporarily replaced `operator[](const std::string&)`'s exact-match scan with a case-insensitive scan that kept the *last* match instead of the first, confirmed both the case-sensitivity and first-match-wins tests fail with the wrong-parameter/wrong-class assertion, then reverted to the original correct implementation. **Real latent hazard found and deliberately deferred (not fixed here, narrow task scope)**: `EffectParameterCollection` stores `EffectParameter` **by value** in a `std::vector` — the identical dangling-pointer hazard class Task 355 fixed for `EffectTechniqueCollection` and Task 351 tracked (unfixed) for `EffectPassCollection` as Task 884. Checked specifically because parameter-lookup pointer safety is this task's own scope: confirmed **currently unexercised** — all 4 stock effects that populate `Parameters` (`AlphaTestEffect`/`DualTextureEffect`/`EnvironmentMapEffect`/`SkinnedEffect`) call every `AddParam(...)` first and only take `params["Name"]` pointers *afterward*, in `CacheEffectParameters()`, so no live dangling-pointer bug exists today. Widened **Task 884** to cover `EffectParameterCollection` alongside `EffectPassCollection` (same fix shape: `vector<unique_ptr<EffectParameter>>`), rather than opening a new task, since it's the identical hazard and identical fix. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass (test-file-only change): EasyGL ctest 3377/3380 (3 pre-existing, unchanged, no flaky `CueTest` this run). Vulkan ctest 3300/3313 (13 pre-existing, unchanged, no flake this run). Bgfx ctest 3283/3283 (100%, no flakes this run). |
| 358 | Verify effect parameter collection enumeration order                          | ✅      | Read FNA's `EffectParameterCollection.cs`/`EffectTechniqueCollection.cs`/`EffectPassCollection.cs` line-by-line: all 3 are identical thin wrappers over a plain `List<T>` populated once at construction; `GetEnumerator()` just returns the list's own enumerator verbatim — no sort, no filter, no reorder anywhere. Enumeration order is therefore always exactly declaration/insertion order. Compared against CNA's `EffectParameterCollection` (`elements_` is a `std::vector`, `Add()` does `push_back`, `begin()`/`end()` are plain pass-throughs to the vector's own iterators) — **already matches FNA exactly, no code fix needed** (verify-only task, same shape as Task 356). `EffectTechniqueCollection` (Task 355, now `vector<unique_ptr<EffectTechnique>>`) and `EffectPassCollection` preserve insertion order the same way; existing `IterationVisitsAllTechniques`/`IterationVisitsAllPasses` tests already cover their sibling behavior at the same shallow "visits all elements" level this task's pre-existing `IterationVisitsAllParameters` test had. **Real, confirmed gap**: no existing test actually asserted iteration *order* specifically (only element *count*) — `IndexByIntReturnsParameter` incidentally exercises order via positional indexing, but nothing exercised `begin()`/`end()` range-for order. Added `EffectParameterCollectionTest.IterationOrderMatchesInsertionOrder` using deliberately non-alphabetical names (`Zebra`, `Apple`, `Mango`) so an accidental sort couldn't hide behind coincidental alphabetical insertion order. **Verified genuine discriminating power**: temporarily changed `Add()` to `elements_.insert(elements_.begin(), ...)` (prepend instead of append) and confirmed the new test failed (along with 3 pre-existing order-sensitive tests — `IndexByIntReturnsParameter`, `IndexByNameFirstMatchWinsOnDuplicateNames`, `GetParameterBySemanticFirstMatchWinsOnDuplicates` — confirming those were genuinely order-sensitive too, not accidentally passing), then reverted to the original `push_back`. Net change is test-file-only (`EffectCollectionTests.cpp`), zero production code diff. | 359 | Add tests for missing parameter lookups                                       | ✅      | Read FNA's `EffectParameterCollection.cs` line-by-line to confirm the exact not-found behavior for all 3 lookup surfaces (Task 357 had already confirmed by-name/by-semantic; this task pins down by-index too). Confirmed: `this[int index]` returns `elements[index]` on a plain `List<EffectParameter>` — a negative or `>=Count` index throws (standard `List<T>` indexer behavior), unlike `this[string name]`/`GetParameterBySemantic`, which do a linear scan and **return `null`** on no match (already verified in Task 357). CNA's `EffectParameterCollection::operator[](int)` already uses `elements_.at(index)`, which throws `std::out_of_range` for the same negative/`>=size` cases — **already matched FNA exactly, no code fix needed**, this was a pure test-coverage task. Added 6 new tests to `EffectCollectionTests.cpp`: `IndexByIntNegativeThrowsOutOfRange`, `IndexByIntEqualToCountThrowsOutOfRange`, `ConstIndexByIntOutOfRangeThrowsOutOfRange` (the mutable/const `operator[](int)` overloads' out-of-range paths, previously untested — only the string/semantic not-found paths had explicit tests before this task), plus the previously-untested **empty-collection** edge case for all 3 lookup surfaces: `IndexByIntOnEmptyCollectionThrowsOutOfRange`, `IndexByNameOnEmptyCollectionReturnsNull`, `GetParameterBySemanticOnEmptyCollectionReturnsNull`. **Verified genuine discriminating power** for the out-of-range throw tests: temporarily replaced `operator[](int)`'s `.at(index)` with an index-clamping `elements_[...]` (silently returning element 0 instead of throwing, guarded only to avoid an actual UB crash on the empty-collection case) and confirmed exactly the 3 non-empty out-of-range tests failed with "it throws nothing" (the empty-collection case was deliberately left throwing in the broken version to keep the test binary from segfaulting, so it correctly stayed green — not a gap, a deliberate test-harness safety choice), then reverted to the correct `.at()`-based implementation. The 3 empty-collection null-return tests reuse the exact same code path Task 357 already proved discriminates correctly for non-empty missing-name/-semantic lookups (an empty `for` loop trivially falls through to `return nullptr`) — not re-broken separately, noted here instead of duplicating Task 357's proof. Vulkan ctest 3306/3320 (13 pre-existing + 1 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest`, unchanged). | 360 | Add effect lifecycle tests: dispose, clone after dispose, apply after dispose | ✅      | **Closes Phase 41** (Tasks 351-360, mirroring how Task 340 closed Phase 39 and Task 350 closed Phase 40). Read FNA's `Effect.Dispose(bool)` (`Graphics/Effect/Effect.cs`) and `GraphicsResource.Dispose(bool)`: both are guarded by `if (!IsDisposed)`, so a second `Dispose()` call is always a safe no-op in FNA — CNA's `GraphicsResource::Dispose(bool)` already carries the identical `if (isDisposed_) return;` guard (confirmed by reading `GraphicsResource.cpp`), so double-dispose was already safe by construction; **no code fix needed**. **A genuinely interesting confirmed finding, not a bug**: FNA's own `Effect`/`EffectPass` have **zero** disposed-state check anywhere (`EffectPass.Apply()` unconditionally calls `parentEffect.OnApply()` then `INTERNAL_applyEffect`, which would hand a zeroed-out native `glEffect` handle straight to `FNA3D_ApplyEffect` after disposal — undefined behavior at the native layer in real FNA/FNA3D, confirmed by reading `EffectPass.cs`/`Effect.cs` directly, not inferred). CNA's `Effect::Apply()` (a CNA-only invention per Task 351, `NOXNA`-tagged) throwing `System::ObjectDisposedException` when `isDisposed_` is therefore a **deliberate, confirmed, beneficial CNA-specific safety improvement over FNA's silent UB** — same category of intentional, documented divergence as `GraphicsAdapter::DeviceId`/`VendorId` (Task 345), correctly NOT reverted to match FNA's unsafe behavior. **Real test-coverage gap closed**: Task 351 already tested `Effect::Apply()`-after-`Dispose()` directly, but nothing tested the *other* real entry point, `EffectPass::Apply()` (which delegates to `owner_->Apply()`) after the owning effect is disposed — added `ApplyOnPassThrowsObjectDisposedExceptionWhenOwnerEffectDisposed`, confirming the guard is enforced through that path too, not just the direct one. Also added `DisposeIsIdempotentAndDoesNotThrow` (calls `Dispose()` twice, asserts no throw and `IsDisposed` stays `true`) for explicit, direct coverage of the double-dispose contract (previously only implied by `DisposeSetsIsDisposed`, never asserted twice-called). **Verified genuine discriminating power** for the pass-level test: temporarily removed the `isDisposed_` check from `Effect::Apply()`, rebuilt, and confirmed both `ApplyAfterDisposeThrowsObjectDisposedException` (Task 351's) and the new `ApplyOnPassThrowsObjectDisposedExceptionWhenOwnerEffectDisposed` fail with "it throws nothing", then reverted. **Honestly documented, not fabricated**: `DisposeIsIdempotentAndDoesNotThrow` has no meaningful way to "break" via a temporary code change — `GraphicsDevice::RemoveResourceReference` is already a safe linear-scan no-op when the resource isn't found (confirmed by reading `GraphicsDevice.cpp`), so even removing the `isDisposed_` guard doesn't produce an observable failure for this specific test; the test still has real value as an explicit lock-in of the double-dispose contract, just not one demonstrated via a broken-then-reverted diff. **`Clone()` after dispose is N/A today**: `Effect::Clone()` does not exist in CNA (deferred to **Task 883** per Task 351's finding — needs a C++ ownership-model decision plus fixing the `EffectPass::Apply()` `owner_`-rebinding aliasing hazard, plus per-subclass overrides); when Task 883 lands, a "clone after dispose" test should be added there alongside `Clone()` itself, not retrofitted here. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass (2 new tests): EasyGL ctest 3386/3389 (3 pre-existing: `EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `easy-gl-resource-smoke-tests`, unchanged). Vulkan ctest 3309/3322 (13 pre-existing, unchanged). Bgfx ctest 3291/3292 (1 reconfirmed-flaky unrelated `CueTest`, unchanged). |

## Phase 42 — BasicEffect exactness

| #   | Task                                                                     | Status | Notes                                      |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------ | ------------------------------------------ |
| 361 | Audit `BasicEffect` properties and defaults against FNA `BasicEffect.cs` | ✅      | **Opens Phase 42.** Read FNA's `Graphics/Effect/StockEffects/BasicEffect.cs` line-by-line against CNA's `BasicEffect.hpp`/`.cpp`. Full property/default comparison table (22 members audited): `World`/`View`/`Projection` = Identity (match), `DiffuseColor` = (1,1,1) (match), `EmissiveColor` = (0,0,0) (match), `AmbientLightColor` = (0,0,0) (match), `SpecularColor` = (1,1,1) via constructor (match), `SpecularPower` = 16 via constructor (match), `Alpha` = 1 (match), `LightingEnabled`/`PreferPerPixelLighting`/`FogEnabled`/`TextureEnabled` = false (match), `FogColor` = (0,0,0) (match), `FogStart` = 0, `FogEnd` = 1 (match), `Texture` = null (match), `Techniques`/`CurrentTechnique` = single default technique (match, per Task 351). **2 real, fixed default-value bugs found**: (1) CNA's `VertexColorEnabled` field defaulted to `true`; FNA's `vertexColorEnabled` field has no initializer, i.e. defaults to C#'s `false`, and FNA's constructor never sets it — fixed to `false`. (2) FNA's `BasicEffect(GraphicsDevice)` constructor unconditionally sets `DirectionalLight0.Enabled = true` (confirmed from source: `DirectionalLight0.Enabled = true;` right after `CacheEffectParameters(null)`), while `DirectionalLight1`/`DirectionalLight2` and `DirectionalLight0`'s own `Direction`/`DiffuseColor`/`SpecularColor` stay at `DirectionalLight`'s own class defaults (all `Vector3.Zero`, confirmed from FNA's `DirectionalLight.cs`) — CNA's constructor set none of this, leaving `DirectionalLight0.Enabled` at its class default `false`; fixed by adding `DirectionalLight0.setEnabledProperty(true);` to `BasicEffect`'s constructor. Net practical effect: a real XNA game that sets `DirectionalLight0.DiffuseColor`/`Direction` without also explicitly setting `Enabled = true` (a legal, common pattern in real XNA code, since XNA's own default already has it enabled) would previously render with that light silently contributing nothing on CNA, unlike real XNA/FNA. **Found 2 existing tests that enshrined the wrong defaults** (from Task 22's original `basic_effect_test.cpp`): `check(fx.VertexColorEnabled == true, "VertexColorEnabled default true")` and `check(!light0.getEnabledProperty(), "DirectionalLight0 default disabled")` — both corrected to assert the real FNA-matching defaults, plus added toggle-back-and-forth assertions and new default-disabled checks for `DirectionalLight1`/`DirectionalLight2` (previously unchecked). Verified genuine discriminating power: temporarily reverted both production fixes and confirmed the corrected test assertions fail, then reverted back to the fixes. **Confirmed correct, not touched**: `EnableDefaultLighting()`'s exact key/fill/back-light and ambient constants (cross-checked against FNA's `EffectHelpers.EnableDefaultLighting`, byte-for-byte identical float constants — real audit work for Task 363, not just a rubber stamp). **Constructor overloads**: FNA has a public `BasicEffect(GraphicsDevice)` and a protected `BasicEffect(BasicEffect cloneSource)` feeding `Effect.Clone()`; CNA has only the former — the clone gap is the same, already-tracked **Task 883** (Task 351/355), not duplicated here; noted that `BasicEffect`'s own `Clone()` override will be one of the per-subclass overrides Task 883 needs. **Real, deliberately deferred architectural finding, not fixed here**: FNA's `BasicEffect.OnApply()` does real work — lazy `EffectDirtyFlags`-gated recomputation of world-view-projection, world-inverse-transpose, fog vector, material color, and a `shaderIndex` upload, all funneled through real `EffectParameter` objects. CNA's `BasicEffect::OnApply()` is an empty stub; all of this instead happens in a separate, ungated `FillGpuDrawParams()` that recomputes everything from scratch on every draw call (no dirty-flag caching) and talks directly to the backend rather than through `EffectParameter` storage — a large, pre-existing architectural divergence (consistent with Task 351's finding that CNA's stock effects don't use the `EffectParameter` reflection pipeline at all). Also confirmed `FillGpuDrawParams()` only forwards `DirectionalLight0` and never reads `SpecularColor`/`SpecularPower`/`DirectionalLight1`/`DirectionalLight2` at all — a genuine pixel-fidelity gap, but squarely in scope for Tasks 368 (one directional light) and 369 (ambient+emissive+specular combination), deliberately left for those tasks rather than fixed here. `GetTypeName()` already correct (`"Microsoft.Xna.Framework.Graphics.BasicEffect"`). Doxygen coverage on every public member already complete, no violations found. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass (production fix touches `BasicEffect.hpp`/`.cpp`, used everywhere): EasyGL ctest 3386/3389 (3 pre-existing, unchanged). Vulkan ctest 3309/3322 (13 pre-existing, unchanged). Bgfx ctest 3292/3292 (100%, no flakes this run). |

Fixed by changing the initializer to `Vector3::Zero` in `DirectionalLight.cpp`, and corrected the constructor's Doxygen comment (`DirectionalLight.hpp`) to state "direction zero" explicitly. Grepped the whole codebase for any code relying on the old nonzero default direction (none found — every real usage, `examples/easygl_basiceffect_default_lighting_test.cpp` and `examples/basic_effect_test.cpp`, always sets `Direction` explicitly via `EnableDefaultLighting()` or a manual assignment before reading it back) and confirmed lighting math is unaffected by construction since `LightingEnabled` defaults to `false` regardless. | 364 | Pixel test: `VertexColorEnabled=false`, no texture, diffuse color only   | ✅      | **First real GPU pixel-readback task in Phase 42** (Tasks 361-363 were all GPU-independent). Derived the expected output formula directly from FNA source, not guessed: read `Graphics/Effect/StockEffects/BasicEffect.cs`'s `OnApply()` `shaderIndex` computation (with `FogEnabled=false` default, `shaderIndex=1` → `VSBasicNoFog`/`PSBasicNoFog`) and `HLSL/BasicEffect.fx` + `Common.fxh`: `ComputeCommonVSOutput()` sets `vout.Diffuse = DiffuseColor` unconditionally, and critically `VSBasicNoFog` never executes `vout.Diffuse *= vin.Color` (that only happens in the `*Vc` shader variants selected when `VertexColorEnabled=true`); `PSBasicNoFog` returns `pin.Diffuse` unmodified. Cross-checked `EffectHelpers.SetMaterialColor()`: with `LightingEnabled=false`, `diffuse.rgb=(DiffuseColor+EmissiveColor)*Alpha`, `diffuse.a=Alpha`. Net formula (EmissiveColor at its default (0,0,0)): **expected fragment = DiffuseColor × Alpha, independent of any vertex-color attribute in the buffer.** **3 real bugs found and fixed, one per backend** — this task's actual GPU pixel-readback proved `FillGpuDrawParams()`'s `vertexColorEnabled` forwarding (confirmed wired in Task 361) was **not actually honored by any of the 3 backends' shaders for the no-texture path**: (1) **EasyGL**: `prog_colored_`'s fragment shader (`EnsureColored3DProgram()`) unconditionally computed `FragColor=vColor*uDiffuseColor` — no gate on the flag at all. Added a `uVertexColorEnabled` uniform (`loc_vertexcolor` on `Prog3D`), gated the multiply (`vc=(uVertexColorEnabled>0.5)?vColor:vec4(1)`), wired from `BindDrawParams()`; preserved the legacy no-`GpuDrawParams` `DrawColoredPrimitives`/`DrawIndexedColoredPrimitives` paths by explicitly forcing the new uniform to `1.0` there (GLSL uniforms default to `0` when never set, which would otherwise silently break those call sites). (2) **Vulkan**: far more severe — `stride==16` (`VertexPositionColor`) draws bypass the general `Ext3D` pipeline entirely (a pre-existing `useExtParams = ... && stride != 16` exclusion) and use a separate, minimal `colored3d`/`GetOrCreatePipeline3D` pipeline whose vertex shader was a bare `fragColor = inColor;` passthrough — **it read neither `DiffuseColor` nor `VertexColorEnabled` at all**, for any combination, always just echoing the raw per-vertex color. Fixed by extending `colored3d.vert.glsl`'s push-constant struct to mirror `FillExtPushConst()`'s existing 32-float/128-byte layout byte-for-byte (so `DrawPrimitivesEx`'s existing fill already populates it correctly with zero new fill code) and gating `fragColor=(vertexColorEnabled>0.5)?inColor*diffuseColor:diffuseColor`; bumped `pipelineLayout3D_`'s push-constant range and the `vkCmdPushConstants` call from 64→128 bytes. Preserved the legacy no-`GpuDrawParams` `DrawColoredPrimitives`/`DrawIndexedColoredPrimitives` paths by explicitly setting `pushConst[16..19]=1` (white) and `pushConst[31]=1` (vertexColorEnabled=true) there, matching the EasyGL fix's shape. **Found and fixed 2 latent stale-test regressions this exposed**: `vulkan_rt2d_test.cpp` and `vulkan_render_target_usage_test.cpp` both draw `VertexPositionColor` quads via `BasicEffect` expecting the raw per-vertex color, relying on the (now-fixed) bug to work, without ever setting `VertexColorEnabled=true` themselves — their own comments were stale from before Task 361 flipped the property's default to `false`; both previously "passed" only because Vulkan's bug ignored the flag entirely. Fixed by adding the missing explicit `fx.VertexColorEnabled = true;` to both (audited every other Vulkan/Bgfx example for the same `VertexPositionColor`+`BasicEffect`-without-the-flag pattern; only these 2 actually assert on pixel color, all others either don't touch pixel color or use a different, unaffected pipeline/path — e.g. `vulkan_instanced_test.cpp` goes through the separate, always-correct `Instanced3D` pipeline). (3) **Bgfx**: `vs_colored3d.sc`/`fs_colored3d.sc` had the identical EasyGL-shaped bug (`gl_FragColor=v_color0;`, no diffuse/flag at all) — added `u_diffuseColor`/`u_vertexColorEnabled3D` uniforms, gated the multiply in the vertex shader (matching the Vulkan/EasyGL fix shape), and likewise forced both to "opaque passthrough" values in the legacy `DrawColoredPrimitives`/`DrawIndexedColoredPrimitives` paths. **A 4th, separate, much larger pre-existing bug discovered while building this task's Bgfx test** (this is genuinely new coverage — no prior Bgfx test ever called `GetBackBufferData()` for any `BasicEffect` draw, confirmed by grepping every existing `examples/bgfx_*.cpp`): a scratch probe proved that **any** `BasicEffect` `DrawUserPrimitives` draw (textured or not, unrelated to this task's own fix) silently produced zero fragments on Bgfx — `GetBackBufferData` read back only the clear color — until `RasterizerState::CullNone` was set explicitly. Root cause: `GraphicsDevice`'s C++-level `rasterizerState_` default is correctly `CullCounterClockwiseFace` (matches FNA), but this default is never pushed to any backend's actual GPU state at device construction; each backend instead starts from its own hardcoded internal field until `setRasterizerStateProperty()` is explicitly called. EasyGL's `ApplyRasterizerState()` only runs on an explicit call (native GL default = no culling in the meantime); Vulkan's `cullMode_` field is hardcoded to `0`/None; **Bgfx's `cullFlags_` field is the only one of the three actually hardcoded to `BGFX_STATE_CULL_CCW`, matching FNA's real default** — so Bgfx is the only backend that silently culled every full-screen NDC quad used throughout this entire pixel-test family (`tl,bl,br`/`tl,br,tr` winding), while EasyGL/Vulkan's own (FNA-incorrect, but mutually-consistent) "effectively CullNone" defaults let the identical quads through. **Not fixed here** (a bigger, separate 3-way default-sync architectural gap, same shape as Task 880's Viewport-GPU-wiring gap) — tracked as new **Task 884**; worked around in this task's own new Bgfx test only, by explicitly setting `RasterizerState::CullNone` (documented in the test file's header comment with full derivation). New tests, one per backend (`examples/easygl_basiceffect_vertexcolor_disabled_test.cpp`, `vulkan_basiceffect_vertexcolor_disabled_test.cpp`, `bgfx_basiceffect_vertexcolor_disabled_test.cpp`): full-screen `VertexPositionColor` quad, bright-red per-vertex color baked into every vertex, `BasicEffect.DiffuseColor=(0.2,0.6,0.9)` with `VertexColorEnabled`/`TextureEnabled` left at their real defaults (both `false`); asserts the read-back center pixel matches `DiffuseColor*255≈(51,153,230)` AND is *not* red. **Verified genuine discriminating power on all 3 backends independently**: temporarily reverted each backend's shader gate back to an unconditional vertex-color multiply, rebuilt, confirmed the "pixel == DiffuseColor" assertion failed with exactly the red-tinted result (`got=(51,0,0)` — `DiffuseColor×vertex-red`, proving the vertex color really was leaking through pre-fix), then reverted all 3 back to the fix. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass (production shader/backend fixes touch all 3 backends' most heavily-used draw path, plus 2 stale-test fixes): EasyGL ctest 3414/3417 (3 pre-existing: `EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `easy-gl-resource-smoke-tests`, unchanged). Vulkan ctest 3336/3350 (13 pre-existing: 5× `Vulkan_BlendState_*`, 5× `Vulkan_DepthStencilState_*`, `Vulkan_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `Vulkan_RenderTargetCube_SampleAfterUnbind`, `Vulkan_DepthBias` one sub-case + 1 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest`, unchanged shape from baseline; confirmed `vulkan_rt2d_test`/`vulkan_render_target_usage_test` now pass cleanly post-fix). Bgfx ctest 3320/3320 (100%, no flakes this run — first-ever clean full pass exercising a real `BasicEffect` pixel-readback test on this backend). |
| 365 | Pixel test: `VertexColorEnabled=true`, no texture                        | ✅      | **The inverse/complement of Task 364.** Confirmed the exact expected-output formula from FNA source, not assumed: `Graphics/Effect/StockEffects/HLSL/Common.fxh`'s `ComputeCommonVSOutput()` sets `vout.Diffuse = DiffuseColor` (already `(DiffuseColor+EmissiveColor)*Alpha` via `EffectHelpers.SetMaterialColor` — `EmissiveColor` at its default `(0,0,0)` here); `BasicEffect.fx`'s `VSBasicVcNoFog` (selected via `shaderIndex=1+2=3` with `LightingEnabled=false`/`TextureEnabled=false`/`VertexColorEnabled=true`) additionally executes `vout.Diffuse *= vin.Color` — a component-wise multiply including alpha; `PSBasicNoFog` passes `pin.Diffuse` through unmodified. **Net formula: expected fragment = DiffuseColor × Alpha × VertexColor, component-wise.** Read Task 364's actual diff (`54aee7a2`) and confirmed exactly how its `VertexColorEnabled` gate was implemented on each backend: **EasyGL**'s `prog_colored_` fragment shader computes `vec4 vc=(uVertexColorEnabled>0.5)?vColor:vec4(1);FragColor=vc*uDiffuseColor;` — the `true` branch (`vc=vColor`) is the exact FNA multiply and was the shader's *original*, pre-Task-364 behavior (only the `false`/`vec4(1)` branch was newly added by Task 364). **Vulkan**'s `colored3d.vert.glsl` computes `fragColor=(pc.vertexColorEnabled>0.5)?inColor*pc.diffuseColor:pc.diffuseColor;` — same shape, same conclusion (the `true` branch is the pre-existing-in-spirit multiply Task 364 wired correctly alongside its `false`-branch fix, since before Task 364 this whole pipeline was a bare vertex-color passthrough that never multiplied by `DiffuseColor` at all — so this task is also implicitly the first real proof that Task 364's Vulkan fix didn't just fix the `false` case while accidentally leaving the `true` case as a no-multiply passthrough). **Bgfx**'s `vs_colored3d.sc` computes `vec4 vc=(u_vertexColorEnabled3D.x>0.5)?a_color0:vec4(1);v_color0=vc*u_diffuseColor;` — identical shape to EasyGL. **Conclusion confirmed by actual GPU pixel readback, not just code reading: all 3 backends already correctly implement the `true` branch as the genuine FNA multiply — this is a pure new-coverage verification task, no bug found**, distinct from Task 364 which found and fixed 3 real bugs in the sibling `false` branch. Added one new pixel-readback test per backend (`examples/easygl_basiceffect_vertexcolor_enabled_test.cpp`, `vulkan_basiceffect_vertexcolor_enabled_test.cpp`, `bgfx_basiceffect_vertexcolor_enabled_test.cpp`, registered in `CMakeLists.txt` following Task 364's exact target-naming/registration pattern): full-screen NDC quad of `VertexPositionColor` vertices carrying a deliberately non-white, non-trivial, semi-transparent-alpha per-vertex color `(200,100,50,200)`, `BasicEffect.VertexColorEnabled` explicitly set `true` (its real FNA default is `false`, per Task 361), `DiffuseColor=(0.8,0.4,0.6)` — chosen so the correctly-multiplied result `(160,40,30)` (exact: `200/255×0.8×255=160`, `100/255×0.4×255=40`, `50/255×0.6×255=30`) is numerically distinct from **both** plausible failure-mode readings: `DiffuseColor` alone would read `(204,102,153)`, `VertexColor` alone would read `(200,100,50)` — the test asserts the readback matches the true product and matches neither single-input fallback, giving strictly stronger discriminating power than Task 364's own test (which only had one failure mode to rule out). Bgfx test includes the same `RasterizerState::CullNone` workaround as Task 364's Bgfx test (Task 884, cull-default gap, unrelated to this task, not re-litigated). **Verified genuine discriminating power on all 3 backends independently**: temporarily forced each backend's shader to output `DiffuseColor` alone regardless of the flag (EasyGL: hardcoded `vc=vec4(1)`; Vulkan: `fragColor=pc.diffuseColor` in `colored3d.vert.glsl`, regenerated into `spirv_shaders.hpp` via `python3 compile_shaders.py`; Bgfx: `v_color0=u_diffuseColor` in `vs_colored3d.sc`, regenerated into `bgfx_shaders.hpp` via its own `compile_shaders.py`), rebuilt each, and confirmed the exact predicted diffuse-only failure `(204,102,153)` on all 3 — not merely "test fails", but fails with the precise value the broken hypothesis predicts — then reverted all 3 back to their committed Task 364 state (confirmed via clean `git status` after each shader-bytecode regeneration). Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass (2 new test executables + `CMakeLists.txt` registration per backend; no production `.hpp`/`.cpp` changed, since no bug was found): EasyGL ctest 3415/3418 (3 pre-existing: `EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `easy-gl-resource-smoke-tests`, unchanged). Vulkan ctest 3337/3351 (13 pre-existing: 5× `Vulkan_BlendState_*`, 5× `Vulkan_DepthStencilState_*`, `Vulkan_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `Vulkan_RenderTargetCube_SampleAfterUnbind`, `Vulkan_DepthBias` one sub-case + 1 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest`, unchanged shape from Task 364's baseline). Bgfx ctest 3320/3321 (1 reconfirmed-flaky, unrelated `NetworkSessionTest.FindReturnsEmptyCollection` — confirmed it passes cleanly on an isolated re-run immediately after, ruling out a genuine regression from this task's changes). |
| 366 | Pixel test: `TextureEnabled=true`, no vertex color                       | ✅      | Derived the expected formula directly from FNA source: `Graphics/Effect/StockEffects/BasicEffect.cs`'s `OnApply()` `shaderIndex` computation (with `VertexColorEnabled=false`, `FogEnabled=false` defaults) selects `shaderIndex=1+4=5` → `VSBasicTxNoFog`/`PSBasicTxNoFog`. `Common.fxh`'s `ComputeCommonVSOutput()` sets `vout.Diffuse = DiffuseColor` (no vertex-color multiply — that only happens in the separate `*Vc`/`*TxVc` shader family); `PSBasicTxNoFog` returns `SAMPLE_TEXTURE(Texture, pin.TexCoord) * pin.Diffuse` verbatim. Net formula (`EmissiveColor` at its default `(0,0,0)`): **expected fragment = TextureColor × DiffuseColor × Alpha, component-wise.** **Verify-only task, same shape as Tasks 356/358/365** — read all 3 backends' actual textured-3D shader paths (EasyGL's `EnsureTextured3DProgram()`: `FragColor=texture(uTexture,vUV)*uDiffuseColor`; Vulkan's `textured3d.vert/frag.glsl` stride-20 `Ext3D` pipeline: `fragTint=pc.diffuseColor` then `outColor=tex*fragTint`; Bgfx's `vs_textured3d.sc`/`fs_textured3d.sc`: `v_color0=u_diffuseColor` then `gl_FragColor=texture2D(...)*v_color0`) and confirmed **all 3 already correctly multiply texture × diffuse** — unlike Task 364's `VertexColorEnabled` finding, this path was never broken (texture sampling is a much more heavily-exercised path elsewhere, e.g. `SpriteBatch`/`Texture2D` tests, than the specific `VertexColorEnabled` flag Task 364 found broken). **No code fix needed.** Pre-existing-coverage check: Task 189's `easygl_basiceffect_combinations_test.cpp` already had a "texture only" case (white diffuse) and a "diffuse tint" case (white texture), but neither combines a *non-white* texture with a *non-white* diffuse, so neither could actually distinguish "texture ignored" from "diffuse ignored" from "both correctly multiplied" — ambiguous/degenerate coverage. Closed that gap with new discriminating tests, one per backend (`examples/easygl_basiceffect_texture_enabled_test.cpp`, `examples/vulkan_basiceffect_texture_enabled_test.cpp`, `examples/bgfx_basiceffect_texture_enabled_test.cpp`, registered in `CMakeLists.txt` as `EasyGL_BasicEffect_TextureEnabled`/`Vulkan_BasicEffect_TextureEnabled`/`Bgfx_BasicEffect_TextureEnabled`): reuses Task 365's exact (200,100,50) texture-color / (0.8,0.4,0.6) DiffuseColor pair (so the correct product (160,40,30) is the same independently-re-derived numeric target), asserting the result is neither texture-alone (200,100,50) nor diffuse-alone (204,102,153). Bgfx test needs the same `RasterizerState::CullNone` workaround as Tasks 364/365 (Task 884, untouched here). **Verified genuine discriminating power on all 3 backends independently**: temporarily stripped the diffuse multiply from EasyGL's fragment shader, Vulkan's `textured3d.frag.glsl` (recompiled via `compile_shaders.py`+`libshaderc`), and Bgfx's `fs_textured3d.sc` (recompiled via `compile_shaders.py`+`shaderc`) in turn; confirmed each backend's new test failed with exactly the texture-only result `(200,100,50)` instead of `(160,40,30)`, then reverted all 3 (confirmed byte-identical via `git diff`). **Deferred finding, not fixed here** (out of this texture-only task's narrow scope, tracked as part of Task 369 "ambient + emissive + specular combination"): `EffectHelpers.SetMaterialColor`'s lighting-disabled branch computes `(DiffuseColor + EmissiveColor) * Alpha`, but CNA's `BasicEffect::FillGpuDrawParams()` computes only `DiffuseColor * Alpha`, omitting `+ EmissiveColor` — invisible in this test (and in Tasks 364/365) since all leave `EmissiveColor` at its default `(0,0,0)`, but a real, confirmed mismatch that Task 369's dedicated emissive-combination test should catch and fix. Bgfx ctest 3321/3322 (1 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest`, unchanged). |
| 367 | Pixel test: `TextureEnabled=true` and `VertexColorEnabled=true`          | ✅      | **Real bug found and fixed on 2 of 3 backends.** Derived the expected formula directly from FNA source: with `LightingEnabled=false`, `VertexColorEnabled=true`, `TextureEnabled=true` (both explicitly set, `FogEnabled` default), `BasicEffect.cs`'s `OnApply()` selects `shaderIndex=1+2+4=7` → `VSBasicTxVcNoFog`/`PSBasicTxNoFog`. `Common.fxh`'s `ComputeCommonVSOutput()` sets `vout.Diffuse=DiffuseColor`; `VSBasicTxVcNoFog` additionally executes `vout.Diffuse *= vin.Color`; `PSBasicTxNoFog` returns `SAMPLE_TEXTURE(Texture,pin.TexCoord) * pin.Diffuse`. **Net formula: expected fragment = TextureColor × VertexColor × DiffuseColor × Alpha, component-wise** — a genuine 3-way product, the stride-24 `VertexPositionColorTexture` path, distinct from Task 365 (vertex×diffuse only) and Task 366 (texture×diffuse only). Read all 3 backends' actual stride-24 shader source before writing any test: **EasyGL**'s `EnsureColoredTextured3DProgram()` fragment shader was `FragColor=texture(uTexture,vUV)*vColor;` — **`uDiffuseColor` was never declared or multiplied at all**, and there was no `VertexColorEnabled` gate either (unlike the sibling no-texture `EnsureColored3DProgram()`, Task 364's fix target). **Bgfx**'s `vs_colored_textured3d.sc` was `v_color0=a_color0;` (fragment just `texture*v_color0`) — the identical bug: no `u_diffuseColor` uniform, no `u_vertexColorEnabled3D` gate, despite both uniforms already existing and being wired for the sibling `vs_colored3d.sc` (Task 364). **Vulkan**'s `colored_textured3d.vert.glsl` already computed `fragTint=(vertexColorEnabled>0.5)?inColor*diffuseColor:diffuseColor` correctly — no bug, confirmed by pixel readback not just code reading. **Fixed EasyGL**: added `uniform vec4 uDiffuseColor;`/`uniform float uVertexColorEnabled;` to the fragment shader, `vc=(uVertexColorEnabled>0.5)?vColor:vec4(1)`, `FragColor=texture(...)*vc*uDiffuseColor` — exact mirror of `EnsureColored3DProgram()`'s Task-364 pattern; added the matching `loc_diffuse`/`loc_vertexcolor` uniform lookups (`BindDrawParams()`'s generic `if(loc>=0)` setter needed no changes). **Fixed Bgfx**: moved the diffuse-multiply and vertex-color gate into `vs_colored_textured3d.sc`'s vertex stage (`v_color0=(u_vertexColorEnabled3D.x>0.5?a_color0:vec4(1))*u_diffuseColor`), mirroring `vs_colored3d.sc`; wired `diffuseColor3DUnif_`/`vertexColorEn3DUnif_` into the `coloredTextured3DProgram_` submit branch in `BgfxGraphicsBackend.cpp` (both uniforms already existed, just weren't set for this program); regenerated `bgfx_shaders.hpp` via `python3 compile_shaders.py <shaderc> <bgfx-src>` (diff confirmed scoped to only the 4 `vs_colored_textured3d_*` byte arrays). Added one new pixel-readback test per backend (`examples/{easygl,vulkan,bgfx}_basiceffect_texture_vertexcolor_enabled_test.cpp`, registered in `CMakeLists.txt` as `{EasyGL,Vulkan,Bgfx}_BasicEffect_TextureVertexColorEnabled`): full-screen NDC quad of `VertexPositionColorTexture` vertices, 3 distinctly-valued non-white/opaque inputs chosen so the true 3-way product and every 2-of-3 partial product are numerically distinct — texture color `(200,100,50)`, vertex color `(150,200,100)`, `DiffuseColor=(0.8,0.4,0.6)` → correct output `(94,31,12)` exactly reproduced on all 3 backends after the fix (verified via `GetBackBufferData`, not assumed); partial-product failure references asserted against: texture×diffuse-only `(160,40,30)` (Task 366's own result, reused numerically), vertex×diffuse-only `(120,80,60)`, texture×vertex-only/diffuse-ignored `(118,78,20)`, plus each single input alone. Bgfx test needs the same `RasterizerState::CullNone` workaround as Tasks 364–366 (Task 884, untouched here). **Found and fixed a second, pre-existing latent bug this task's fix exposed**: Task 189's `easygl_basiceffect_combinations_test.cpp` case (d) ("Color×Texture") set `VertexColorEnabled=false` while still expecting the vertex color to appear in the output — it only ever passed because EasyGL's stride-24 shader ignored the flag entirely (the exact bug this task fixes); after the fix it correctly produced texture×diffuse only (white, flag correctly honored), failing the test's stale expectation. Fixed by setting `VertexColorEnabled=true` (restoring the test's own stated intent — "Vertex color × texture" — rather than weakening the assertion), confirmed green again. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass: EasyGL ctest 3417/3420 (3 pre-existing: `EasyGL_MRT_TwoAttachments`, `EasyGL_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `easy-gl-resource-smoke-tests`, unchanged after the case-(d) fix — confirmed zero other stride-24 consumers were affected by grepping every `VertexPositionColorTexture` use in `examples/`/`tests/` for reliance on the old unconditional-vertex-color-multiply behavior: `easygl_vertex_formats_test.cpp`'s stride-24 case already sets `VertexColorEnabled=true` and leaves `DiffuseColor` at its default White, so the new `×uDiffuseColor` multiply is a no-op there — reran it directly to confirm, still 4/4 PASS). Bgfx ctest 3323/3323, **100% pass, zero failures** (better than the usual 1-flaky-`CueTest` baseline — didn't flake this run). Vulkan ctest 3340/3353, 13 pre-existing failures, exact-name match against Task 365's documented baseline (5× `Vulkan_BlendState_*`, 5× `Vulkan_DepthStencilState_*`, `Vulkan_GraphicsDevice_ReferenceStencil`, `Vulkan_RenderTargetCube_SampleAfterUnbind`, `Vulkan_DepthBias`) — no new failures, no `Vulkan_FillMode_WireFrame` or `CueTest` flakes this run. |
| 368 | Pixel test: one directional light enabled                                | ✅      | **2 real bugs found and fixed, one shared (all 3 backends), one Bgfx-only.** Derived the expected formula directly from FNA source: with `LightingEnabled=true` and `DirectionalLight1`/`2` both disabled (their real default), `BasicEffect.cs`'s `oneLight` optimization selects `shaderIndex=1+16=17` → `VSBasicOneLight`/`PSBasicVertexLightingNoFog`. `Lighting.fxh`'s `ComputeLights(eyeVector,worldNormal,1)`: `dotL=dot(-Direction,N)`, `diffuse=max(dotL,0)` (the `step(0,dotL)*dotL` idiom — a back-facing surface contributes exactly zero, not a negative value), `result.Diffuse=diffuse*Light0.DiffuseColor*DiffuseColor.rgb+EmissiveColor`. With `EmissiveColor` at its default `(0,0,0)` (deferred to Task 369, matching Tasks 364–367's precedent) and `AmbientLightColor` added per `ComputeLights`' actual formula: **expected fragment = (AmbientLightColor + DirectionalLight0.DiffuseColor × max(dot(-Light0Direction,N),0)) × DiffuseColor**, texture kept white to isolate the lighting formula (same one-variable-at-a-time style as Tasks 365/366). `SpecularColor`/`SpecularPower` deliberately not exercised: `DirectionalLight0.SpecularColor` left at its default `(0,0,0)` makes FNA's own specular term mathematically zero regardless of whether CNA forwards it, so this test doesn't depend on the known, separately-tracked "SpecularColor/SpecularPower/DirectionalLight1/2 not forwarded" gap (squarely Task 369's scope, not this one's). **Bug 1 (shared C++, all 3 backends)**: `BasicEffect::FillGpuDrawParams()` forwarded `DirectionalLight0`'s `Direction`/`DiffuseColor` to the GPU **unconditionally, never checking `DirectionalLight0.Enabled` at all**. Real FNA's `DirectionalLight.Enabled` setter (`DirectionalLight.cs`) zeroes the light's GPU-facing diffuse/specular parameters when disabled, regardless of what `DiffuseColor` is still set to at the C# property level — CNA's `DirectionalLight` class has no such side effect (a plain flag, consistent with this project's stock effects bypassing FNA's `EffectParameter` reflection pipeline, Task 351/361 finding), so the gating has to happen in `FillGpuDrawParams()` itself. Fixed by reading `DirectionalLight0.getEnabledProperty()` and substituting `Vector3::Zero` for the forwarded diffuse color when disabled — one fix, common code, covers all 3 backends. **Bug 2 (Bgfx-only, much wider-reaching than this task)**: `BgfxGraphicsBackend.cpp`'s `MakeBgfxLayout(stride)` **never declared a `Normal` or `TexCoord0` vertex attribute for any stride except 52** — every non-skinned stride (16/20/24/32) fell through to a single `else` branch that only ever declared `Position` + `Color0` + raw `skip()` padding, regardless of what the actual vertex struct contains. For stride 32 (`VertexPositionNormalTexture`, this task's own vertex format), that means `a_normal` was **never bound to real data** — bgfx supplies an unbound attribute's default, effectively zero, so `normalize(a_normal)` combined with any light direction always produced `NdotL≈0`, silently sinking every lit pixel to ambient-only regardless of the real per-vertex normal. Confirmed by testing with an intentionally fully-saturated normal `(0,0,-1)` (`NdotL` should be `1`) — it produced the *identical* ambient-only result as the back-facing/disabled cases, proving the normal wasn't reaching the shader at all, not just a formula/sign bug. The same root cause silently breaks `TexCoord0` interpolation for strides 20/24 too (`VertexPositionTexture`, `VertexPositionColorTexture`) — invisible in every prior task's tests (Tasks 189, 364–367) because they universally use 1×1 solid-color textures, where an unbound/defaulted UV samples the identical texel as a correct UV. Fixed by giving `MakeBgfxLayout()` a dedicated branch per stride (20: Position+TexCoord0; 24: Position+Color0+TexCoord0; 32: Position+Normal+TexCoord0; 52 unchanged; default/16: Position+Color0+pad, matching the original behavior for the one stride it was actually correct for) — byte offsets computed automatically by `bgfx::VertexLayout::add()`'s sequential-append behavior, matching each real CNA vertex struct's field order exactly. **Verified genuine discriminating power for both fixes independently**: reverted the `FillGpuDrawParams()` fix (`git stash`) and reran — the `DirectionalLight0.Enabled=false` case failed exactly as predicted (showed the lit result instead of ambient-only), then restored and reconfirmed 5/5 pass. The `MakeBgfxLayout()` fix was confirmed load-bearing by the fully-saturated-normal probe described above (before the fix: ambient-only regardless of normal; after: correctly saturated). Added one new pixel-readback test per backend (`examples/{easygl,vulkan,bgfx}_basiceffect_one_light_test.cpp`, registered in `CMakeLists.txt` as `{EasyGL,Vulkan,Bgfx}_BasicEffect_OneLight`): a non-saturating `NdotL=0.5` normal (`(0.866,0,-0.5)`, light direction `(0,0,1)`) to prove the dot product is real and not a boolean lit/unlit check — expected `(77,51,51)` from `Ambient=(0.1,0.1,0.1)`, `Light0.DiffuseColor=(1.0,0.6,0.2)`, material `DiffuseColor=(0.5,0.5,1.0)`; a back-facing normal (`(-0.866,0,0.5)`) to prove the negative-dot clamp, expected ambient-only `(13,13,26)`; and `DirectionalLight0.Enabled=false` reusing the lit-geometry normal, expected the same ambient-only `(13,13,26)`, proving the new `Enabled` gate. Failure-mode references asserted against: NdotL=1-saturated `(140,89,77)` and ambient-additive-term-dropped `(64,38,26)`, both numerically well clear of the real `(77,51,51)` result. Bgfx test needs the same `RasterizerState::CullNone` workaround as Tasks 364–367 (Task 884, untouched here). Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass, zero new failures despite Bug 2's wide blast radius: EasyGL ctest 3417/3421 (3 pre-existing + 1 reconfirmed-flaky `NetworkSessionTest.FindReturnsEmptyCollection`, passes cleanly in isolation), Vulkan ctest 3339/3354 (13 pre-existing + 2 reconfirmed-flaky `ENetDiscoveryServiceTest.UnregisterHostStopsAnsweringQueries`/`CueTest.StopAsAuthoredTransitionsFromStoppingToStoppedOnceTailFinishes`, both pass cleanly in isolation), Bgfx ctest **3324/3324, 100% pass** — the Bgfx vertex-layout fix in particular was re-verified against the *entire* existing Bgfx suite (not just this task's own new test) precisely because of its wide reach, and nothing regressed. |
| 369 | Pixel test: ambient + emissive + specular combination                    | ✅      | **Scope narrowed after auditing the real size of the remaining gaps — 1 real bug found and fixed, 2 new dedicated follow-up tasks opened rather than rushing a bundled fix.** Re-derived the authoritative formula from FNA source, not assumed: `EffectHelpers.SetMaterialColor(lightingEnabled, alpha, diffuseColor, emissiveColor, ambientLightColor, ...)` has two completely different branches. **Disabled-lighting branch** (`LightingEnabled=false` — the *default*, and the case every one of Tasks 364–367's tests actually used): `diffuse = (DiffuseColor + EmissiveColor) * Alpha`, forwarded as the single GPU diffuse parameter — ambient/lights are never computed at all in this branch. **Enabled-lighting branch**: `DiffuseColor` param = `DiffuseColor*Alpha` (unchanged), but a *second*, confusingly-named `EmissiveColor` GPU parameter is set to `(EmissiveColor + AmbientLightColor*DiffuseColor)*Alpha` — FNA's shader-side `ComputeLights` formula `result.Diffuse = mul(diffuse,lightDiffuse)*DiffuseColor.rgb + EmissiveColor` is only correct because that GPU "EmissiveColor" already has the ambient term baked in. Confirmed algebraically that CNA's independently-structured lit formula (`(ambient+light*NdotL)*diffuseColor`, ambient forwarded as its own raw uniform rather than baked into the emissive slot, verified correct back in Task 368) is mathematically identical to FNA's once `+EmissiveColor` (raw, alpha-scaled, added **after** the ambient/diffuse multiply, not multiplied into it) is included — algebra: FNA expands to `(light*NdotL+ambient)*diffuseColor*alpha + emissiveColor*alpha`, exactly matching CNA's `(ambient+light*NdotL)*(diffuseColor*alpha) + emissiveColor*alpha` once the missing term is added. **Bug found and fixed (disabled-lighting branch, shared C++, all 3 backends, no shader changes needed)**: `BasicEffect::FillGpuDrawParams()` forwarded `DiffuseColor*Alpha` alone in every case, **always silently dropping `EmissiveColor`** — the exact gap Task 366 deferred. Fixed by branching in `FillGpuDrawParams()` exactly like `SetMaterialColor()` does: `(DiffuseColor+EmissiveColor)*Alpha` when `!LightingEnabled`, plain `DiffuseColor*Alpha` when lit (since the lit path's emissive term is handled separately — see below). Added one new pixel-readback test per backend (`examples/{easygl,vulkan,bgfx}_basiceffect_emissive_test.cpp`, registered as `{EasyGL,Vulkan,Bgfx}_BasicEffect_Emissive`): reused Task 366/367's `(200,100,50)` texture color with new `DiffuseColor=(0.3,0.2,0.1)`/`EmissiveColor=(0.2,0.1,0.4)` (sum `(0.5,0.3,0.5)`) so the correct product `(100,30,25)` is numerically distinct from either "component ignored" failure mode (`(60,20,5)` diffuse-only, `(40,10,20)` emissive-only). **Verified genuine discriminating power**: reverted the fix (`git stash`) and reran — 1/3 assertions correctly failed with the exact pre-fix value `(60,20,5)`; restored and reconfirmed 3/3 on all 3 backends, all exactly `(100,30,25)`. **Deliberately NOT fixed here, scoped out into 2 new dedicated tasks after auditing the real size of the work** (matching this project's own precedent of not bundling large, multi-pipeline-site changes into a single task — see Tasks 868/870/880's "Do not do yet" entries in `NEXT.md`): **Task 885** — the lit-path `+EmissiveColor` term (needs a new `uEmissiveColor`/`u_emissiveColor` uniform + shader-formula change on EasyGL's `EnsureLit3DProgram()` and Bgfx's `vs_lit_textured3d.sc`/`fs_lit_textured3d.sc`, both straightforward; but on **Vulkan specifically requires expanding the shared 128-byte `pipelineLayoutExt3D_` push-constant budget** — confirmed via `vulkaninfo` that the actual test GPU supports up to 256 bytes, but this exact 128-byte array (`FillExtPushConst()`'s `float[32]`) is also reused byte-for-byte by `DrawPrimitivesEx`'s `SkinnedEffect` path, so widening it is a genuine shared-architecture change outside a single BasicEffect pixel-test task's scope, not merely a Vulkan-shader-only tweak) and multi-light forwarding (`DirectionalLight1`/`2` still completely unforwarded, confirmed unchanged since Task 361). **Task 886** — real specular highlights: confirmed **zero specular infrastructure exists anywhere for `BasicEffect`** on any backend (no `GpuDrawParams` fields for `specularColor`/`specularPower`/per-light specular color — only the unrelated, much simpler `envMapSpecular` flat-tint field used by `EnvironmentMapEffect`; no eye-position wiring in `BasicEffect::FillGpuDrawParams()` at all, though the `eyePositionWorld`-from-inverse-view technique already exists as prior art in `EnvironmentMapEffect.cpp`/`SkinnedEffect.cpp` to reuse); implementing real Blinn-Phong specular needs new world-space-position varyings, half-vector math, and specular-power exponentiation added to all 3 backends' lit shaders — a genuinely new feature, not a bug fix, sized similarly to the already-tracked Task 868/870/878/879 backend-parity items. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass, zero new failures: EasyGL ctest 3419/3422 (3 pre-existing), Vulkan ctest 3342/3355 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes this run), Bgfx ctest 3324/3325 (1 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest.PlayCalledTwiceWhileAlreadyPlayingIsANoOpAndDoesNotDuplicateInstances`, passes cleanly in isolation). |
| 370 | Cross-backend BasicEffect image comparison suite                         | ✅      | **Closes Phase 42.** Capstone test combining everything Tasks 364–369 verified individually into one scene — `TextureEnabled`+`VertexColorEnabled`+`DiffuseColor`+`EmissiveColor`, `LightingEnabled=false` — to prove the fixes compose correctly together, not just in isolation. Expected formula (re-derived, same shaderIndex=7 family as Task 367, now with Task 369's `+EmissiveColor` fold-in): `TextureColor × VertexColor × (DiffuseColor+EmissiveColor) × Alpha`. **First `BasicEffect` pixel test in the project to use a real multi-texel texture** (a 2×2, 4-distinct-color texture) instead of a 1×1 solid color — deliberately chosen to exercise the exact Bgfx `TexCoord0`-binding path Task 368 found and fixed (`MakeBgfxLayout()` previously left `a_texcoord0` permanently unbound for stride-24, invisible with 1×1 textures since every UV samples the same texel regardless). 4 separate draws per backend, each holding UV constant across the whole quad to sample exactly one texel (at its 0.25/0.75 center, avoiding bilinear-filtering-boundary ambiguity), each independently checked against its own FNA-derived expected value: `(200,100,50)`→`(99,52,23)`, `(50,200,100)`→`(25,104,47)`, `(100,50,200)`→`(49,26,93)`, `(150,150,150)`→`(74,78,70)` (`VertexColor=(180,220,140)`, `DiffuseColor=(0.6,0.4,0.8)`, `EmissiveColor=(0.1,0.2,0.05)`). **Result: all 3 backends produced byte-identical pixel output at all 4 sample points** — `(99,52,23)`, `(25,103,47)`, `(49,26,93)`, `(74,78,70)` on EasyGL, Vulkan, *and* Bgfx alike (the top-right sample's G channel is 1 off from the hand-computed `104` on all 3 equally — ordinary floating-point rounding-direction noise, not a per-backend discrepancy) — genuine cross-backend consistency, confirmed empirically rather than assumed, not just "each matches its own formula in isolation." **No new bugs found** — pure integration verification, passed cleanly on the first attempt because Tasks 364–369's fixes were already in place; this is itself a meaningful confirmation that those fixes don't just work in their own narrow test but hold up when combined. Added one test per backend (`examples/{easygl,vulkan,bgfx}_basiceffect_combined_test.cpp`, registered as `{EasyGL,Vulkan,Bgfx}_BasicEffect_Combined`). Bgfx test needs the same `RasterizerState::CullNone` workaround as Tasks 364–369 (Task 884, untouched here). Wrote `docs/basiceffect-support.md`, a full Phase 42 synthesis (mirroring `docs/rendertarget-support.md`'s Phase 39 precedent): per-task summary, full support matrix, and the 2 tracked follow-ups (Tasks 885/886) opened by Task 369. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass, zero new failures: EasyGL ctest 3419/3423 (3 pre-existing + 1 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest`), Vulkan ctest 3341/3356 (13 pre-existing + 1 reconfirmed order-dependent `Vulkan_FillMode_WireFrame` flake + 1 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest`), Bgfx ctest 3325/3326 (1 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest`, failed once then passed twice on reruns — genuinely timing-flaky, not a regression). |

| 371 | Audit `AlphaTestEffect` properties and defaults against FNA   | ✅      | **Opens Phase 43. Verify-only — no bugs found**, unlike Task 361's `BasicEffect` opener. Read FNA's `AlphaTestEffect.cs` line-by-line against CNA's `AlphaTestEffect.hpp`/`.cpp`. **All defaults match exactly**: `FogEnabled`/`VertexColorEnabled`=false, `World`/`View`/`Projection`=Identity, `DiffuseColor`=`Vector3.One`(1,1,1), `Alpha`=1, `FogStart`=0, `FogEnd`=1, `AlphaFunction`=`CompareFunction.Greater`, `ReferenceAlpha`=0 (unclamped int, matching FNA — no `[0,255]` clamp exists in either), `isEqNe`=false. Confirmed CNA's `EffectDirtyFlags` constants (`WorldViewProj=1, MaterialColor=8, Fog=16, FogEnable=32, AlphaTest=64, ShaderIndex=128`) are byte-exact matches of FNA's actual `[Flags] enum EffectDirtyFlags` (`EffectHelpers.cs`) — CNA correctly omits the unused `World=2`/`EyePosition=4` bits since `AlphaTestEffect.OnApply()` never references them in FNA either. `OnApply()`'s inlined `SetWorldViewProjAndFog`/`SetFogVector` logic matches FNA's shared helper literal-for-literal (fog-vector degenerate-case zero-divide guard, `M13/M23/M33/M43` fog-plane extraction, all present). The `AlphaTest` vec4 switch statement (`Less`/`LessEqual`/`GreaterEqual`/`Greater`/`Equal`/`NotEqual`/`Never`/`Always`) matches FNA's 8-case switch exactly, including the `0.5/255` comparison-tolerance threshold and the `eqNe` shader-index-invalidation side effect. `Clone()`'s field-copy list matches FNA's clone constructor (both correctly omit copying `isEqNe` — it's always safely recomputed via the fresh instance's `DirtyAll`-forced `OnApply()`). **Confirmed, not a bug, tracked separately**: `FillGpuDrawParams()` never forwards `FogEnabled`/`FogColor`/`FogStart`/`FogEnd` to any backend at all — `AlphaTestEffect`'s fog support is a total no-op on the GPU today, but this is squarely **Task 378**'s ("verify fog behavior in AlphaTestEffect") own scope, not an omission from this properties/defaults audit. **Zero existing test coverage found** (no `AlphaTestEffectTests.cpp` at all) — Task 372's job. No production code changed, no rebuild needed (pure source audit, matching Task 340/365/366's "no code changed, no regression risk" precedent). |
| 372 | Unit test default values for all `AlphaTestEffect` properties | ✅      | Task 371 found zero existing test coverage for `AlphaTestEffect` at all — wrote new `tests/Microsoft/Xna/Framework/Graphics/AlphaTestEffectTests.cpp` from scratch, mirroring Task 362's `BasicEffectTests.cpp` fixture/style exactly (`GraphicsDevice gd; AlphaTestEffect fx{gd};` fixture, one `TEST_F` per property). 27 tests: all 8 property defaults (`World`/`View`/`Projection`=Identity, `DiffuseColor`=One, `Alpha`=1, `FogEnabled`=false, `FogStart`=0, `FogEnd`=1, `FogColor`=Zero, `Texture`=null, `VertexColorEnabled`=false, `AlphaFunction`=Greater, `ReferenceAlpha`=0 — all reconfirmed matching Task 371's audit, no new bugs), a round-trip setter test per property (every setter actually stores what it's given — genuinely new coverage, since Task 371 was audit-only and never exercised the setters), a `Clone()` test (sets every property to a non-default value, clones, confirms all 8 mutable properties survive the copy — matches FNA's clone-constructor field list), and a `GetTypeName()` test (confirms the NOXNA `"Microsoft.Xna.Framework.Graphics.AlphaTestEffect"` string). No production code changed (pure test-authoring task, no bugs to fix). Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass (pure C++ property tests, no pixel rendering — re-verified on all 3 backend configs anyway per standing practice since `CnaTests` is backend-specific per build): EasyGL ctest 3447/3450 (3 pre-existing, +27 new tests, exact expected count), Vulkan ctest 3370/3383 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes), Bgfx ctest 3353/3353 (100% pass, no flakes). |
| 373 | Pixel test every `CompareFunction` on EasyGL                  | ✅      | **Verify-only, zero bugs — genuine new coverage, not a duplicate of Task 190.** Task 190's `easygl_alphatest_modes_test.cpp` only ever tested the exact `alpha==reference` boundary (both 128/255) for all 8 `CompareFunction` values — necessary (it's the *only* value that can distinguish `Less` from `LessEqual`, `Greater` from `GreaterEqual`, `Equal` from everything else) but insufficient: it never independently confirmed which *side* of the boundary passes for the inequality functions, since a single point can't distinguish "correct threshold direction" from "coincidentally correct at this one value". Added `easygl_alphatest_comparefunction_sweep_test.cpp`: sweeps 3 alpha values (`64/255` below, `128/255` at, `192/255` above) against a fixed `reference=128` for all 8 functions — 24 total assertions, fully characterizing each function's pass/fail region rather than one data point. Re-derived expected below/at/above triples directly from FNA's `AlphaTestEffect.cs` `OnApply()` switch semantics (e.g. `Less`: drawn/discard/discard; `LessEqual`: drawn/drawn/discard; `GreaterEqual`: discard/drawn/drawn; `Equal`: discard/drawn/discard; `NotEqual`: drawn/discard/drawn — every function's 3-point signature is unique, confirming the sweep has real discriminating power beyond Task 190's single point). **24/24 PASS, no bugs found** — `AlphaTestEffect`'s comparison logic was already fully correct on EasyGL (matches Task 371's audit finding of a faithful `FillGpuDrawParams()`/`OnApply()` port).
