Copying Task 190/373's `dev.SetDepthTestEnabled(false)` call verbatim (present in the EasyGL/Vulkan test bodies) **crashed on Bgfx**: `GraphicsDevice::SetDepthTestEnabled`/`SetBlendEnabled`/`SetDepthWriteEnabled` are unconditional-throw stubs on the Bgfx backend (`ThrowNo3DState()`) — confirmed this is a known, pre-existing, intentional gap by finding 2 other existing Bgfx tests (`bgfx_env_map_test.cpp`, `bgfx_render_target_cube_sample_test.cpp`) that already document skipping the same call for the same reason. Fixed by removing the call (not needed here: each of the 24 iterations calls `dev.Clear()`, which resets the depth buffer, before drawing a single full-screen quad at `z=0`, so depth-test state is irrelevant regardless of its default). Also needs the standard `RasterizerState::CullNone` workaround (Task 884). 24/24 PASS after the fix, same expected values as Tasks 373/374. Registered as `Bgfx_AlphaTest_CompareFunctionSweep`. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass, zero new failures: EasyGL ctest 3447/3451 (3 pre-existing + 1 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest`, confirmed via 3 isolated reruns to be genuinely timing-flaky — 2/3 passed), Vulkan ctest 3370/3384 (13 pre-existing + 1 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest`), Bgfx ctest 3354/3354 (100% pass). |
| 376 | Verify alpha reference value scaling 0–255 vs 0–1             | ✅      | **Verify-only, zero bugs — dedicated, GPU-independent lock-in complementing Tasks 371/373's already-correct findings.** Tasks 371 (source audit) and 373 (pixel sweep, one fixed `reference=128`) already established the `referenceAlpha/255.0f` scaling is correct, but neither tested multiple *reference* values directly, nor exercised true out-of-range inputs via a fast unit test. Extended `AlphaTestEffectTests.cpp` with a parameterized `AlphaTestReferenceScalingTest` (`::testing::TestWithParam<int>`, values `{-10, 0, 1, 64, 128, 254, 255, 300}` — boundary plus genuinely out-of-range) calling `FillGpuDrawParams()` directly (no GPU/pixel readback needed, pure CPU arithmetic) and checking the `alphaTest` vec4 output precisely for both switch-case shapes FNA's `AlphaTestEffect.cs` uses: `CompareFunction::Greater` (`threshold` folded into `X`, `Y` unused) and `CompareFunction::Equal` (`X`=plain reference, `threshold` in `Y`). 16 parameterized assertions (8 values × 2 shapes) all matched `referenceAlpha/255.0f` (±`0.5/255` where applicable) within `1e-6` float tolerance — confirms the scaling holds exactly across the full practical range **and** stays unclamped for out-of-range inputs, matching FNA's own unclamped `int referenceAlpha` field exactly (re-confirmed Task 371's finding: neither FNA nor CNA clamps `ReferenceAlpha` to `[0,255]`). Added one more direct test (`ReferenceAlphaIsNotClampedOnSet`) confirming the property getter/setter itself round-trips `-10`/`300` unmodified. 17 new tests total, zero bugs found (formula was already correct, per Task 371/373's precedent). Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass, zero new failures: EasyGL ctest 3465/3468 (3 pre-existing, +17 new tests), Vulkan ctest 3374/3388 (13 pre-existing + 1 reconfirmed-flaky statistical `CueTest.PlayWeightedVariationFavorsHigherWeightEntryStatistically`), Bgfx ctest 3371/3371 (100% pass). |
| 377 | Verify vertex color and diffuse color interaction             | ✅      | **"If supported" resolved by investigation: supported and correct on EasyGL; confirmed a real, significant, previously-unknown bug on Vulkan and Bgfx, deliberately not fixed here (new Task 887 opened).** FNA reference (`Graphics/Effect/StockEffects/HLSL/AlphaTestEffect.fx`): `VSAlphaTestVcNoFog` does `vout.Diffuse *= vin.Color` on top of `ComputeCommonVSOutput()`'s `vout.Diffuse=DiffuseColor`; `PSAlphaTestLtGtNoFog`/`PSAlphaTestEqNeNoFog` compute `color=SAMPLE_TEXTURE(...)*pin.Diffuse` and run `clip(...)` against **that final, already-vertex-color-multiplied `color.a`** — meaning `VertexColorEnabled` doesn't just tint RGB, it also feeds into whether the alpha test passes at all. Expected: RGB = `TextureColor×VertexColor×DiffuseColor×Alpha`; alpha-test reference comparison runs against the same fully-combined alpha, not material alpha alone. **EasyGL: correct, confirmed by pixel test.** EasyGL has no separate "AlphaTestEffect-only" shader family — it reuses the same generic per-stride shaders (`EnsureColoredTextured3DProgram()` for this stride-24 case) that `BasicEffect` also uses, each already carrying `uAlphaTest`+`discard` logic. Added `easygl_alphatest_vertexcolor_diffuse_test.cpp`: white 1×1 texture (isolates the vertex×diffuse interaction), `VertexColor=(200,100,50,200)`, `DiffuseColor=(0.6,0.4,0.8)`, `Alpha=0.8`. Two `CompareFunction::Greater` cases distinguish "combined alpha used" from "diffuse alpha alone": `ReferenceAlpha=100` → combined alpha `160/255` passes, confirms RGB `(96,32,32)` exactly; `ReferenceAlpha=180` → combined alpha `160/255` correctly fails/discards, while the wrong "diffuse-alone" hypothesis's alpha (`204/255`) would have incorrectly passed. **2/2 PASS, exact match.** **Vulkan and Bgfx: confirmed real bug, `VertexColorEnabled` has zero effect, true by default.** Both backends use a fundamentally different architecture for `AlphaTestEffect` — one generic alpha-test pipeline/shader shared across all vertex strides (`alpha_test3d.vert/frag.glsl` on Vulkan; `vs/fs_alpha_test3d.sc` on Bgfx) that **only ever declares `position`+`texcoord` vertex inputs, never a color attribute** (confirmed by reading `GetOrCreatePipelineAlphaTest3D`'s `VkVertexInputAttributeDescription` array — always exactly 2 attributes, position+UV, regardless of stride). This routes ahead of the already-correct, already-vertex-color-capable `colored_textured3d` pipeline whenever `alphaTestActive` is true — and critically, **`AlphaTestEffect`'s own defaults (`AlphaFunction=Greater`, `ReferenceAlpha=0`) already produce `alphaTest.z=-1`**, so `needsAlphaTest`/`alphaTestActive` is true for essentially all real-world `AlphaTestEffect` usage, not just an edge case — only the pointless `AlphaFunction=Always` setting avoids this pipeline. **Verified empirically, not just by code reading**: built a temporary (uncommitted) Vulkan port of the exact same test — both cases failed, with `got=(122,82,163)` for *both* `ReferenceAlpha=100` and `=180`, exactly matching the predicted "vertex color completely dropped, `RGB=TextureColor×DiffuseColor×Alpha` only" formula (`0.6×0.8×255=122.4`, `0.4×0.8×255=81.6`, `0.8×0.8×255=163.2`) — and both references incorrectly "pass" because the diffuse-alone alpha (`204/255`) clears both thresholds regardless of vertex color. Deleted the temporary test/CMake entry after confirming (not committed — would encode today's confirmed-wrong behavior as a "passing" assertion, an anti-pattern that could be silently forgotten once Task 887 fixes it; mirrors Task 366's precedent of documenting deferred findings in prose rather than committing a test that asserts wrong behavior). **Opened Task 887** to track the actual fix: unifying Vulkan/Bgfx's alpha-test dispatch with their already-correct per-stride textured/colored-textured pipelines (mirroring EasyGL's architecture) — a genuinely large, multi-shader-file (6 shader files across 2 backends), multi-backend change, not a Task-377-sized fix. No production code changed for Task 377 itself (EasyGL was already correct). Full EasyGL regression: 3466/3469 (3 pre-existing, +1 new test); Vulkan/Bgfx untouched (no production or committed test changes for either). |
| 378 | Verify fog behavior in AlphaTestEffect                        | ✅      | **Real bug found and fixed on EasyGL; a much larger, pre-existing, project-wide gap discovered and NOT fixed (new Task 888).** `AlphaTestEffect::FillGpuDrawParams()` never forwarded `FogEnabled`/`FogColor`/`FogStart`/`FogEnd` to the GPU at all — fog was a total no-op for `AlphaTestEffect` regardless of settings, even though EasyGL's shared per-stride shaders (`EnsureColored3DProgram()` etc., the same shaders `BasicEffect` also uses) already fully implement fog generically (`uFogEnabled`/`uFogColor`/`uFogStart`/`uFogEnd` + `mix(uFogColor,FragColor.rgb,vFogFactor)` blend, wired since before this task, and already forwarded correctly by `BasicEffect::FillGpuDrawParams()`). **Fixed** by forwarding these 4 fields in `AlphaTestEffect::FillGpuDrawParams()`, mirroring `BasicEffect`'s exact pattern (one subtlety: `FogColor` is backed by an `EffectParameter*`, not a plain field like `BasicEffect`'s `fogColor_`, so the fix reads it via `getFogColorProperty()` instead) — no shader changes needed on EasyGL. Added `easygl_alphatest_fog_test.cpp`: a 3-point Z sweep (not just near/far endpoints) proving the blend is genuine interpolation, not an on/off switch — `DiffuseColor=(0.8,0.2,0.4)`, `FogColor=(0.1,0.6,0.9)`. **Real implementation detail discovered while designing the test**: CNA's fog formula uses raw, unprojected vertex Z directly (`World`/`View`/`Projection` all Identity in every existing pixel test in this project), meaning Z values outside OpenGL's valid clip-space NDC range `[-1,1]` get frustum-clipped away entirely before the fragment shader runs — `FogStart=-0.9`/`FogEnd=0.9` (not FNA-typical world-space distances like `0`/`10`) were chosen to stay safely inside that range. 3 assertions: `z=-0.9` (at `FogStart`) → unblended `(204,51,102)`; `z=0.9` (at `FogEnd`) → pure fog `(26,153,230)`; `z=0` (halfway) → 50/50 blend `(115,102,166)`. **3/3 PASS, near-exact match** (`(25,153,229)` vs `(26,153,230)` at full fog, well within tolerance). **Verified genuine discriminating power**: reverted the fix (`git stash`) and reran — 2/3 assertions correctly failed (both non-zero-Z cases showed the unblended material color, proving fog was genuinely a no-op before the fix); restored and reconfirmed 3/3. **Much larger finding, confirmed not fixed**: while checking whether Vulkan/Bgfx's shaders could receive the same simple fix, grepped **every** `.glsl` (Vulkan) and `.sc` (Bgfx) shader file in both backends for "fog" — **zero matches anywhere**, not just in the `AlphaTestEffect`-specific shaders Task 377 already flagged. This means fog is a **total, project-wide no-op on Vulkan and Bgfx for every 3D effect**, including `BasicEffect` — despite `BasicEffect::FillGpuDrawParams()` already (and correctly) forwarding the fog fields; the C++ side was never the problem there, only the missing GPU-side implementation. Implementing real fog on Vulkan/Bgfx means adding fog uniforms/varyings + the blend formula to essentially every 3D shader in both backends (8+ shader pairs × 2 backends) — a genuinely large, project-wide feature addition, not a Task-378-sized fix. **Opened Task 888** to track it. No Vulkan/Bgfx test added here, for the same reason as Task 377 (would encode today's confirmed no-op as a passing assertion). Full 3-backend regression, zero new failures despite the shared-code change: EasyGL ctest 3467/3470 (3 pre-existing, +1 new test), Vulkan ctest 3375/3388 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes), Bgfx ctest 3371/3371 (100% pass). |
| 379 | Verify texture disabled or null texture behavior              | ✅      | **Real bug found and fixed on Bgfx — general, not `AlphaTestEffect`-specific.** FNA's `AlphaTestEffect` has no `TextureEnabled` flag at all (unlike `BasicEffect`) — every one of its 4 shader variants unconditionally samples `Texture`; a texture is *required* for meaningful use, and FNA itself doesn't define what happens with a null texture (sampling an unbound texture unit is graphics-API-dependent undefined behavior, not a documented XNA fallback). CNA's own, already-established, cross-backend convention (used by every texture-sampling shader, not just this effect) is to fall back to a 1×1 opaque **white** texture rather than leaving the sampler unbound — a safe, defined, intentional deviation from FNA's undefined behavior. **EasyGL and Vulkan: already correct**, confirmed by pixel test (`EnsureDefaultWhiteTexture()`/`defaultWhiteDescSet_`, both pre-existing). **Bgfx: real bug found.** Every one of Bgfx's 7 texture-binding call sites in `DrawPrimitivesEx` only called `bgfx::setTexture()` when a real texture was present (`if (params.texture0 && ...)`), with **no fallback at all** — a null-texture draw silently left whatever texture the *previous* draw call had bound (confirmed empirically: `git stash`-reverted rerun showed `(0,0,0)` black, not the previous draw's texture nor the correct white fallback — genuinely undefined, not just "wrong in one specific way"). **Fixed** by adding a `defaultWhiteTexture3D_` (1×1 opaque white, created once in the constructor, destroyed in the destructor) and an `else` branch to all 7 call sites uniformly — the bug affected every texture-sampling dispatch branch (`dualTexture`, `skinned`, `envMap`, `alphaTest`, `lighting`, `textureEnabled`, `textureEnabled+vertexColorEnabled`), not just the one `AlphaTestEffect` happens to exercise, so fixing only the `AlphaTestEffect`-relevant branch would have left 6 others silently broken. Added one pixel test per backend (`examples/{easygl,vulkan,bgfx}_alphatest_null_texture_test.cpp`, registered as `{EasyGL,Vulkan,Bgfx}_AlphaTest_NullTexture`): draws with a real, distinctive texture first (establishes "previous draw" state), then with `Texture=nullptr`, using non-white `DiffuseColor=(0.6,0.4,0.8)` so 3 hypotheses are numerically distinct — correct white-fallback `(153,102,204)`, stale-previous-texture-retained `(120,40,40)`, or black-fallback `(0,0,0)`. **3/3 PASS on all 3 backends, exact match.** **Verified genuine discriminating power**: reverted the Bgfx fix and reran — the null-texture case correctly failed with `(0,0,0)` instead of `(153,102,204)`; restored and reconfirmed 3/3. **Noted, not fixed** (deliberately out of scope — a much narrower, lower-impact edge case): Bgfx's *second* texture slot (`texColor3DSampler2_`/`params.texture1`, used only by `DualTextureEffect`) has the identical unconditional-skip shape, but `DualTextureEffect` always requires both textures by design, unlike `AlphaTestEffect` where a null texture is a real usage pattern — not worth its own task number given the narrow scope, just noted here for whoever next touches `DualTextureEffect`. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass, zero new failures despite the wide-reaching (7-call-site) Bgfx change: EasyGL ctest 3468/3471 (3 pre-existing, +1 new test), Vulkan ctest 3376/3389 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes), Bgfx ctest **3372/3372, 100% pass** — re-verified against the entire Bgfx suite given the fix's reach across all 7 texture-binding sites, and nothing regressed. |
| 380 | Document AlphaTestEffect backend parity                       | ✅      | **Closes Phase 43.** Wrote `docs/alphatesteffect-support.md`, synthesizing Tasks 371–379 (mirrors `docs/basiceffect-support.md`'s Phase 42 style): per-task summary sections, a full 3-backend support matrix (property defaults, `CompareFunction` sweep, `ReferenceAlpha` scaling, `VertexColorEnabled` interaction, fog, null-texture fallback), and an "Open, tracked follow-up work" section listing Task 887 (Vulkan/Bgfx alpha-test vertex-color unification) and Task 888 (Vulkan/Bgfx project-wide fog). No production code or tests changed — pure documentation task. |

| 381 | Audit `DualTextureEffect` properties and defaults against FNA     | ✅      | **Opens Phase 44. Verify-only — no bugs in this task's own scope**, mirroring Task 371's `AlphaTestEffect` opener. Read FNA's `DualTextureEffect.cs` line-by-line against CNA's `DualTextureEffect.hpp`/`.cpp`. **All defaults match exactly**: `FogEnabled`/`VertexColorEnabled`=false, `World`/`View`/`Projection`=Identity, `DiffuseColor`=`Vector3.One`, `Alpha`=1, `FogStart`=0, `FogEnd`=1, `FogColor`=Zero (via zero-initialized `fogColorParam_`, same pattern already confirmed correct for `AlphaTestEffect` in Task 371), `Texture`/`Texture2`=null. Confirmed CNA's `EffectDirtyFlags` constants used here (`WorldViewProj=1, MaterialColor=8, Fog=16, FogEnable=32, ShaderIndex=128, All=-1`) are byte-exact matches of the subset FNA's `DualTextureEffect.cs` actually uses (correctly omits `World=2`/`EyePosition=4`/`AlphaTest=64`, none of which `DualTextureEffect` ever references). Property setters' dirty-flag side effects match FNA exactly: `World`/`View` mark `WorldViewProj|Fog`, `Projection` marks `WorldViewProj` only, `DiffuseColor`/`Alpha` mark `MaterialColor`, `FogEnabled`/`VertexColorEnabled` mark `ShaderIndex` (plus `FogEnable` for the former) only on actual value change. `OnApply()`'s `SetWorldViewProjAndFog`/`SetFogVector` logic (inlined, same as `AlphaTestEffect`) matches FNA's shared `EffectHelpers` helper literal-for-literal, including the degenerate `fogStart==fogEnd` zero-divide guard and the `M13/M23/M33/M43` fog-plane extraction. The material-color formula (`Vector4(diffuseColor*alpha, alpha)`) and shader-index formula (`+1` if `!fogEnabled`, `+2` if `vertexColorEnabled`) match FNA's switch-free arithmetic exactly. `Clone()`'s field-copy list (`fogEnabled`, `vertexColorEnabled`, `world`/`view`/`projection`, `diffuseColor`, `alpha`, `fogStart`, `fogEnd`, plus `texture_`/`texture2_` — the latter two mirroring FNA's base `Effect(cloneSource)` constructor's `Parameters[i].texture = cloneSource.Parameters[i].texture` copy loop, done directly here since CNA stores `Texture`/`Texture2` as raw fields rather than through `EffectParameter` reflection, per the already-documented Task 351 architecture) matches FNA's clone constructor exactly. **Confirmed, not a bug, tracked separately for Task 388**: `FillGpuDrawParams()` never forwards `FogEnabled`/`FogColor`/`FogStart`/`FogEnd` to any backend at all, despite `DualTextureEffect` implementing `IEffectFog` with fully-correct defaults and `OnApply()` logic — the exact same bug shape Task 378 found and fixed for `AlphaTestEffect`, deliberately left for Task 388 ("verify fog behavior in `DualTextureEffect`") rather than fixed here, mirroring Task 371's own precedent of deferring the identical `AlphaTestEffect` finding to its dedicated fog task. **Test coverage**: zero dedicated unit tests for property defaults exist (no `DualTextureEffectTests.cpp`) — Task 382's job, mirroring Task 372's precedent — though nonzero pixel-integration coverage already exists from earlier phases (Tasks 133/135/191/293/294/296/297: two-layer blending, sampler-state/texture-address-mode/filter interaction, all EasyGL + one Vulkan test), none of which exercise fog, `Clone()`, or property-default assertions directly. `GetTypeName()` already correct (`"Microsoft.Xna.Framework.Graphics.DualTextureEffect"`). Doxygen coverage on every public member already complete. No production code changed, no rebuild needed (pure source audit, matching Task 340/361/365/366/371's "no code changed, no regression risk" precedent). |

No production code changed (pure test-authoring task, no bugs to fix). | 383 | Pixel test: two white textures, diffuse color red                 | ✅      | **Real bug found and fixed, all 3 backends.** FNA's `DualTextureEffect.fx` `PSDualTexture` does `color = SAMPLE(Texture); color.rgb *= 2; color *= overlay * pin.Diffuse;` — a `*2` doubling factor on the *first* texture's RGB channels only (never alpha), present in every existing FNA/XNA `DualTextureEffect` shader variant. Every prior CNA `DualTextureEffect` test (Tasks 133/135/191/293/294/296/297) used pure 0/1-saturated texture values, where a missing `*2` is invisible (`1×2` clamps right back to `1`, `0×2` stays `0`) — so this genuine, previously-undiscovered gap survived undetected across 3 backends and 7 prior tasks. Confirmed by reading all 3 backends' dual-texture fragment shaders directly: EasyGL's `EnsureDualTextured3DProgram()`, Vulkan's `dual_texture3d.frag.glsl`, and Bgfx's `fs_dual_texture3d.sc` all computed `texture0 * texture1 * diffuseColor` with no doubling anywhere. **Fixed** by adding `base.rgb *= 2.0` (RGB only, matching FNA exactly) to all 3 backends' dual-texture fragment shaders, then regenerating `spirv_shaders.hpp` (Vulkan) and `bgfx_shaders.hpp` (Bgfx) — confirmed via diff that only the `dual_texture3d`/`fs_dual_texture3d` byte arrays changed in each regenerated header. New pixel test (`examples/{easygl,vulkan,bgfx}_dualtextureeffect_doubling_test.cpp`, first-ever Bgfx `DualTextureEffect` pixel test) with 2 sub-cases: (a) `tex0=gray(100)`, `tex1=white`, `diffuse=default` → correct `100×2=200` vs buggy `100`, a 100-unit-of-255 separation, genuinely discriminating; (b) two white textures + `diffuse=red` → red (the literal task-title case, included for continuity but **not discriminating** for this bug since both hypotheses saturate to the same clamped result — documented explicitly in the test file rather than silently treating (b) as sufficient coverage). **3/3 PASS on all 3 backends, exact match** (`(200,200,200)`/`(255,0,0)`). **Verified genuine discriminating power**: `git stash`-reverted the EasyGL production fix and reran — sub-case (a) correctly failed with `(100,100,100)`, sub-case (b) still incorrectly passed (confirming (b) alone would have missed this bug entirely, as predicted); restored and reconfirmed 3/3. **Found and fixed one real regression** caused by correctly implementing the doubling factor: the pre-existing `EasyGL_TextureFilter_PointVsLinear`/`Vulkan_TextureFilter_PointVsLinear` test (Task 297, shared source file across both backends) used a white overlay texture and relied on the *absence* of doubling to keep its calibrated mid-range blend-detection window (`90–165`) meaningful — after the fix, a 50/50 red/green blend (`~127,127,0`) doubled to `~254,254,0`, pushing both channels near saturation and breaking the `IsBlended`/`IsPure` predicates. Fixed by adding `fx.setDiffuseColorProperty(Vector3(0.5f,0.5f,0.5f))` to the shared test source (`2×0.5=1`, exactly cancelling the doubling and restoring the test's original calibration) — mirrors Task 367's own precedent of a shader-bug fix requiring an update to a pre-existing test that "only passed *because of* the bug being fixed." Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass: EasyGL ctest 3495/3498 (3 pre-existing, +1 new test — `EasyGL_DualTextureEffect_Doubling` — plus the `EasyGL_TextureFilter_PointVsLinear` fix), Vulkan ctest 3416/3429 (13 pre-existing failures, exact-name match, no flakes this run, `Vulkan_TextureFilter_PointVsLinear` also fixed via the same shared source file), Bgfx ctest 3399/3399 (100% pass, no flakes). |
| 384 | Pixel test: magenta × yellow = red                                | ✅      | **Closed a real coverage gap: Bgfx never had this test.** EasyGL (Task 133) and Vulkan (Task 135) already cover this exact case, and EasyGL's Task 191 goes further with 4 more combinations — but no Bgfx equivalent existed anywhere before this task. Added `examples/bgfx_dual_texture_test.cpp`, mirroring Task 133/135's exact structure (`texture0=magenta(1,0,1,1)`, `texture1=yellow(1,1,0,1)`, `diffuse=default white` → `(1,0,0)` red). **Noted, deliberately not treated as new discriminating coverage**: this saturated 0/1-valued combination cannot detect the `color.rgb *= 2` doubling-factor bug Task 383 found and fixed (both `1×2` and `1×1` clamp to the same `1`) — that discriminating coverage already exists separately via Task 383's `{easygl,vulkan,bgfx}_dualtextureeffect_doubling_test.cpp` (gray×white case). This task's own scope is purely "does the basic two-texture multiply work," which it already demonstrably does per Tasks 133/135/191's existing EasyGL/Vulkan coverage — Bgfx was simply the last backend missing it. **Bug found and fixed while writing the test (not a `DualTextureEffect` bug)**: naively copied the EasyGL/Vulkan test's `device.SetDepthTestEnabled(false)` call, which **crashed** on Bgfx — `GraphicsDevice::SetDepthTestEnabled`/`SetBlend*` are unconditional-throw stubs on Bgfx (the same pre-existing, intentional, already-documented gap Task 375 found and worked around); fixed by removing the call, harmless here since `Clear()` resets the depth buffer before each single full-screen quad draw at `z=0`. Also needed the standard `RasterizerState::CullNone` workaround (Task 884) and the retry-until-non-black-frame loop, both matching `bgfx_alphatest_null_texture_test.cpp`'s established idiom. **1/1 PASS** (`(255,0,0)`, exact match). Bgfx-only change — no EasyGL/Vulkan production code or tests touched, so only the Bgfx suite was rebuilt/re-run: Bgfx ctest **3400/3400, 100% pass**, zero regressions. |
| 385 | Pixel test: alpha value affects output alpha/color as FNA expects | ✅      | **Verify-only, zero bugs — but required deliberately working around a real, unrelated, already-tracked confound (Task 868/870) to test honestly.** FNA's `DualTextureEffect::OnApply()` sets `diffuseColorParam = Vector4(diffuseColor * alpha, alpha)` — i.e. the forwarded RGB is **alpha-premultiplied** and the shader's final fragment RGB is inherently premultiplied too, which is why FNA pairs stock effects with `BlendState.AlphaBlend` (`ColorSourceBlend=One, ColorDestinationBlend=InverseSourceAlpha`) rather than a traditional non-premultiplied blend. Task 381's audit already confirmed CNA's `FillGpuDrawParams()` matches this formula exactly at the C++ level; this task adds the missing verification layers. **Layer 1 (GPU-independent, all 3 backends)**: 3 new direct unit tests in `DualTextureEffectTests.cpp` calling `FillGpuDrawParams()` and checking `diffuseColor[0..3]` for `Alpha={0.5, 1.0, 0.0}` — confirms `RGB=DiffuseColor×Alpha`, `alpha=Alpha` unscaled, and that `Alpha` never mutates the stored `DiffuseColor` property itself. **Layer 2 (real GPU pixel test, EasyGL + Bgfx)**: `DiffuseColor=red`, `Alpha=0.5`, drawn with real `BlendState.AlphaBlend` over an opaque blue background (`texture1=gray(0.5)` deliberately cancels Task 383's `*2` doubling factor to isolate this test's own variable) — correct premultiplied output is `(128,0,128)`; a hypothetical "forgot to premultiply RGB" bug would instead blend to `(255,0,128)`, a 127-unit R-channel separation. **1/1 PASS on EasyGL and Bgfx, exact match.** **Layer 2 deliberately narrowed on Vulkan**: Vulkan's `BlendState` support is already known-fake (Task 868/870 — every Vulkan pipeline hardcodes `colorBlendFactor=SRC_ALPHA/ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA` regardless of the requested `BlendState`), which would make the same full RGB-premultiplication test spuriously fail on Vulkan for a reason entirely unrelated to `DualTextureEffect` (misattributing an already-tracked bug), the same anti-pattern Tasks 377/378 avoided by not committing a test that encodes a known-confounded result as meaningful coverage. Found, however, that every one of those same hardcoded Vulkan pipelines already uses the *correct* `srcAlphaBlendFactor=ONE`/`dstAlphaBlendFactor=ZERO` for the **alpha channel** specifically (matching `BlendState.Opaque`'s real semantics) — so a narrower, still-genuine alpha-channel-only pixel test (`Alpha=0.5` over an opaque black background, `BlendState.Opaque`, reading back only `.a`) is NOT confounded by the color-channel bug and validates Vulkan's real end-to-end alpha forwarding safely. **1/1 PASS on Vulkan** (`got.a=128`). **Verified genuine discriminating power**: temporarily reverted `FillGpuDrawParams()`'s premultiplication (`p.diffuseColor[0..2] = diffuseColor_.X/Y/Z` without `*alpha_`) — both new unit tests correctly failed, and the EasyGL pixel test correctly failed with `(255,0,128)`, the exact predicted buggy value; restored and reconfirmed all green. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass: EasyGL ctest 3499/3502 (3 pre-existing, +3 unit tests +1 pixel test), Vulkan ctest 3417/3430 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes, +1 pixel test), Bgfx ctest 3401/3401 (100% pass, +1 pixel test). |
| 386 | Verify first texture null behavior                                | ✅      | **Verify-only, zero bugs, all 3 backends.** FNA's `DualTextureEffect` has no `TextureEnabled` flag (like `AlphaTestEffect`, unlike `BasicEffect`) — every shader variant unconditionally samples both `Texture` and `Texture2`; CNA's established cross-effect convention (Task 379's precedent) is to fall back to a 1×1 opaque white texture when a slot is left null. Source-read all 3 backends' dual-texture dispatch branches for the **first** texture slot specifically: **EasyGL** (`EnsureDualTextured3DProgram()`'s draw dispatch, `loc_texture` binding) already has the white-fallback else-branch; **Vulkan** (`DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx`'s `needsDualTex` branch) already does `v0 = vs0 ? vs0->GetVkImageView() : defaultWhiteView_`; **Bgfx** (`DrawPrimitivesEx`'s `params.dualTexture` branch, `texColor3DSampler_`) already has the identical else-branch — this is because **Task 379's general 7-call-site Bgfx fix already covered this exact branch**, confirmed by re-reading the code: the dual-texture branch's slot-0 handling is one of the 7 sites Task 379 touched. **All 3 backends already correct — no fix needed.** Added one pixel test per backend (`examples/{easygl,vulkan,bgfx}_dualtextureeffect_null_texture0_test.cpp`), mirroring Task 379's exact methodology: draws a real, distinctive `Texture` first (establishes "previous draw" state), then switches to `Texture=null` with a **non-saturated** `Texture2=(80,40,120)` so 3 hypotheses are numerically distinct — correct white-fallback `white(1,1,1)×2(Task 383 doubling)×Texture2 = (160,80,240)`, stale-previous-texture `(200,20,20)`, or black-fallback `(0,0,0)`. **2/2 PASS on all 3 backends, exact match** (`(160,80,240)`), confirming both "correct fallback value" and "no stale-state leak" simultaneously. No stash-revert verification performed (no fix was made — nothing to revert). **Noted, correctly deferred**: Bgfx's *second* texture slot (`texColor3DSampler2_`/`Texture2`) still has the identical unconditional-skip gap Task 379 explicitly flagged as not fixed there — squarely Task 387's scope, not this task's. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass: EasyGL ctest 3498/3502 (3 pre-existing + 1 reconfirmed-flaky `NetworkSessionTest.FindReturnsEmptyCollection`, 3/3 isolated reruns passed), Vulkan ctest 3418/3431 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes), Bgfx ctest 3402/3402 (100% pass). |
| 387 | Verify second texture null behavior                               | ✅      | **Real bug found and fixed on Bgfx — exactly the gap Task 379 predicted and deliberately left unfixed.** Task 386 had already confirmed EasyGL and Vulkan correctly fall back to white for the first texture slot; this task re-confirmed both already handle the **second** slot (`Texture2`) identically — EasyGL's `EnsureDualTextured3DProgram()` dispatch (`loc_texture2` branch) and Vulkan's `DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx` (`v1 = vs1 ? ... : defaultWhiteView_`) both already had the correct fallback, confirmed by source-reading (no fix needed on either). **Bgfx: confirmed the real bug Task 379 explicitly flagged** — `texColor3DSampler2_`'s binding only ever called `bgfx::setTexture()` when `params.texture1` was non-null (`if (params.texture1 && bgfx::isValid(...))`), with **no else branch at all**, unlike slot 0 which Task 379 already fixed. A null-`Texture2` draw silently left whatever the *previous* draw's slot 1 had bound. **Fixed** by adding an else-branch mirroring slot 0's own Task 379 fix exactly (`bgfx::setTexture(1, texColor3DSampler2_, defaultWhiteTexture3D_, samplerFlags_[1])`) — a small, single-call-site fix, unlike Task 379's 7-site scope, since this is the only remaining unconditional-skip site in the codebase. Added one pixel test per backend (`examples/{easygl,vulkan,bgfx}_dualtextureeffect_null_texture2_test.cpp`), mirroring Task 386's exact methodology with slots swapped: draws a real, distinctive `Texture2` first, then `Texture2=null` with non-saturated `Texture=(80,40,120)` — correct white-fallback `(160,80,240)`, stale-previous-texture `(20,200,20)`, or black-fallback `(0,0,0)`. **2/2 PASS on all 3 backends, exact match.** **Verified genuine discriminating power**: `git stash`-reverted the Bgfx fix and reran — failed with `(0,0,0)` (black, not literally the stale previous-draw texture — the same "surprise but still a correct failure" pattern Task 379 candidly noted for slot 0); restored and reconfirmed 2/2. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass, zero new failures despite the Bgfx production change: EasyGL ctest 3501/3504 (3 pre-existing, +1 new test), Vulkan ctest 3419/3432 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes, +1 new test), Bgfx ctest **3403/3403, 100% pass** — re-verified against the entire Bgfx suite given the fix's reach (every effect using the dual-texture dispatch path), nothing regressed. |
| 388 | Verify fog behavior in DualTextureEffect                          | ✅      | **Real bug found and fixed on EasyGL; the pre-existing project-wide Vulkan/Bgfx fog gap (Task 888) confirmed unfixable here.** Task 381's audit had already found `FillGpuDrawParams()` never forwards `FogEnabled`/`FogColor`/`FogStart`/`FogEnd` — the identical bug shape Task 378 found and fixed for `AlphaTestEffect` — and deliberately deferred it here. **Larger than Task 378's fix**: unlike `AlphaTestEffect` (which reuses a shared per-stride shader that already had fog infra), `DualTextureEffect` uses its own dedicated `EnsureDualTextured3DProgram()` shader on EasyGL, which had **no fog uniforms at all**. Fixed on both sides: added `uFogEnabled`/`uFogColor`/`uFogStart`/`uFogEnd` uniforms plus the standard `vFogFactor=clamp((FogEnd-z)/(FogEnd-FogStart),0,1)` / `mix(FogColor,original,vFogFactor)` blend to the vertex+fragment shader (mirroring `EnsureTextured3DProgram()`'s identical pattern exactly — a small, single-shader, well-contained addition, not a project-wide change), then forwarded the 4 fog fields in `FillGpuDrawParams()` (mirroring `AlphaTestEffect`'s Task 378 C++ pattern exactly). Added `easygl_dualtextureeffect_fog_test.cpp`: same 3-point Z-sweep methodology as Task 378 (`z=-0.9` unblended, `z=0.9` full fog, `z=0` half fog, proving genuine interpolation not an on/off switch), using `Texture2=gray(128)` to cancel Task 383's `*2` doubling factor (`1×2×0.502≈1.004`) so the pre-fog material color isolates cleanly to ≈`DiffuseColor`. **3/3 PASS, near-exact match** (`(205,51,102)`/`(25,153,229)`/`(115,102,166)` vs expected `(205,51,102)`/`(26,153,230)`/`(115,102,166)`, well within tolerance). **Verified genuine discriminating power**: reverted both the shader and C++ fixes (`git stash`) and reran — 2/3 assertions correctly failed (`z=0.9`/`z=0` both showed the unblended material color, proving fog was genuinely a no-op before the fix); restored and reconfirmed 3/3. **Confirmed, not fixed here** (already tracked as Task 888, opened by Task 378): Vulkan and Bgfx still have zero fog GPU implementation for any 3D effect — no Vulkan/Bgfx test added, matching Task 377/378's precedent of not committing a test that would encode a known no-op as a passing assertion. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass (production fix touches shared `DualTextureEffect.cpp`, compiled into all 3 backends, so all 3 rebuilt even though only EasyGL's shader changed): EasyGL ctest 3502/3505 (3 pre-existing, +1 new test), Vulkan ctest 3422/3435 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes), Bgfx ctest 3406/3406 (100% pass). |
| 389 | Cross-backend DualTextureEffect comparison suite                  | ✅      | **Capstone test, zero new bugs — closes out Phase 44's pixel-verification work (Tasks 383–388), mirroring Task 370's `BasicEffect` capstone precedent.** Combined the `color.rgb *= 2` doubling factor (Task 383), the two-texture multiply, and `DiffuseColor` into one scene — expected fragment = `Texture × 2 × Texture2 × DiffuseColor × Alpha`. Used, for the first time in any `DualTextureEffect` pixel test, a **real 2×2 multi-texel texture** (every prior task used a 1×1 solid color) sampled at all 4 texel centers via 4 separate draws, deliberately exercising per-vertex UV correctly reaching the right texel through the full doubling+two-texture+diffuse pipeline. `Texture2=gray(128,128,128)` deliberately cancels the doubling factor (`2×128/255≈1.004`, same compensation technique as Tasks 385/386/388) so expected values reduce to ≈`TexelColor×DiffuseColor`. **Noted, not changed** (a pre-existing, already-consistent simplification across every prior `DualTextureEffect` test in this project, not something this capstone is scoped to fix): CNA's `DualTextureEffect` shares one UV set across both texture slots (`VertexPositionTexture`, stride 20) rather than FNA's real `VSInputTx2`'s independent `TexCoord`/`TexCoord2` — harmless for this test since `Texture2` is a flat gray texture. Fog deliberately excluded from the combined assertions: Task 388 fixed it on EasyGL only, and including it here would make the shared cross-backend expected values diverge per backend (Vulkan/Bgfx still lack any fog GPU implementation, Task 888), defeating the capstone's own point of proving identical output across backends. **Result: all 3 backends produced byte-identical pixel output**, matching the derived expected formula at all 4 sample points — `(120,40,40)`/`(30,80,80)`/`(60,20,161)`/`(90,60,120)`, exact match on EasyGL, Vulkan, and Bgfx. No new bugs found — pure integration verification, passed cleanly on the first attempt thanks to Tasks 383–388's fixes already being in place. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass: EasyGL ctest 3503/3506 (3 pre-existing, +1 new test), Vulkan ctest 3423/3436 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes, +1 new test), Bgfx ctest 3407/3407 (100% pass, +1 new test). |

| 391 | Audit `EnvironmentMapEffect` properties and defaults against FNA | ✅      | **Opens Phase 45. Verify-only for this task's own scope — no bugs found**, mirroring Task 361/371/381's opener precedent. Read FNA's `Graphics/Effect/StockEffects/EnvironmentMapEffect.cs` line-by-line against CNA's `EnvironmentMapEffect.hpp`/`.cpp`. **All 14 properties/defaults match exactly**: `World`/`View`/`Projection`=Identity, `DiffuseColor`=`Vector3.One`, `EmissiveColor`=Zero, `AmbientLightColor`=Zero, `Alpha`=1, `FogEnabled`=false, `FogStart`=0, `FogEnd`=1, `FogColor`=Zero, `Texture`/`EnvironmentMap`=null, `EnvironmentMapAmount`=1 (constructor-assigned, matches FNA's own constructor-assigned default exactly), `EnvironmentMapSpecular`=Zero, `FresnelFactor`=1 (constructor-assigned) — **including the subtle detail that `fresnelEnabled_` defaults to `true` post-construction** (FNA's class-field default is `false`, but the constructor's own `FresnelFactor = 1;` call flips it true via the property setter's own enabled-tracking side effect; CNA's `fresnelEnabled_ = true` class-member default already encodes this correctly, confirmed by tracing FNA's constructor call order literal-for-literal — a detail easy to get wrong that wasn't). `DirectionalLight0.Enabled` is correctly set `true` in the constructor (matching FNA exactly, same pattern as `BasicEffect`'s Task 361 finding); `DirectionalLight1`/`DirectionalLight2` correctly remain at their class defaults (disabled). Confirmed `IEffectLights.LightingEnabled`'s explicit-interface always-true getter and throw-on-`false` setter (FNA: `NotSupportedException`; CNA: `std::runtime_error`, matching the project's established exception-mapping convention) — `EnvironmentMapEffect` is the first effect audited in this project with a hard-locked lighting mode. `EnableDefaultLighting()`'s exact key/fill/back-light and ambient constants cross-checked directly against `EffectHelpers.EnableDefaultLighting()` — byte-for-byte identical float literals (same constants Task 363 already confirmed for `BasicEffect`, since both effects call the identical shared FNA helper). `OnApply()`'s `SetWorldViewProjAndFog`/`SetLightingMatrices`/`SetMaterialColor` logic (all 3 shared `EffectHelpers` calls in FNA, inlined in CNA per the established Task 351 architecture) matches literal-for-literal, including `SetLightingMatrices`'s two independently-gated `DirtyWorld`/`DirtyEyePosition` blocks and `SetMaterialColor`'s lighting-enabled-branch emissive+ambient*diffuse pre-combination formula. `Clone()`'s field-copy list matches FNA's *effective* clone behavior (FNA's C# clone constructor body doesn't explicitly copy `environmentMapAmount`/`environmentMapSpecular`/`fresnelFactor`, relying instead on its native MojoShader-level effect-data clone to carry those parameter values across; CNA has no such native clone — Task 883's already-tracked gap — so its clone constructor correctly compensates by explicitly copying all 3 values itself, achieving the same net effect through a different but equivalent mechanism, not a bug). **Real, confirmed, deliberately-deferred finding, same shape as Task 885's `BasicEffect` gap**: `FillGpuDrawParams()` only forwards `DirectionalLight0` — confirmed via `EnvironmentMapEffect.fx` that this effect shares `BasicEffect`'s identical `Lighting.fxh`/`ComputeLights(eyeVector,worldNormal,numLights)` mechanism (same `oneLight` shader-variant optimization, same up-to-3-light support in real FNA), meaning `DirectionalLight1`/`DirectionalLight2` are silently ignored by CNA's GPU dispatch today, exactly like the already-tracked `BasicEffect` gap. **Opened as new Task 890** rather than folded into Task 885, since it's a distinct effect class with its own `FillGpuDrawParams()`/shader dispatch, though the fix shapes are closely related (same `GpuDrawParams` light1/2 fields, likely the same Vulkan push-constant expansion) — noted as a candidate to tackle alongside Task 885. **Zero existing test coverage found** (no `EnvironmentMapEffectTests.cpp` at all, though pre-existing pixel-integration tests exist from earlier phases: `easygl_env_map_test.cpp`/`vulkan_env_map_test.cpp`/`bgfx_env_map_test.cpp`) — Task 392's job. `GetTypeName()` already correct (`"Microsoft.Xna.Framework.Graphics.EnvironmentMapEffect"`). Doxygen coverage on every public member already complete. No production code changed, no rebuild needed (pure source audit, matching Task 340/361/371/381's "no code changed, no regression risk" precedent). |
| 392 | Unit test default values for all properties                      | ✅      | **Real bug found and fixed, affecting 4 stock effects (`AlphaTestEffect`, `DualTextureEffect`, `EnvironmentMapEffect`, `SkinnedEffect`), not just `EnvironmentMapEffect`.** Task 391 found zero existing test coverage for `EnvironmentMapEffect` property defaults — wrote new `tests/Microsoft/Xna/Framework/Graphics/EnvironmentMapEffectTests.cpp` from scratch, mirroring Task 372/382's fixture/style exactly. 42 tests: all 14 property defaults (`World`/`View`/`Projection`=Identity, `DiffuseColor`=One, `EmissiveColor`=Zero, `AmbientLightColor`=Zero, `Alpha`=1, `LightingEnabled`=true-locked, `DirectionalLight0.Enabled`=true/`Light1`/`Light2`=false, `FogEnabled`=false, `FogStart`=0, `FogEnd`=1, `FogColor`=Zero, `Texture`/`EnvironmentMap`=null, `EnvironmentMapAmount`=1, `EnvironmentMapSpecular`=Zero, `FresnelFactor`=1 — all reconfirmed matching Task 391's audit, no new default-value bugs), `LightingEnabled`'s throw-on-`false`/no-throw-on-`true` behavior, `EnableDefaultLighting()`'s ambient-color and all-3-lights-enabled effects, a round-trip setter test per property, and a `Clone()` test. **The `Clone()` test caught a real, previously-undiscovered bug**: `EnvironmentMapEffect`'s copy constructor never copied `FogColor`'s actual value — `CacheEffectParameters()` re-links `fogColorParam_` to a **fresh, zero-valued** `FogColor` parameter in the clone's own `Parameters` collection, and nothing in the copy constructor body ever transfers the source's current `FogColor` value into it, silently resetting `FogColor` to black on every clone. Traced why this survived Tasks 372/382 undetected: their own `CloneCopiesAllProperties` tests set `FogEnabled`/`FogStart`/`FogEnd` but never `FogColor` itself, so the gap was never exercised. **Confirmed via source-reading that the identical bug shape exists in `AlphaTestEffect`, `DualTextureEffect`, and `SkinnedEffect`** (all 4 effects that both implement `IEffectFog` via an `EffectParameter`-backed `FogColor` *and* have a working copy constructor — `BasicEffect` doesn't have `Clone()`/a copy constructor implemented at all yet, tracked separately as Task 883). **Fixed all 4** by adding `if (fogColorParam_) fogColorParam_->SetValue(src.getFogColorProperty());` to each copy constructor (a one-line, well-contained fix per file, following the exact same call-`getFogColorProperty()`-on-`src`-first pattern already used for the other explicitly-copied fields). **Extended Task 372/382's existing `AlphaTestEffectTests.cpp`/`DualTextureEffectTests.cpp` `CloneCopiesAllProperties` tests** to also set and check `FogColor`, closing the exact gap that let this bug go undetected — no new test file needed for `SkinnedEffect` (no `SkinnedEffectTests.cpp` exists yet; that belongs to Phase 46, `SkinnedEffect`'s own dedicated phase) but the production fix was still applied there since the bug is real and the fix is trivially safe/correct regardless of when its own dedicated tests land. **Verified genuine discriminating power**: `git stash`-reverted all 4 production fixes and reran all 3 `CloneCopiesAllProperties` tests — all 3 correctly failed with `FogColor` reset to `(0,0,0)`; restored and reconfirmed all green. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass (production fix touches 4 shared `.cpp` files, compiled into all 3 backends): EasyGL ctest 3545/3548 (3 pre-existing, +42 new tests), Vulkan ctest 3465/3478 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes), Bgfx ctest 3448/3449 (1 reconfirmed-flaky `NetworkSessionTest.FindReturnsEmptyCollection`, 3/3 isolated reruns passed — network-timing flake, unrelated to graphics changes). |
| 393 | Pixel test with `EnvironmentMapAmount=0`                         | ✅      | **Verify-only, zero bugs in this task's own scope — but surfaced an important formula-level finding flagged for Task 394.** Read FNA's `EnvironmentMapEffect.fx`'s `PSEnvMap` pixel shader directly: `color.rgb = SAMPLE_TEXTURE(...)×pin.Diffuse; envmap = SAMPLE_CUBEMAP(...)×color.a; color.rgb = lerp(color.rgb, envmap.rgb, pin.Specular.rgb)` where `pin.Specular.rgb` is `EnvironmentMapAmount` (or a per-pixel Fresnel-weighted variant of it). This is a **lerp** (interpolate/replace), not an additive blend. Cross-checked against CNA's actual EasyGL/Vulkan shader formula (confirmed via Task 134/136's own documented derivation): `rgb = litRGB×texColor.rgb + envColor×uEnvMapAmount + uEnvMapSpecular` — a pure **additive** blend. **At `EnvironmentMapAmount=0` specifically, both formulas coincide exactly** (FNA's lerp reduces to the unweighted lit/textured color; CNA's additive term is simply zeroed) — meaning this task's own literal scope cannot discriminate between the two formulas, and both predict "cube map fully ignored." Added one pixel test per backend, using a highly saturated, distinctive pure-green cube map (unlike the base texture's color) so any nonzero leak would be immediately visible: `Texture=(200,100,50)`, `EmissiveColor=(0.5,0.5,0.5)`, `DiffuseColor` left at its default `(1,1,1)` (deliberately, to avoid a second, unrelated, not-yet-investigated question — whether `uDiffuseColor.rgb` double-multiplies into the already-diffuse-scaled ambient term baked into `uEmissiveColor` — from confounding this test's expected-value derivation), `EnvironmentMapAmount=0`, `EnvironmentMapSpecular=Zero`. Expected `litRGB×texColor = (0.5,0.5,0.5)×(200,100,50)/255 = (100,50,25)`. **1/1 PASS on all 3 backends, exact match.** **Real, previously-undiscovered formula discrepancy flagged for Task 394** (not fixed or separately tracked here, since Task 394 — "Pixel test with `EnvironmentMapAmount=1` and white cubemap" — is exactly where the two formulas diverge sharply and is already the next scheduled task): at `EnvironmentMapAmount=1`, FNA's lerp **fully replaces** the lit/textured color with the cube map (`lerp(x,y,1)=y`), while CNA's additive formula would instead **add** the cube map on top of the still-present lit/textured color (`x+y`) — a real, testable difference the moment the lit/textured term is nonzero. The pre-existing Task 134/192/136/278 tests never caught this because they always set the lit/textured contribution to exactly zero in their env-map-amount=1 sub-cases, making the two formulas' outputs coincidentally identical there too. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass: EasyGL ctest 3546/3549 (3 pre-existing, +1 new test), Vulkan ctest 3466/3479 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes, +1 new test), Bgfx ctest 3450/3450 (100% pass, +1 new test — using real `GetBackBufferData()` pixel verification, not Task 278's older smoke-test-only approach, since Bgfx readback has worked reliably since at least Task 379). |
| 394 | Pixel test with `EnvironmentMapAmount=1` and white cubemap       | ✅      | **Real, confirmed formula bug found and fixed on all 3 backends — exactly the discrepancy Task 393 flagged.** FNA's real `PSEnvMap` pixel shader **lerps** between the lit/textured color and the cube map: `color.rgb = lerp(color.rgb, envmap.rgb, EnvironmentMapAmount)` — at `Amount=1` this **fully replaces** the lit color with the cube map. CNA's actual shader formula on all 3 backends computed `rgb = litRGB×texColor.rgb + envColor×Amount + specular` — a pure **additive** blend, not an interpolation. **The literal task title's "white cubemap" case cannot discriminate this** (both formulas saturate to `(255,255,255)` identically — the same 0/1-saturation blind spot Task 383 already taught this project to watch for), so a second sub-case using a deliberately non-saturated **gray cubemap `(128,128,128)`** combined with a genuinely nonzero lit/textured contribution (`litRGB×texColor=(100,50,25)`, same setup as Task 393) was added: FNA's lerp predicts `(128,128,128)` (fully replaced); CNA's additive formula predicts `(100,50,25)+(128,128,128)=(228,178,153)` (added on top) — unambiguously different. **Empirically confirmed CNA's actual pre-fix output was `(228,178,153)`, exact match to the additive-formula prediction** — every pre-existing env-map test (Tasks 134/192/136/278) always zeroed the lit/textured contribution in their own `Amount=1` sub-cases, coincidentally hiding this bug since `0+cubemap` and `lerp(0,cubemap,1)=cubemap` produce identical results. **Fixed** on all 3 backends by changing the additive term to a proper `mix()`/`lerp` (`vec3 baseColor=litRGB*texColor.rgb; vec3 rgb=mix(baseColor,envColor,Amount)+specular;`) — EasyGL's `EnsureEnvMapped3DProgram()` fragment shader, Vulkan's `env_map3d.frag.glsl`, and Bgfx's `fs_env_map3d.sc`, then regenerated `spirv_shaders.hpp`/`bgfx_shaders.hpp` (confirmed via diff that only the `env_map3d`/`fs_env_map3d` byte arrays changed). **Deliberately not touched in this task** (a separate, narrower, not-yet-broken concern): `EnvironmentMapSpecular`'s real FNA formula multiplies by the cube map's **alpha** channel (`+= EnvironmentMapSpecular * envmap.a`), while CNA currently treats it as a flat additive constant ignoring cube-map alpha entirely — every existing test's cube maps happened to use `alpha=255` throughout, so this specific sub-formula was never exercised with a non-1 alpha; left for Task 395 (`EnvironmentMapSpecular`'s own dedicated task) to verify/fix specifically. **Also deliberately not touched**: FNA's default-enabled Fresnel edge-weighting (`FresnelFactor=1` by default multiplies the blend factor by a per-pixel `pow(1-|viewAngle|,FresnelFactor)` term) is not implemented at all in CNA's shader (confirmed: no Fresnel uniform exists in any of the 3 env-map shaders) — this doesn't affect this task's own `Amount=1` exact-replacement assertion (`lerp(x,y,1)=y` regardless of any Fresnel weighting, since Fresnel only matters for values strictly between 0 and 1), but is a real, larger gap explicitly already scoped as Task 396's own job ("Verify `FresnelFactor` if present"). **2/2 PASS on all 3 backends, exact match** (`(255,255,255)`/`(128,128,128)`). **Verified genuine discriminating power**: `git stash`-reverted all 3 shader fixes and reran — all 3 backends correctly failed with the exact predicted `(228,178,153)` pre-fix value; restored and reconfirmed 2/2 on all 3. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass, zero new failures despite the shared-shader-formula change (affects every `EnvironmentMapEffect` draw with `EnvironmentMapAmount` strictly between 0 and 1, or exactly 1, on a non-degenerate cube map): EasyGL ctest 3547/3550 (3 pre-existing, +1 new test, all pre-existing env-map tests unaffected since they used saturated/degenerate values), Vulkan ctest 3467/3480 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes, +1 new test), Bgfx ctest 3451/3451 (100% pass, +1 new test). |
| 395 | Pixel test for `EnvironmentMapSpecular`                          | ✅      | **Real, confirmed formula bug found and fixed on all 3 backends — exactly the gap Task 394 flagged and deferred.** FNA's real `PSEnvMapSpecular` pixel shader does `envmap = SAMPLE_CUBEMAP(EnvironmentMap, pin.EnvCoord) * color.a; color.rgb = lerp(color.rgb, envmap.rgb, pin.Specular.rgb); color.rgb += EnvironmentMapSpecular * envmap.a;` — the specular term is scaled by `envmap.a`, i.e. **the cube map's own alpha channel further scaled by the combined texture×diffuse alpha** (`color.a = texColor.a * DiffuseColor.a`). CNA's actual shader formula on all 3 backends instead added `EnvironmentMapSpecular` as a **flat, unscaled constant**, never reading the cube map's alpha channel at all (`.rgb`/`.xyz` swizzles discarded it outright). Designed the test with `EnvironmentMapAmount=0` (isolating the specular term from the still-open, deliberately-deferred `envmap.rgb×color.a` nuance opened below as Task 891 — at `Amount=0` the base lerp reduces to `baseColor` regardless of that nuance) and opaque texture/diffuse (`texColor.a=DiffuseColor.a=1`, so `combinedAlpha=1`, isolating purely to the cube map's own alpha channel): (a) opaque cubemap (`alpha=255`) — **not discriminating** (scaling by 1.0 changes nothing, both formulas agree), included as a sanity check only, matching the same "saturated value can't discriminate" lesson from Tasks 383/394; (b) translucent cubemap (`alpha=128`), deliberately non-saturated — **discriminating**: `baseColor=(100,50,25)` (same setup as Tasks 393/394), FNA's alpha-scaled formula predicts `(100,50,25)+(0.4,0.4,0.4)×(128/255)≈(151,101,76)`, CNA's flat-additive formula predicts `(100,50,25)+(0.4,0.4,0.4)×255=(202,152,127)` — unambiguously different. **Empirically confirmed CNA's actual pre-fix EasyGL output was exactly `(202,152,127)` in both sub-cases** (`git stash`-reverted the EasyGL fix and reran: case (a) still passed at `(202,152,127)`, case (b) failed at `(202,152,127)` instead of the expected `(151,101,76)`, an exact match to the flat-additive-formula prediction), confirming the bug empirically before committing to any fix. **Fixed** on all 3 backends by sampling the cube map's full `vec4` (not just `.rgb`/`.xyz`) and scaling the specular term by `envSample.a × combinedAlpha`: EasyGL's `EnsureEnvMapped3DProgram()` fragment shader (`vec4 envSample=texture(uEnvMap,reflDir); float combinedAlpha=uDiffuseColor.a*texColor.a; vec3 rgb=mix(baseColor,envSample.rgb,uEnvMapAmount)+uEnvMapSpecular*envSample.a*combinedAlpha;`), Vulkan's `env_map3d.frag.glsl` (same shape, `ep.envMapSpec_pad.xyz * envSample.a * combinedAlpha`), and Bgfx's `fs_env_map3d.sc` (`u_envMapSpecular.xyz * envSample.w * combinedAlpha`), then regenerated `spirv_shaders.hpp` (via `compile_shaders.py`, no arguments needed — self-contained `ctypes`/`libshaderc` script, unlike Bgfx's path-sensitive script) and `bgfx_shaders.hpp` (via `compile_shaders.py <shaderc> <bgfx-src>/src`, using the exact same correct include path Task 394 already established) — confirmed via diff both times that only the `EnvMap3d`/`fs_env_map3d` byte arrays changed, nothing else. **Deliberately left unfixed and newly opened as Task 891**: FNA's `envmap = SAMPLE_CUBEMAP(...) * color.a` scales **both** `envmap.rgb` (used in the base lerp) and `envmap.a` (used for the specular term) by `color.a` — this task only fixed the specular half; the base lerp's `envColor`/`envSample.rgb` term is still used unscaled by `combinedAlpha` in all 3 backends' `mix()` calls, a real but separate, narrower nuance that only matters when `texColor.a × DiffuseColor.a` is not 1 (every existing env-map test, including this one, deliberately used opaque textures/diffuse colors to isolate the specular fix cleanly, so this nuance remains completely unexercised by any current test). **2/2 PASS on all 3 backends, exact match** (`(202,152,127)`/`(151,101,76)`). **Verified genuine discriminating power**: `git stash`-reverted the EasyGL shader fix and reran — case (b) correctly failed with the exact predicted flat-additive value `(202,152,127)`; restored and reconfirmed 2/2. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass, zero new failures (change only affects `EnvironmentMapEffect` draws with a nonzero `EnvironmentMapSpecular` on a cube map with alpha strictly less than 255): EasyGL ctest 3548/3551 (3 pre-existing, exact-name match, +1 new test), Vulkan ctest 3468/3481 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes, +1 new test), Bgfx ctest 3452/3452 (100% pass, +1 new test). |
| 396 | Verify `FresnelFactor` if present                                | ✅      | **Real, confirmed missing-feature gap found and fixed on all 3 backends — exactly the gap Task 394 flagged.** FNA's real vertex shader computes the env-map blend factor via `ComputeFresnelFactor(eyeVector,worldNormal) = pow(max(1-abs(dot(eyeVector,worldNormal)),0),FresnelFactor) * EnvironmentMapAmount` when Fresnel is enabled (the default, since `FresnelFactor=1` is set in the constructor — confirmed by Task 391's audit), replacing the flat `EnvironmentMapAmount` used when disabled. Tasks 393/394 already confirmed CNA implemented **no Fresnel uniform at all** in any of the 3 backends' env-map shaders — the blend factor was always the flat `EnvironmentMapAmount` regardless of view angle. Added 2 new `GpuDrawParams` fields (`fresnelEnabled`, `fresnelFactor`), forwarded from `EnvironmentMapEffect::FillGpuDrawParams()`, and threaded a `viewAngle=dot(E,N)` / `pow(max(1-abs(viewAngle),0),FresnelFactor)*Amount` per-pixel term into all 3 backends' fragment shaders (computed per-pixel rather than FNA's per-vertex, using the eye/normal vectors already available from Tasks 383/394's reflection-vector math — an acceptable CNA-vs-FNA precision improvement, not a behavioral divergence, since both reduce to the identical formula on flat, single-normal test geometry with no interpolation). Vulkan/Bgfx repurposed 2 previously-unused padding floats each (`light0Diff_pad.w`→`fresnelEnabled`, `envMapSpec_pad.w`→`fresnelFactor` on Vulkan; `u_envMapAmount.y`→`fresnelEnabled`, `u_envMapSpecular.w`→`fresnelFactor` on Bgfx) rather than growing the UBO/uniform count, avoiding any push-constant-budget concerns. **Test design**: (a) reused Tasks 393-395's own grazing/coplanar camera setup (`View=Identity`, eye and quad in the same plane) as a **non-discriminating sanity check** — `viewAngle=dot(eyeVector,normal)≈0` there (eyeVector has no Z component while the normal is pure Z), so `pow(max(1-0,0),F)=1` and the Fresnel-weighted formula reduces to exactly the flat `EnvironmentMapAmount`, matching every prior task's already-passing `(128,128,128)` result — confirms the fix didn't regress any earlier task's degenerate-camera assumption. (b) a genuinely new **head-on perspective camera** (`View=CreateLookAt((0,0,3),Zero,(0,1,0))`, `Projection=CreatePerspectiveFieldOfView(π/4,1,0.1,100)`, quad still at `World=Identity`) where, at the screen center, `eyeVector` is exactly parallel to the surface normal (`viewAngle≈1`), making `pow(max(1-1,0),F)=pow(0,F)=0` for any `F>0` — the Fresnel-weighted formula **fully suppresses** the cube map at normal incidence, the physically-correct behavior (reflections weakest head-on, strongest at grazing angles). This is genuinely discriminating: FNA's correct Fresnel-weighted result is `baseColor≈(100,50,25)` (cube map suppressed), while CNA's pre-fix flat-`Amount` formula gives `(128,128,128)` (cube map fully applied, ignoring view angle) — unambiguously different. **Empirically confirmed CNA's actual pre-fix EasyGL output was exactly `(128,128,128)` in case (b)** (`git stash`-reverted the fix and reran: case (a) still passed unchanged, case (b) failed at `(128,128,128)` instead of the expected `(100,50,25)`, an exact match to the missing-Fresnel prediction), confirming the gap empirically before committing to the fix. **2/2 PASS on all 3 backends, exact match** (`(128,128,128)`/`(100,50,25)`). **Verified genuine discriminating power**: `git stash`-reverted the shared production fix (`IGraphicsBackend.hpp`, `EnvironmentMapEffect.cpp`) and reran on EasyGL — case (b) correctly failed with the exact predicted flat-`Amount` value `(128,128,128)`; restored and reconfirmed 2/2 (mirrors Task 395's single-backend stash-revert methodology, since the shared C++ layer is what all 3 backends' shaders read from). Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass, zero new failures (the Fresnel term only changes output when `viewAngle` deviates from the `~0` value every pre-existing test happened to use, and is additionally gated behind `fresnelEnabled`, which defaults to matching FNA's real default so no test needed updating): EasyGL ctest 3549/3552 (3 pre-existing, exact-name match, +1 new test), Vulkan ctest 3469/3482 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes, +1 new test), Bgfx ctest 3453/3453 (100% pass, +1 new test). |
| 397 | Verify eye position affects reflection vector                    | ✅      | **Verify-only, zero bugs in its own scope** — `EyePosition` (derived from `Matrix::Invert(view_).Translation`) already correctly drives `reflDir = reflect(-eyeVector, worldNormal)` on all 3 backends. Every prior env-map test in this phase (393-396) used **solid-color cube maps**, which cannot detect a wrong reflection vector at all — every face samples the same value regardless of which face is actually hit, a real testing gap that this task's own title exists to close. Built a cube map with a **distinct color per face** (`PositiveX`=red, `NegativeX`=cyan, `PositiveY`=green, `NegativeY`=magenta, `PositiveZ`=blue, `NegativeZ`=yellow) and 2 genuinely different camera positions, using `Matrix::CreateLookAt(eye, Vector3.Zero, up)` so the quad's centre always projects to the screen's centre regardless of how oblique `eye` is (the `target` parameter stays fixed at the origin in both cases). Set `EnvironmentMapAmount=1`, `FresnelFactor=0` (explicitly disabling Task 396's newly-added Fresnel edge-weighting, whose default-enabled behavior would otherwise suppress the env map's contribution at the near-normal-incidence camera angle used in case (a), confounding this test's own math) and `EmissiveColor=Zero` with no lights enabled (zeroing the lit/textured `baseColor` term entirely, so the output is exactly the sampled cube face color with zero other confound). (a) Eye straight on at `(0,0,3)`: `eyeVector=(0,0,1)` exactly equals the quad's own normal, so `reflDir = reflect((0,0,-1),(0,0,1)) = (0,0,1)` exactly — dominant `+Z` — expect `PositiveZ`'s blue. (b) Eye off-axis at `(5,0,0.5)`: `eyeVector≈(0.995,0,0.0995)`, `reflDir≈(-0.995,0,0.0995)` — dominant `-X` by roughly a 10× margin over the residual `Z` component, cleanly clear of any face-boundary ambiguity — expect `NegativeX`'s cyan, unambiguously different from case (a)'s result. **2/2 PASS on all 3 backends, exact match** (`(0,0,255)`/`(0,255,255)`, both well within the standard tolerance-20 comparison). Discriminating power confirmed by construction rather than a `git stash` revert (no code was changed in this task): had `EyePosition` not actually been wired into the reflection-vector math (e.g. hardcoded, or the view matrix's inverse never taken), both camera positions would have produced the *same* degenerate reflection vector and therefore the *same* sampled face — the fact that (a) and (b) produce 2 clearly different, exactly-predicted face colors is itself proof the wiring works correctly end-to-end on all 3 backends. No production code changed, no regression risk (pure new test, matching Task 340/361/371/381/386/391/393's "verify-only" precedent). Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass: EasyGL ctest 3550/3553 (3 pre-existing, exact-name match, +1 new test), Vulkan ctest 3470/3483 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes, +1 new test), Bgfx ctest 3454/3454 (100% pass, +1 new test). |
| 398 | Verify normal matrix/world transform correctness                 | ✅      | **Real, confirmed formula bug found and fixed on 2 of 3 backends.** XNA content ships `WorldInverseTranspose` for exactly this reason: transforming a normal by `World` directly is only correct when `World` is a pure rotation/uniform-scale/translation; under **non-uniform scale** the correct normal transform is `transpose(inverse(World3x3))`. Audited all 3 backends' actual normal-transform code: **EasyGL** computed the "normal matrix" as the raw upper-left 3x3 of `World` on the CPU side (`BindDrawParams()`, shared infra also used by `BasicEffect`'s lit-textured pipeline) — **WRONG** under non-uniform scale. **Vulkan** already computes `transpose(inverse(mat3(world)))` directly in `env_map3d.vert.glsl` — **already CORRECT**, confirmed by inspection, no fix needed. **Bgfx** transformed the normal via `mul(u_world, vec4(a_normal,0.0))` directly in `vs_env_map3d.sc` — **WRONG**, same shape as EasyGL's bug. Notably, `EnvironmentMapEffect::OnApply()` **already computes the correct `WorldInverseTranspose`** and stores it in its own `EffectParameter` (for API/content-pipeline fidelity, confirmed during Task 391's audit) — but this correct value was never forwarded into the actual GPU dispatch path on either buggy backend, which each independently (and incorrectly) derived their own "normal matrix" from the raw `World` matrix. **Test design**: assigned every vertex a synthetic, non-axis-aligned normal `(0,1,1)/√2` (decoupled from the quad's actual flat z=0 geometry — the shader has no way to validate a supplied normal is geometrically consistent with its triangle, a valid way to isolate the normal-transform math in isolation) and applied `World = CreateScale(1,1,20)` — a large non-uniform scale along Z, the very axis this normal has a large component along (the scale factor is deliberately large, not a modest 4x, so the resulting reflection vectors land deep inside their respective cube faces at a ~10:1 dominant-to-secondary ratio rather than near a face edge; an early attempt with a milder 4x scale hit a real, previously-undocumented cube-map-seamless-filtering artifact — with only a 1x1-texel-per-face test cube map, a reflection vector landing near a face boundary gets bilinearly blended across 2-3 adjacent faces, reading back neither pure expected color, a genuinely new discovered testing pitfall worth remembering for any future coarse-cubemap pixel test). With the camera looking straight on from `(0,0,3)`: **correct** (inverse-transpose) transform gives a Y-dominant normal → reflection vector dominant `NegativeZ` (yellow); **buggy** (raw multiply) transform gives a Z-dominant normal with the *opposite* sign relationship → reflection vector dominant `PositiveZ` (blue) — unambiguously different. **Empirically confirmed CNA's actual pre-fix output was exactly the predicted buggy blue `(1,12,242)`/`(1,12,242)` on both EasyGL and Bgfx** (`git stash`-reverted each fix independently and reran). **Fixed** on both backends by computing the correct `transpose(inverse(World3x3))` via the standard cofactor/determinant shortcut (`normalMatrix = cofactor(World3x3)/det(World3x3)`, mathematically equivalent and avoiding an explicit 3x3 inverse) on the CPU side: EasyGL's `BindDrawParams()` (shared code — this fix also correctly improves `BasicEffect`'s lit-textured pipeline on EasyGL, though no regression was possible since no existing `BasicEffect` test uses non-uniform-scale `World`), and Bgfx's `BgfxGraphicsBackend.cpp` (new `ComputeNormalMatrix3x3()` helper) + a new `u_normalMatrix` `Mat3` uniform threaded into `vs_env_map3d.sc`, then regenerated `bgfx_shaders.hpp` (confirmed via diff that only the `vs_env_map3d_*` byte arrays changed). **Real, confirmed, deliberately out-of-scope finding opened as new Task 892**: while checking Bgfx's sibling `vs_lit_textured3d.sc` (`BasicEffect`'s lit-textured shader) for the same issue, found it has a **materially worse** bug — it transforms the normal by the full `u_wvp` (World×View×Projection) matrix, not even `World` alone, which is geometrically meaningless for a direction vector; confirmed this has been completely invisible in every existing `BasicEffect` Bgfx test (367-370) since all of them leave `View`/`Projection` at `Identity`, making `u_wvp` collapse to `World` alone (still wrong, but happens to reduce to something closer to identity-safe for those specific Identity-heavy tests) — not fixed here since it's a different effect class, out of Phase 45's `EnvironmentMapEffect`-specific scope. **1/1 PASS on all 3 backends, exact match** (`(255,255,0)`). **Verified genuine discriminating power on both fixed backends independently**: `git stash`-reverted EasyGL's fix and reran — failed with the exact predicted buggy blue value; restored, reconfirmed pass; repeated identically for Bgfx. Vulkan required no fix and passed unconditionally (verify-only for that backend). Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass, zero new failures (shared-code fix touches `BasicEffect`'s lit pipeline too on EasyGL, but no existing test exercises non-uniform-scale `World` there): EasyGL ctest 3551/3554 (3 pre-existing, exact-name match, +1 new test), Vulkan ctest 3471/3484 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes, +1 new test), Bgfx ctest 3455/3455 (100% pass, +1 new test). |
| 399 | Cross-backend EnvironmentMapEffect comparison suite              | ✅      | **Capstone test combining everything Tasks 393–398 verified individually into one scene** (mirroring Task 370/389's precedent for `BasicEffect`/`DualTextureEffect`), to prove the fixes compose correctly together rather than just in isolation. Scene deliberately combines: Task 394's lerp-based blend formula (exercised via `mix()` with a tiny nonzero blend factor rather than a hardcoded 0/1 special case); Task 395's `EnvironmentMapSpecular × envmap.a × combinedAlpha` formula (a translucent, `alpha=128` cube map); Task 396's Fresnel edge-weighting (`FresnelFactor` left at its real default of `1`) — combined with a head-on camera angle, this suppresses the base cube-map blend almost entirely (`blendFactor≈0.00004`, hand-verified negligible accounting for the actual pixel-center sample offset — shifts the final result by well under `0.01` per channel), isolating the env-map contribution to almost exactly the specular term alone, a genuinely different code path than Task 395's own `Amount=0` isolation technique, landing on the same easily-verified numeric target; Task 397's `EyePosition`-driven reflection math, exercised via a real (non-`Identity`) `Matrix::CreateLookAt`/`CreatePerspectiveFieldOfView` camera; and Task 398's now-fixed normal transform, exercised via a non-identity, non-uniform-scale `World = CreateScale(2,1,1)` — deliberately scaling only X (not Z) so the quad's own Z-aligned normal is unaffected by the correct-vs-buggy normal-matrix distinction either way (Task 398's own dedicated test already covers that specific discrimination; this task's job is confirming the rest of the pipeline still works correctly with a genuinely non-identity `World` in play, not re-litigating the normal-matrix fix itself). **Expected value re-derived independently**: `baseColor = EmissiveColor×Texture = (0.5,0.5,0.5)×(200,100,50) = (100,50,25)`; `combinedAlpha = texColor.a×DiffuseColor.a = 1`; `blendFactor≈0` (Fresnel-suppressed); `rgb = mix(baseColor, envColor, blendFactor≈0) + EnvironmentMapSpecular×envmap.a×combinedAlpha ≈ (100,50,25) + (0.4,0.4,0.4)×(128/255) ≈ (151,101,76)` — reusing Task 395's own exact numeric target, now reached via the Fresnel-suppression code path instead of Task 395's `Amount=0` isolation, a meaningfully different exercise of the same value. **Result: all 3 backends produced the exact predicted `(151,101,76)` on the first attempt** — EasyGL, Vulkan, and Bgfx alike — genuine cross-backend consistency, confirmed empirically. **No new bugs found** — pure integration verification; a clean pass on the first attempt is itself a meaningful confirmation that Tasks 394–398's fixes don't just work in narrow isolation but compose correctly when combined in one scene. Added one test per backend (`examples/{easygl,vulkan,bgfx}_environmentmapeffect_combined_test.cpp`, registered as `{EasyGL,Vulkan,Bgfx}_EnvironmentMapEffect_Combined`), matching Task 370/389's exact per-backend-file convention rather than a single cross-file image-diff binary. Bgfx test needs the same `RasterizerState::CullNone` workaround as every prior env-map test in this phase (Task 884, untouched here). Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass: EasyGL ctest 3551/3555 (3 documented pre-existing failures + 1 reconfirmed-flaky `CueTest.PlayCalledTwiceWhileAlreadyPlayingIsANoOpAndDoesNotDuplicateInstances`, an audio-timing test unrelated to this session's graphics-only changes — reran in isolation 3× and passed 3/3, confirming genuine pre-existing flakiness, not a regression), Vulkan ctest 3472/3485 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes, +1 new test), Bgfx ctest 3456/3456 (100% pass, +1 new test). |
| 400 | Document EnvironmentMapEffect backend parity                     | ✅      | **Closes Phase 45.** Wrote `docs/environmentmapeffect-support.md`, synthesizing Tasks 391–399 (mirrors `docs/basiceffect-support.md`/`docs/alphatesteffect-support.md`/`docs/dualtextureeffect-support.md`'s established style): per-task summary sections, a full 3-backend support matrix (property defaults, `Clone()`'s `FogColor` fix, cube-map lerp-vs-additive blend formula, `EnvironmentMapSpecular`'s alpha scaling, base-lerp `envColor` alpha scaling gap, Fresnel edge-weighting, `EyePosition`/reflection-vector correctness, `World`-transform/normal-matrix correctness, `DirectionalLight1`/`2` forwarding gap, cross-backend consistency), and an "Open, tracked follow-up work" section listing Task 890 (`DirectionalLight1`/`2` unforwarded, opened by Task 391), Task 891 (base-lerp `envColor` not alpha-scaled, opened by Task 395), and Task 892 (`BasicEffect`'s worse Bgfx normal-matrix bug, found while fixing Task 398). No production code or tests changed — pure documentation task, matching Task 380/390's precedent. |
