## Phase 46 — SkinnedEffect exactness

| #   | Task                                                                                | Status | Notes                       |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | --------------------------- |
| 401 | Audit `SkinnedEffect` properties, bone limit, and defaults against FNA              | ✅      | **Opens Phase 46.** Read FNA's `Graphics/Effect/StockEffects/SkinnedEffect.cs` line-by-line against CNA's `SkinnedEffect.hpp`/`.cpp`, mirroring Task 361/371/381/391's opener precedent. **All property defaults match exactly**: `World`/`View`/`Projection`=Identity, `DiffuseColor`=`Vector3.One`, `EmissiveColor`/`AmbientLightColor`=Zero, `Alpha`=1, `PreferPerPixelLighting`=false, `FogEnabled`=false, `FogStart`=0, `FogEnd`=1, `FogColor`=Zero, `Texture`=null, `WeightsPerVertex`=4, `MaxBones`=72 (constant), `SpecularColor`=`Vector3.One` (constructor-assigned), `SpecularPower`=16 (constructor-assigned) — including the constructor's `SetBoneTransforms(identityBones)` call initializing all 72 bone slots to `Matrix.Identity`, matched exactly. `DirectionalLight0.Enabled` correctly set `true` in the constructor (matching FNA, same pattern as every other stock effect audited so far); `DirectionalLight1`/`DirectionalLight2` correctly remain at class defaults (disabled). `IEffectLights.LightingEnabled`'s always-true getter and throw-on-`false` setter matches FNA's `NotSupportedException` semantics (`std::runtime_error`, per established convention). `SetBoneTransforms`/`GetBoneTransforms`'s bounds-checking matches FNA exactly: `SetBoneTransforms` throws on empty or `>MaxBones` input; `GetBoneTransforms` throws on `count<=0` or `count>MaxBones`, and restores `M44=1` on every returned matrix (FNA's own 4×3-to-4×4 conversion quirk, since bones are packed as `float4x3` on the GPU). `OnApply()`'s `SetWorldViewProjAndFog`/`SetLightingMatrices`/`SetMaterialColor`/shader-index logic (inlined per Task 351's established architecture) matches FNA's `EffectHelpers` calls literal-for-literal, including the exact shader-index formula constants (`+1` no-fog, `+2`/`+4` for 2/4 weights, `+12` per-pixel-preferred, `+6` one-light) — byte-for-byte identical to FNA's own `if/else` chain. **Real, confirmed, FIXED bug found**: `Clone()`'s copy constructor set the private `specularColor_`/`specularPower_` cache fields directly (`specularColor_ = src.specularColor_; specularPower_ = src.specularPower_;`) but never updated the freshly-recreated `specularColorParam_`/`specularPowerParam_` `EffectParameter`s — since the getters (`getSpecularColorProperty()`/`getSpecularPowerProperty()`) always read from the parameter when it's non-null (which it always is post-`CacheEffectParameters()`), the cache-field assignment was dead code and every clone's `SpecularColor`/`SpecularPower` silently reset to `(0,0,0)`/`0` regardless of the source's actual values — **the identical architectural bug shape Task 392 already fixed for `FogColor` across 4 stock effects**, but for a different pair of properties on `SkinnedEffect` specifically (undetected until now since `SkinnedEffect` has zero existing test coverage). **Fixed** with the same one-line-per-field pattern: `if (specularColorParam_) specularColorParam_->SetValue(src.getSpecularColorProperty());` / `if (specularPowerParam_) specularPowerParam_->SetValue(src.getSpecularPowerProperty());`, added alongside the (now provably necessary, previously-dead) cache-field assignments. Verification deferred to Task 402's `Clone()` test (mirrors Task 392's exact precedent of a `Clone()` test catching this class of bug). **3 new real, confirmed, deliberately deferred findings, each opened as its own new backlog task**: (1) **Task 893** — `FillGpuDrawParams()` only forwards `DirectionalLight0`, the identical shape as `BasicEffect`'s Task 885 and `EnvironmentMapEffect`'s Task 890 (`SkinnedEffect.fx` shares the same `Lighting.fxh`/`ComputeLights` mechanism); (2) **Task 894** — `SpecularColor`/`SpecularPower` have **zero GPU implementation on any of the 3 backends** (confirmed: `GpuDrawParams` has no generic specular-color/power fields at all, only the unrelated `envMapSpecular` used by `EnvironmentMapEffect`) — the identical shape as `BasicEffect`'s already-tracked Task 886 (real Blinn-Phong specular highlights, a new feature with zero existing infrastructure), extended here to a second effect that likely shares the same fix plumbing; (3) **Task 895** — `WeightsPerVertex` is a **complete GPU no-op on all 3 backends**: FNA's real `Skin(vin, boneCount)` HLSL function only sums the first `boneCount` weight/index pairs (`for (int i=0;i<boneCount;i++) skinning += Bones[vin.Indices[i]]*vin.Weights[i];`), meaning `WeightsPerVertex=1`/`2` genuinely ignore the remaining weight slots' contributions entirely — but CNA's skinning vertex shader on all 3 backends (`aBoneWeights`/`a_weight`/`aBoneWeights` in EasyGL/Bgfx/Vulkan respectively) unconditionally sums **all 4** weights regardless of the property's value, confirmed via direct shader-source reading on all 3 backends. Only produces a visible difference from FNA when a vertex's "unused" (beyond the declared `WeightsPerVertex` count) weight slots hold genuinely nonzero stale/garbage data instead of `0` — realistic authored content typically zeros unused slots, so this is architecturally real but may be practically invisible for well-formed content; still a confirmed, testable divergence from FNA's real per-vertex-weight-count semantics. **Also noted, NOT opened as a bug** (an acceptable, strictly-more-accurate deviation, same class as Task 396's per-pixel-Fresnel note): `PreferPerPixelLighting=false`'s default per-vertex-lighting behavior is *also* effectively a no-op on CNA, since `NdotL` is computed in the fragment shader unconditionally on every backend (confirmed via shader-source reading) — CNA always renders with per-pixel lighting quality regardless of this flag, which can only ever look *better or identical* to FNA's real per-vertex-lit default, never worse, so this is deliberately not tracked as a bug. No production code changed beyond the one `Clone()` fix; no rebuild-breaking changes. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass (the `Clone()` fix touches shared `SkinnedEffect.cpp`, compiled into all 3 backends): EasyGL ctest 3552/3555 (3 pre-existing, exact-name match, no test-count change since no new tests were added yet — that's Task 402's job), Vulkan ctest 3472/3485 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes, no test-count change), Bgfx ctest 3456/3456 (100% pass, no test-count change). |

(`World`/`View`/`Projection`=Identity, `DiffuseColor`=One, `EmissiveColor`/`AmbientLightColor`=Zero, `SpecularColor`=One, `SpecularPower`=16, `Alpha`=1, `PreferPerPixelLighting`=false, `LightingEnabled`=true-locked, `DirectionalLight0.Enabled`=true/`Light1`/`Light2`=false, `FogEnabled`=false, `FogStart`=0, `FogEnd`=1, `FogColor`=Zero, `Texture`=null, `WeightsPerVertex`=4, `MaxBones`=72 — all reconfirmed matching Task 401's audit, no new default-value bugs), `LightingEnabled`'s throw-on-`false`/no-throw-on-`true` behavior, `EnableDefaultLighting()`'s ambient-color and all-3-lights-enabled effects (byte-for-byte identical constants to Task 363's own `BasicEffect` confirmation, since both share the identical FNA `EffectHelpers.EnableDefaultLighting()` helper), `SetBoneTransforms`/`GetBoneTransforms`'s full bounds-checking matrix (empty throws, `>MaxBones` throws, `count<=0`/negative throws, exactly-`MaxBones` succeeds, default bones are all `Matrix.Identity`), `WeightsPerVertex`'s accept-1/2/4/throw-otherwise validation, a round-trip setter test per property including `SetBoneTransforms`/`GetBoneTransforms` round-tripping a non-identity bone matrix, and a `Clone()` test. **The `Clone()` test is the regression guard for Task 401's own fix**: it deliberately sets `SpecularColor`/`SpecularPower` to non-default values before cloning, exactly the scenario Task 401 found broken. **Verified genuine discriminating power**: `git stash`-reverted Task 401's one-line `Clone()` fix (temporarily, via direct edit + rebuild rather than a full stash since the file also carries this task's own `MaxBones` linker-fix addition) and reran — `CloneCopiesAllProperties` failed exactly as predicted, with `clone->getSpecularColorProperty()` reading back `(0,0,0)` instead of `(0.7,0.8,0.9)` and `clone->getSpecularPowerProperty()` reading back `0` instead of `64`; restored and reconfirmed all 52 green. **Also fixed a genuinely new, unrelated build-breaking discovery**: `EXPECT_EQ(SkinnedEffect::MaxBones, 72)` ODR-uses the in-class-initialized `static const int MaxBones = 72;`, which had no out-of-line definition anywhere — causing a linker error (`undefined reference to SkinnedEffect::MaxBones`) the moment any code takes its address/reference (as GTest's comparison machinery does internally). **Fixed** by adding the missing out-of-line definition `const int SkinnedEffect::MaxBones;` to `SkinnedEffect.cpp`, per CLAUDE.md's own documented "Static Members and Named Constants" convention — a pre-existing gap that had simply never been exercised until this was the first place any test code needed `MaxBones`'s address. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass (fix touches shared `SkinnedEffect.cpp`, compiled into all 3 backends): EasyGL ctest 3604/3607 (3 pre-existing, exact-name match, +52 new tests), Vulkan ctest 3524/3537 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes, +52 new tests), Bgfx ctest 3508/3508 (100% pass, +52 new tests). |
| 403 | Verify `SetBoneTransforms` accepts exactly supported bone count                     | ✅      | **Already fully satisfied by Task 402's own test coverage — no new code needed.** Task 402's `SetBoneTransformsAcceptsExactlyMaxBones` test (`tests/Microsoft/Xna/Framework/Graphics/SkinnedEffectTests.cpp`) already constructs a `std::vector<Matrix>` of exactly `SkinnedEffect::MaxBones` (72) elements and asserts `SetBoneTransforms` accepts it without throwing (`EXPECT_NO_THROW`) — precisely this task's own scope. Discovered while reviewing plan_graphics.md's remaining Phase 46 backlog immediately after completing Task 402; rather than write a duplicate test, this row documents the pre-existing coverage and marks the task complete. No production code or test changes in this task specifically. |
| 404 | Verify `GetBoneTransforms` returns independent copy or expected reference semantics | ✅      | **Verify-only, zero bugs found.** FNA's real `GetBoneTransforms(count)` calls `bonesParam.GetValueMatrixArray(count)`, which allocates a brand-new `Matrix[]` array on every call rather than returning an alias into the parameter's internal storage — a genuine "independent copy" contract (C#'s array reference semantics don't change this: the *array object itself* is freshly allocated each call, even though arrays are reference types). Read CNA's `EffectParameter::GetValueMatrixArray()` implementation directly: it builds a brand-new `std::vector<Matrix> r` and `push_back`s freshly-constructed `Matrix` value objects copied out of `floatData_` — genuinely independent, no aliasing, no shared pointers, no views into internal storage. Added a test that calls `GetBoneTransforms()`, mutates element `[0]` of the returned vector to a distinctive non-identity transform, then calls `GetBoneTransforms()` again and asserts the second call's `[0]` is still `Matrix.Identity` — proving the mutation of the first call's returned copy had zero effect on the effect's actual stored bone state. **Discriminating power confirmed by construction rather than a `git stash` revert** (no bug exists to revert): had `GetValueMatrixArray()` instead returned a reference/view/shared-storage alias into `floatData_`, mutating the first call's returned vector would have corrupted the underlying storage and the second call would have read back the corrupted value instead of `Identity` — the test's own pass/fail boundary is exactly the boundary between correct-copy and incorrect-alias semantics. 1 new test added to `SkinnedEffectTests.cpp` (`GetBoneTransformsReturnsIndependentCopy`), all 53 tests in the file green. No production code changed. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass: EasyGL ctest 3605/3608 (3 pre-existing, exact-name match, +1 new test), Vulkan ctest 3525/3538 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes, +1 new test), Bgfx ctest 3509/3509 (100% pass, +1 new test). |
| 405 | Verify too many bones throws correct exception                                      | ✅      | **Already fully satisfied by Task 402's own test coverage — no new code needed.** Task 402's `SetBoneTransformsThrowsWhenExceedingMaxBones` test already constructs a `std::vector<Matrix>` of `SkinnedEffect::MaxBones + 1` elements and asserts `SetBoneTransforms` throws `std::invalid_argument` (matching FNA's `ArgumentException` for the identical over-limit case, per the project's established exception-mapping convention); `GetBoneTransformsThrowsWhenExceedingMaxBones` covers the equivalent boundary for the read side, throwing `std::out_of_range` (matching FNA's `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`). Discovered alongside Task 403 while reviewing the remaining Phase 46 backlog; this row documents the pre-existing coverage and marks the task complete. No production code or test changes in this task specifically. |
| 406 | Pixel test: identity bone palette                                                   | ✅      | **First real pixel/rendering test for `SkinnedEffect` in this phase** (Tasks 401–405 were all unit tests/audits, no GPU rendering exercised yet) — **verify-only, zero bugs found on all 3 backends.** Confirms the skinning math correctly degenerates to a no-op when every bone transform is `Identity`: for a vertex bound 100% to a single bone (`weight=1`, all other weights `0`), `skinMat = weight × Bones[index] = 1 × Identity = Identity`, so `mul(Position, skinMat) = Position` unchanged — the mesh should render at exactly its authored position with zero deformation. Deliberately structured as a **direct contrast with the pre-existing (Task 123) `examples/skinned_effect_integration_test.cpp`**, which uses the identical quad geometry (NDC x: -1..0, y: -1..1) and identical stride-52 vertex layout but sets bone 0 to a `+0.5` X translation, shifting the quad from the left half of the screen into the centre. This test uses `SkinnedEffect`'s own real default bone palette (every one of the 72 slots defaults to `Matrix.Identity` — confirmed by Task 401's audit and Task 402's own `BoneTransformsDefaultToIdentity` test; deliberately **no** `SetBoneTransforms` call made at all) and confirms the quad does **not** move: read-back points at NDC ≈ -0.75 (left quarter) and NDC ≈ -0.25 (inside the quad, near its right edge) both show the textured/lit quad color; a point at NDC ≈ +0.75 (right quarter, well outside the quad's un-shifted position) shows the green background — cleanly contrasting with Task 123's own already-verified translation behavior, where the equivalent centre point would show the shifted quad instead. **Real, previously-undiscovered Bgfx-specific test-harness pitfall found and worked around** (not a `SkinnedEffect`/backend bug): the initial test implementation read 3 distinct screen rectangles from a single rendered Bgfx frame inside one retry-loop iteration — every prior multi-region Bgfx pixel test in this project (confirmed by auditing all existing `bgfx_*_test.cpp` files) only ever reads **one** rectangle per draw+retry pass. The 3-reads-per-frame approach produced `left=(160,0,0)` (correct) but `centre=(0,0,0)`/`right=(0,0,0)` (both wrong — neither the expected quad color nor the green background), indicating Bgfx's `GetBackBufferData()`/screenshot-capture readback path only reliably reflects the **first** read call per rendered frame; subsequent calls in the same frame return stale/blank data. **Fixed the test** (not production code) by refactoring to a `renderAndRead()` helper that performs its own full clear+draw+retry-loop+single-read pass per check point (3 independent render passes total, mirroring the established one-rectangle-per-pass convention already used by every other multi-point Bgfx test in this project) — confirmed all 3 checks then pass correctly. Also found and worked around a second, already-known Bgfx quirk while writing the test: `GraphicsDevice::SetDepthTestEnabled()` is a hard-throw stub on Bgfx (Task 375's finding) — omitted entirely, matching the established `bgfx_dual_texture_test.cpp` precedent (`Clear()` already resets the depth buffer and the quad draws at a fixed `z=0` every frame, so depth-test toggling was never actually needed here). **1/1 PASS on all 3 backends, exact match.** Added `examples/{easygl,vulkan,bgfx}_skinnedeffect_identity_bones_test.cpp`; Vulkan/EasyGL needed no such multi-read workaround (single retry loop with all 3 reads per iteration works correctly on both). No production code changed. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass: EasyGL ctest 3606/3609 (3 pre-existing, exact-name match, +1 new test), Vulkan ctest 3526/3539 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes, +1 new test), Bgfx ctest 3510/3510 (100% pass, +1 new test). |
| 407 | Pixel test: single translation bone                                                 | ✅      | **Verify-only, zero bugs found.** Formalizes the pre-existing (Task 123) `examples/skinned_effect_integration_test.cpp` scenario (EasyGL-only) into this phase's own per-backend, per-task naming/registration convention, extending it to Vulkan and Bgfx for the first time. All vertices bound 100% to bone 0 (`weight=1`, all other weights `0`); bone 0 set to a real, non-identity `Matrix.CreateTranslation(+0.5, 0, 0)`: `skinMat = weight × Bones[index] = 1 × Translate(+0.5,0,0)`, so `mul(Position, skinMat)` shifts every vertex by exactly that translation. Quad authored at NDC x: -1..0, y: -1..1 (left half); after the bone translation it covers NDC x: -0.5..0.5 (screen centre) — the exact inverse contrast to Task 406's identity-bone baseline (there the quad stayed put; here it moves). Read-back points at NDC ≈ -0.75 (left quarter, now outside the shifted quad → green), NDC ≈ 0.00 (centre, now inside the shifted quad → textured/lit), and NDC ≈ +0.75 (right quarter, still outside → green). **All 3 backends produced the exact predicted output on the first attempt**: EasyGL `centre=(174,0,0)`, Vulkan `centre=(160,0,0)`, Bgfx `centre=(160,0,0)` (small EasyGL/Vulkan-Bgfx RGB differences are the same pre-existing per-backend lighting-precision variance seen throughout this phase, not a bug). Bgfx used Task 406's own `renderAndRead()` per-checkpoint helper (one full clear+draw+retry+read pass per rectangle) plus the established `RasterizerState::CullNone` (Task 364/884) and omitted-`SetDepthTestEnabled` (Task 375) workarounds; EasyGL/Vulkan needed no such workaround (single retry loop with all 3 reads per iteration works correctly on both, matching Task 406's own finding). Added `examples/{easygl,vulkan,bgfx}_skinnedeffect_translation_bone_test.cpp` — the pre-existing Task 123 test is left untouched as its own independent, earlier-established EasyGL coverage. No production code changed. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass: EasyGL ctest 3607/3610 (3 pre-existing, exact-name match, +1 new test), Vulkan ctest 3527/3540 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes, +1 new test), Bgfx ctest 3511/3511 (100% pass, +1 new test). |
| 408 | Pixel test: two-bone blend                                                          | ✅      | **Verify-only, zero bugs found.** Confirms FNA's real `Skin(vin, boneCount)` formula for `boneCount=2`: `skinMat = Bones[Indices[0]] × Weights[0] + Bones[Indices[1]] × Weights[1]`. Since matrix multiplication is linear in the matrix, `mul(Position, skinMat)` equals `Weights[0]×mul(Position,Bones[0]) + Weights[1]×mul(Position,Bones[1])`. Deliberately chose a **discriminating, non-trivial value pair**: Bone 0 = `Translate(-0.5,0,0)`, Bone 1 = `Translate(+1.5,0,0)`, weights split `0.5`/`0.5` → net blended shift = `0.5×(-0.5) + 0.5×(+1.5) = +0.5` — a value distinct from EITHER individual bone's own shift (`-0.5` or `+1.5`), so a bug that picked one bone alone (ignoring the other's weight, a plausible failure mode e.g. for a naive `WeightsPerVertex` mishandling) would produce a clearly wrong, differently-observable result rather than accidentally matching. The net effect (`+0.5` shift) numerically matches Task 407's single-bone-translation result, reached via a genuinely different mechanism (2 nonzero weighted bones instead of 1); reused Task 407's identical NDC quad geometry and read-back layout for direct comparability. **All 3 backends produced the exact predicted output on the first attempt**: EasyGL `centre=(174,0,0)`, Vulkan `centre=(160,0,0)`, Bgfx `centre=(160,0,0)` — byte-identical to Task 407's own per-backend values, strong evidence the blend is computed correctly end-to-end on all 3 backends. **Discriminating power independently verified**: temporarily changed the EasyGL test's weights to `(1,0)` (bone 0 alone, full weight) and rebuilt/reran — predicted shift becomes `-0.5` (not `+0.5`), moving the quad to NDC x: `-1.5..-0.5`; observed exactly the predicted swap: `left=(174,0,0)` (now inside the shifted quad), `centre=(0,255,0)` (now outside, green) — confirmed the shader genuinely reads and applies both weights rather than hardcoding one bone or ignoring the split. Restored the real `0.5`/`0.5` test afterward and reconfirmed it passes. Bgfx used Task 406's own `renderAndRead()` per-checkpoint helper plus the established `RasterizerState::CullNone` (Task 364/884) and omitted-`SetDepthTestEnabled` (Task 375) workarounds; EasyGL/Vulkan needed no such workaround. Added `examples/{easygl,vulkan,bgfx}_skinnedeffect_twobone_blend_test.cpp`. No production code changed. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass: EasyGL ctest 3608/3611 (3 pre-existing, exact-name match, +1 new test), Vulkan ctest 3528/3541 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes, +1 new test), Bgfx ctest 3512/3512 (100% pass, +1 new test). |
| 409 | Cross-backend SkinnedEffect comparison suite                                        | ✅      | **Capstone, zero new bugs found.** Combines Tasks 406–408's individually-verified pieces (identity-bone no-op, single-bone translation, 2-bone weighted blend) into **one scene, one bone-palette upload, one `DrawPrimitives` call covering 3 quads** — proving the pieces compose correctly *together within a single draw*, not just when each is exercised in its own dedicated draw call (Tasks 406–408 each used their own isolated scene). All 3 quads share the identical authored geometry (NDC x: -1.0..-0.5, y: -1..1) and are distinguished **only** by their per-vertex weight/index data, directly testing that the vertex shader reads genuinely per-vertex skinning attributes rather than some accidental global/uniform override: Quad A (`w0=1,i0=0` → `Bones[0]=Identity`) stays at x: -1.0..-0.5; Quad B (`w0=1,i0=1` → `Bones[1]=Translate(+0.75,0,0)`) shifts to x: -0.25..0.25; Quad C (`w0=w1=0.5,i0=2,i1=3` → `Bones[2]=Translate(+1.0,0,0)`, `Bones[3]=Translate(+2.0,0,0)`, blended shift `0.5×1.0+0.5×2.0=+1.5`, distinct from either individual bone) shifts to x: 0.5..1.0. The 3 post-transform quads land in exactly the 3 non-overlapping regions this phase's own established read-back columns (`W/8`, `W/2`, `7W/8`) already probe — reusing the identical pixel columns from every Task 406–408 test, but now each column answers "which quad is visible here" instead of "is the one quad inside or outside." **All 3 backends produced the exact predicted output on the first attempt**, each backend byte-identical across all 3 quads within itself (EasyGL: all 3 = `(174,0,0)`; Vulkan/Bgfx: all 3 = `(160,0,0)`) — matching each backend's own established single-quad values from Tasks 406–408 exactly, strong evidence the 3 independent bone/weight combinations are applied correctly and simultaneously within one draw call. Bgfx used Task 406's own `renderAndRead()` per-checkpoint helper (extended to accept a `triCount` parameter since this test draws 3 quads' worth of triangles instead of 1) plus the established `RasterizerState::CullNone` (Task 364/884) and omitted-`SetDepthTestEnabled` (Task 375) workarounds. Added `examples/{easygl,vulkan,bgfx}_skinnedeffect_combined_test.cpp` (naming mirrors Task 370/389/399's own `_combined_test.cpp` capstone convention). No production code changed. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass: EasyGL ctest 3609/3612 (3 pre-existing, exact-name match, +1 new test), Vulkan ctest 3529/3542 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes, +1 new test), Bgfx ctest 3513/3513 (100% pass, +1 new test). |
| 410 | Document SkinnedEffect backend parity                                               | ✅      | **Closes Phase 46.** Wrote `docs/skinnedeffect-support.md`, synthesizing Tasks 401–409 (mirrors `docs/basiceffect-support.md`/`docs/alphatesteffect-support.md`/`docs/dualtextureeffect-support.md`/`docs/environmentmapeffect-support.md`'s established style): per-task summary sections, a full 3-backend support matrix (property defaults, `Clone()`'s `SpecularColor`/`SpecularPower` fix, `MaxBones`/bone-count bounds-checking, `GetBoneTransforms` independent-copy semantics, identity/single-bone/two-bone-blend pixel-verified correctness, multi-quad/multi-bone composition), and an "Open, tracked follow-up work" section listing Task 893 (`DirectionalLight1`/`2` unforwarded, opened by Task 401), Task 894 (`SpecularColor`/`SpecularPower` zero GPU implementation, opened by Task 401), and Task 895 (`WeightsPerVertex` complete GPU no-op, opened by Task 401). No production code or tests changed — pure documentation task, matching Task 380/390/400's precedent. |

## Phase 47 — SpriteBatch renderer correctness

| #   | Task                                                                     | Status | Notes                 |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------ | --------------------- |
| 411 | Create mock/recording `ISpriteBatchBackend` for deterministic unit tests | ✅      | **Opens Phase 47.** Audited `SpriteBatch`'s architecture first: it already has a dedicated `ISpriteBatchBackend` interface (separate from the 3D `IGraphicsBackend`), with `Begin()/End()/SetTransformMatrix()/SetCustomEffect()/SetSamplerFilter()/SetSamplerAddressMode()/Draw()×3` — `flushBatch()`'s sort logic (`std::stable_sort` by `layerDepth` for FrontToBack/BackToFront, by raw `Texture2D*` for `Texture`, no sort for `Deferred`) followed by `flushSingle()` calling `backend_->Draw(s.texture->GetBackend(), ...)` is the single dispatch point every sort-mode test needs to observe. Found **2 real gaps blocking any mock-based test**, both fixed as part of this task's own scope (infrastructure prerequisites, not bugs in existing behavior): (1) `SpriteBatch` had **no injection seam** — `SpriteBatch(GraphicsDevice&)` always calls `GetBackend().CreateSpriteBatch()` (a real GPU backend), and the existing `SpriteBatch()` default constructor leaves `backend_` permanently null with no setter, making it impossible to attach any backend (mock or real) without a full `GraphicsDevice`. **Fixed** by adding a new `NOXNA` constructor `SpriteBatch(std::unique_ptr<ISpriteBatchBackend>)` that bypasses `GraphicsDevice` entirely (`GraphicsResource(nullptr)` base, matching the existing default constructor's own null-device precedent). (2) `flushSingle()` dereferences `Texture2D::GetBackend()` (`*backend_`), but the existing `Texture2D::CreateCpuOnlyForTests()` test factory leaves `backend_` null (by design, for CPU-pixel-only tests) — a null dereference the moment any queued sprite is flushed. **Fixed** by adding a new `NOXNA` factory `Texture2D::CreateWithBackendForTests(int w, int h, std::shared_ptr<ITextureBackend>)`, mirroring `CreateCpuOnlyForTests`'s exact established pattern (default-construct, then directly assign private fields from within the class's own static method). Built the actual mock: `tests/Microsoft/Xna/Framework/Graphics/RecordingSpriteBatchBackend.hpp` (new shared test-utility header, mirrors the project's existing `*TestAccess.hpp` convention, e.g. `tests/Microsoft/Xna/Framework/Audio/CueTestAccess.hpp`) with `DummyTextureBackend` (minimal `ITextureBackend`, identifies a texture without a real GPU resource) and `RecordingSpriteBatchBackend` (records every `Begin`/`End`/`Draw` call, in order, into an inspectable log). Added 5 new tests to `SpriteBatchTests.cpp` proving the injection point + mock work end-to-end: construction doesn't throw, `Begin()`/`End()` dispatch correctly (call counts), a `Draw()` call is recorded with byte-exact parameters (deliberately non-default rotation/origin/color/layerDepth so the assertion is genuinely discriminating, not accidentally matching zero-initialized defaults), and 2 distinct `Texture2D` instances produce 2 distinct recorded backend pointers (the precondition Task 414's `SpriteSortMode::Texture` grouping assertion will depend on). Deliberately scoped Tasks 412–416's own per-`SpriteSortMode` assertions (Immediate flush timing, Deferred order preservation, Texture grouping, FrontToBack/BackToFront ordering) out of this task — this task's job was only proving the shared infrastructure itself works. Full 3-backend rebuild + regression pass (change touches shared `SpriteBatch.cpp`/`Texture2D.cpp`, compiled into all 3 backends): EasyGL ctest 3614/3617 (3 pre-existing, exact-name match, +5 new tests), Vulkan ctest 3534/3547 (13 pre-existing, exact-name match, no flakes, +5 new tests), Bgfx ctest 3518/3518 (100% pass, +5 new tests). |

| 413 | Complete tests for `SpriteSortMode::Deferred`                            | ⬜      | Task 162 dependency   |
| 414 | Complete tests for `SpriteSortMode::Texture`                             | ⬜      | Task 163 dependency   |

| 664 | Fix Vulkan: multiple `SpriteBatch::Begin()`/`End()` calls per frame — only the last batch renders | ⬜ | Confirmed bug (session 2026-07-02, see `NEXT.md` §5). Root cause not yet isolated — likely `VulkanSpriteBatchBackend` reuses/overwrites a single per-frame vertex/index staging buffer or descriptor set across `Begin`/`End` cycles instead of flushing or double-buffering per batch. Repro: 2+ `Begin()`/`Draw()`/`End()` cycles in one `Draw(GameTime)`, each drawing a distinct-colour quad to a distinct screen region; expect both regions correct, currently only the last one renders. Do not guess-fix; instrument/trace the Vulkan command recording first. |
| 665 | Fix Vulkan: `SpriteBatch::Begin()`'s `SamplerState` (Filter/AddressU/AddressV) has no effect | ⬜ | `VulkanSpriteBatchBackend` doesn't override `SetSamplerFilter`/`SetSamplerAddressMode` (mirrors the pre-Task-269 EasyGL bug, same root cause, different backend). Fix shape: mirror Task 269's EasyGL fix — store pending filter/address values set via `SetSamplerFilter`/`SetSamplerAddressMode`, apply via the existing per-slot `VkSampler` cache (Task 118, `GetOrCreateTexSamplerDescSet`) at flush time, slot 0. Verify UV is not separately clamped anywhere in the Vulkan sprite draw path (mirrors the second EasyGL bug Task 269 found — check before assuming only the sampler wiring is missing). Add a `Vulkan_TextureAddressMode` pixel-readback test analogous to `EasyGL_TextureAddressMode`. |

## Phase 56 — WebGPU backend: infrastructure and CMake setup

> **⛔ FORBIDDEN as of 2026-07-06** (previously just "deprioritized" as of 2026-07-02). Do not
> work on this phase or Phases 57–69 at all — not started, not stubbed — until the project owner
> explicitly lifts this restriction. See `CLAUDE.md` ("WebGPU Is Forbidden For Now"). Task numbers
> were moved to 10001+ (from 501–661) to free up numbering space for the Phase 70–73 wave; no
> other content changed.
>
> WebGPU backend uses **wgpu-native v29** (C API header `webgpu.h` + `wgpu.h`).
> Installed at `vendor/wgpu-native/`. Shaders are written in **WGSL** (not SPIR-V).
> Push constants do not exist in WebGPU — replaced by uniform buffers (bind group 0, binding 0).
> Backend selection: `-DCNA_GRAPHICS_BACKEND=WEBGPU`, build dir `cmake-build-webgpu`.
>
> Strategy: mirror the Vulkan backend structure, adapt to WebGPU API differences.
> Estimated total effort: ~4–6 weeks (Tasks 501–750).

| #   | Task                                                                                                          | Status | Notes                                                                 |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 10001 | Add `CNA_GRAPHICS_BACKEND=WEBGPU` CMake option; find `vendor/wgpu-native` headers + libs; define `CNA_BACKEND_WEBGPU` | ⬜ | Mirror VULKAN block in CMakeLists.txt |
| 10002 | Create `include/CNA/Internal/Backends/WebGPU/WebGPUGraphicsBackend.hpp` — class skeleton, all IGraphicsBackend sub-interfaces declared | ⬜ | ~12 nested backend classes |
| 10003 | Create `src/CNA/Internal/Backends/WebGPU/WebGPUGraphicsBackend.cpp` — stub all methods (throw not-implemented) | ⬜ | Compiles clean, no functionality yet |
| 10004 | SDL3 surface creation: obtain `WGPUSurface` via `SDL_GetProperty(SDL_PROP_WINDOW_WGPU_SURFACE_POINTER)` or `wgpuInstanceCreateSurface` | ⬜ | Prerequisite for all rendering |
| 10005 | `WGPUInstance` + `WGPUAdapter` + `WGPUDevice` + `WGPUQueue` initialization via `wgpuCreateInstance` / `wgpuInstanceRequestAdapter` / `wgpuAdapterRequestDevice` | ⬜ | All synchronous in wgpu-native |
| 10006 | Swap chain: `WGPUSurface` configure + `wgpuSurfaceGetCurrentTexture` + `wgpuTextureCreateView` for backbuffer | ⬜ | Replaces `vkAcquireNextImageKHR` |
| 10007 | Command encoder: `wgpuDeviceCreateCommandEncoder` + `wgpuCommandEncoderFinish` + `wgpuQueueSubmit` per frame | ⬜ | Replaces Vulkan command buffer recording |
| 10008 | Render pass: `wgpuCommandEncoderBeginRenderPass` with color attachment (backbuffer view) + depth attachment | ⬜ | Equivalent to `vkCmdBeginRenderPass` |
| 10009 | `Clear()`: set clear color in `WGPURenderPassColorAttachment.clearValue`; implement depth clear in pass descriptor | ⬜ | |
| 10010 | `Present()`: `wgpuSurfacePresent()` after queue submit | ⬜ | |

## Phase 70 — SDL_Renderer: 2D backend verified perfection

> Runs first, per "Execution order" above. SDL_Renderer is 2D-only by design (see file header),
> so this phase is deliberately smaller than Phases 72–73 — there's no 3D pipeline, blend/depth/
> raster state matrix, or stock-effect shader breadth to cover. "Perfect" here means: every 2D
> XNA feature SDL_Renderer is supposed to support is pixel-verified specifically *through*
> SDL_Renderer (not assumed from EasyGL parity), and every unsupported 3D path throws the
> correct, specific exception rather than a generic one.

### SpriteBatch on SDL_Renderer

| #   | Task | Status | Notes |

| 670 | Pixel test: `SpriteSortMode::Immediate` per-draw flush on SDL_Renderer | ⬜ | Mirrors Task 161 |
| 671 | Pixel test: rotation around origin on SDL_Renderer | ⬜ | `SDL_RenderTextureRotated` |

## Phase 71 — EasyGL: final gap closure and perfection gate

> Runs second. Deliberately small: EasyGL is already the implicit default target of most
> unlabeled tasks across Phases 34–55 (see the coverage-analysis artifact from this session —
> among 235 pending core tasks, 189 name no backend and default to EasyGL by established
> convention). This phase covers only what genuinely isn't tracked anywhere else yet.

| #   | Task | Status | Notes |
| --- | ---- | ------ | ----- |
| 732 | Implement real `SurfaceFormat` GPU forwarding on EasyGL | ⬜ | `CreateTexture`/`CreateTexture3D`/`CreateTextureCube` currently hardcode `GL_RGBA8` regardless of requested format; map Color/Bgr565/Bgra4444/Bgra5551/Rgba1010102/Rg32/Rgba64/Single/Vector2/Vector4/HalfSingle/HalfVector2/HalfVector4/HdrBlendable to real GLES 3.2 internal formats; throw clearly for genuinely unsupported ones |
| 733 | Implement `FillMode::WireFrame` emulation on EasyGL via barycentric-coordinate geometry shader + `fwidth()` edge detection | ⬜ | GLES 3.2 has geometry shaders in core — no native `glPolygonMode`, but this is emulatable, not a hardware wall (corrects the earlier "N/A on GLES" framing) |
| 734 | Add a non-geometry-shader barycentric fallback for GLES 3.2 drivers with unreliable geometry-shader support | ⬜ | Vertex-attribute barycentrics (requires unwelding shared vertices) as a documented fallback path; note in `docs/easygl_bugs.md` |
| 735 | Implement `Texture3D` mip-chain generation on EasyGL | ⬜ | `EasyGLTexture3DBackend`'s `mipMap` constructor parameter is currently unused (`bool /*mipMap*/`) — single level always, even though `Texture3D::LevelCount` now correctly reports the requested count (Task 271) |
| 736 | Verify/implement `TextureCube` mip-chain generation on EasyGL | ⬜ | Confirm whether `EasyGLTextureCubeBackend` has the same unused-`mipMap`-parameter pattern as Task 735 found for Texture3D; fix if so |
| 737 | Add explicit `TextureAddressMode::Mirror` regression test for the Task 269 SpriteBatch SamplerState fix | ⬜ | Only Wrap vs. Clamp were independently pixel-tested; Mirror is fixed by the same code path but never verified on its own |
| 738 | Close the remaining EasyGL-scoped ⬜ tasks in Phases 34–55 | ⬜ | Not new work — this is the actual bulk of "EasyGL perfection." See Phases 34, 35, 36, 37, 38 (partial), 39, 40, 41, 42, 43 (partial), 44, 45, 46, 47, 48, 49, 50, 51 (EasyGL row), 52, 53 (EasyGL-relevant rows), 54, 55 (Tasks 493–494 specifically) |
| 739 | Final EasyGL perfection gate: re-run Tasks 493–494 (full unit + integration suite) after Tasks 732–738 land | ⬜ | Zero unexplained failures; update `docs/xna-4-api-coverage.md` overall EasyGL estimate |

## Phase 74 — XNA compiled effect bytecode (`.fx`) support via MojoShader

Opened by Task 352's full-support decision. Full research/reasoning behind this phase lives in
`docs/fx-bytecode-support-plan.md` — read that first. Dedicated task-number block (`10200`+),
mirroring the WebGPU backend's `10000`+ convention (Phases 56–69), since this is comparable in
scope: a new vendored native dependency, two independent per-backend GPU-shader-translation
paths, an unknown-feasibility third backend, and real test-fixture sourcing with no XNA Content
Pipeline tooling available in this project.

| #     | Task                                                                                                   | Status | Notes                                                                                                    |
| ----- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 10200 | Vendor MojoShader (`third_party/mojoshader` submodule) + `cmake/ThirdPartyMojoShader.cmake` + `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` entry | ⬜ | Source confirmed available/readable locally at `/rv/data/library/github.com/u3d-community/U3D/Source/ThirdParty/MojoShader` (zlib-licensed); needs its own proper upstream submodule remote, not a copy from that path |
| 10201 | C++ wrapper around `mojoshader_effects.c`'s parser: compiled-effect byte blob → CNA-native technique/pass/parameter reflection data | ⬜ | The container-parsing half of "full support" — independent of any GPU shader translation |
| 10202 | EasyGL path: compile MojoShader `GLSL`/`GLSL120` profile output via EasyGL's existing shader-program creation path | ⬜ | Closest to free — MojoShader's own primary supported output target |
| 10203 | Vulkan path — vendoring/feasibility: decide and vendor a GLSL→SPIR-V compiler (glslang is the default candidate; only present locally as Android NDK/Flatpak build tooling today, not a repo-vendorable checkout) | ⬜ | Blocking prerequisite for Task 10204; MojoShader has no SPIR-V output profile |
| 10204 | Vulkan path implementation: MojoShader GLSL output → SPIR-V (via Task 10203's compiler) → Vulkan pipeline creation | ⬜ | Depends on Task 10203 |
